# Changelog

All notable changes to the UNITARES Governance Framework will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

---

## [3.2.0] - 2026-10-04

### Added
- **check-in responses: `outcomes_in_e`:** when an agent's behavioral E includes the outcome term (at least three outcomes recorded in the last 24h), a check-in response now says which outcomes count and what they do. The fields are: how many outcomes were counted and how many were adverse, by type, and by `verification_source` when the outcome query fetched it (the default query does not, and the field then says `not_fetched` rather than calling recorded sources unrecorded); the term's real share of each E observation after the later blends (about 0.14, not the nominal 0.20, when both are active); its current contribution and range; and how much recording one more adverse outcome lowers the observation (an upper bound once the 20-outcome window is full, since the record that leaves may itself be adverse). It also states the two things the formula does not record: outcomes count without attribution to whether the agent's change caused them, and adverse means rework was needed, not fault. Mirror, compact and standard modes show it only when an adverse outcome is counted and the counts changed since the last account this process showed, and mirror adds one line. `response_mode='full'` always carries it, and `sync_state` lifts it beside `state_summary`. It restates the derivation the check-in already built: E, I, the verdict path and every persisted value are unchanged. Changing how outcomes count is deferred to after the 2026-12-01 registered read (#2610). (#2636)
- **startup warning for published compose secrets:** the server now logs a startup warning naming each of `LEASE_PLANE_BEARER_TOKEN`, `UNITARES_CONTINUITY_TOKEN_SECRET` and `UNITARES_LEASE_ATTESTATION_SIGNING_KEY` that still holds the default value from `docker-compose.yml`. Those defaults are in this repository, so they are safe only while the host ports stay on loopback. Inside a container with REST in local posture it also warns that `UNITARES_HTTP_API_TOKEN` is not what admits callers, since every peer there is in a private range; use `UNITARES_MCP_BEARER_TOKENS`. Warnings only; no behavior changes. (#2624)
- **knowledge graph:** the lean discovery digest (and `memory_suggestions`) now carries `closure_class`, and a closed, resolved, `wont_fix` or `superseded` row with no class is labelled `closure_standard: "undeclared"`. No reopen, tag or backfill is applied. (#2631)
- **audit:** a resume that a continuity token proves (PATH 0) now writes a `path0_token_accept_observed` audit event recording the token's age and whether it had expired. It is an observation only, kept out of calibration and written off the request thread: PATH 0 still accepts an expired token, as before, and this records how often that happens before any decision to enforce expiry. (#2638)

### Changed
- **local model endpoint:** the model behind `consult`, `call_model`, the in-process dialectic reviewer, check-in coaching and knowledge synthesis is now named by `UNITARES_MODEL_BASE_URL` (an OpenAI-compatible base URL, `/v1` included; default `http://localhost:11434/v1`) and `UNITARES_MODEL_ID` (default still `gemma4:latest`). The older names `UNITARES_OLLAMA_BASE`, `UNITARES_OLLAMA_BASE_URL` and `UNITARES_LLM_MODEL` keep working as aliases until v3.3.0; setting only an old name logs nothing, and `scripts/dev/unitares_doctor.py` prints one line per old name in use. The server now classifies the endpoint as local or external from its URL alone, never from DNS: loopback and RFC 1918 addresses and networks in `UNITARES_TRUSTED_NETWORKS` (the set the REST access checks trust) are local, and so are `localhost`, `host.docker.internal` and hostnames listed in the new `UNITARES_MODEL_LOCAL_HOSTS`. `UNITARES_MODEL_PRIVACY=local|external` overrides it. A `privacy='local'` request (the default) against an external endpoint is refused with `MODEL_ENDPOINT_NOT_LOCAL` before anything is sent, on every path that reads the setting, including the orchestrated reviewer's local backend and the local resident runner. The in-process reviewer tries Ollama's native `/api/chat` only after a cached `/api/version` probe says the endpoint is Ollama. `unitares model` lists models from `{base}/models` and writes the new names; the doctor checks that the endpoint lists the configured model. `call_model`'s `provider` and `model` descriptions change (interface contract 1.26.0). A call that reaches an external endpoint (with `privacy='auto'` or `'cloud'`) is recorded as external, not local, and `list_inference_hosts` reports the endpoint the same way. Local-route clients never follow redirects and ignore `HTTP_PROXY`/`HTTPS_PROXY`, so a local prompt cannot be carried to a host nothing classified. The model setting is `UNITARES_MODEL_ID`, not `UNITARES_MODEL`, which already names the model behind an agent's work in check-ins. A default install on local Ollama behaves as before. Other servers than Ollama are not yet verified end to end. (#2571, #2615)
- **tool descriptions and hints:** `knowledge(action="update")` says that a supplied `details` replaces the stored one; `search_shared_memory` describes `agent_id`, `status` and `query` as search filters; `sync_state`'s `complexity` no longer advertises a scale object the schema rejects, and its ambiguity error now asks for a 0-1 float or a named level; `delegate_inference`'s `timeout_s` notes client timeouts; the `degenerate_write_rejected` recovery names the marker and the workaround (interface contract 1.27.0, #2627). `dialectic`'s description calls it the on-record, state-altering lane and points opening a review at `request_review` and outside opinions at `consult` (#2592). Revision hints in envelopes and in the store/search descriptions now say `use_tool(tool_name="update_finding", ...)`, which works whether or not `update_finding` is advertised (#2632). Lineage, `not_on_roster` and borderline-S guidance text is plainer; no threshold or logic changes (#2626).
- **external calibration mirror:** `get_agent_external_calibration` scores only confidence the agent itself reported, and skips outcomes marked `calibration_excluded`. Previously it took the latest confidence of any audit event, usually a server-derived value, which is circular when scored against outcomes. `auto_attest` audit details now record `confidence_source` and `epistemic_class`, and a weak-identity clamp marks the value `confidence_dampened`. No verdict, risk, trust-tier or response change. (#2603)
- **knowledge synthesis:** a model-written topic summary must cite its source discoveries by reference. One with missing or invalid references is discarded and the deterministic summary is used instead. (#2597)
- **self-recovery reflections** are tagged `ephemeral` when written, so they archive after seven days (still retrievable with `include_archived=true`) instead of staying open until the 90-day archive pass. New reflections only. (#2643)
- **in-tree SDK:** `GovernanceClient` lists the server's advertised tools on connect and sends a call to a tool missing from a progressive listing through `use_tool`, which keeps MCP result validation and stops a per-call warning; servers that do not advertise `use_tool` keep direct dispatch. The in-tree SDK version moves to 0.4.1; it reaches PyPI when `sdk-v0.4.1` is tagged. (#2534)
- **dependencies:** `pydantic>=2.12.0,<3.0.0` is now declared (every tool schema imports it; it used to arrive only through `mcp`); `numpy` moves from `>=1.24.0` to `>=2.3.2` (the first release with CPython 3.14 wheels; the old floor could never install); `starlette` moves from `>=0.37.0` to `>=1.0.0`; `fakeredis` joins the `dev` extra (#2641). The `openai` ceiling moves from `<3.0.0` to `<4.0.0` (#2563).
- **documentation:** check-in guidance in `docs/guides/CODEX_START.md` and `commands/checkin.md` no longer says to check in once per turn as a baseline; it matches `AGENTS.md`: check in when there is real state to report (#2642). The install guide notes that a reinstall reuses existing Docker volumes and says to `docker compose down` first (#2628). The user manual drops a maintainer-specific "no paid model API" rule and a deployment-specific model name (#2566).

### Fixed
- **auto-emitted outcomes:** signal words now match only at a word boundary, so "unresolved" no longer counts as "resolved" and "unblocked" no longer counts as "blocked". A check-in reporting open problems had been recorded as `task_completed` (and vice versa), which fed the behavioral outcome terms. Existing rows are not rewritten. (#2611)
- **gateway (:8768):** a strict-identity refusal (`refused: true`) is now reported as an error, not as "Note saved" or "proceed"; `status`, `checkin`, `search`, `note` and `query` accept an optional `client_session_id` and forward it, so a bound agent can write through the gateway; `search` reads the result key the server actually returns (it always reported 0 results) and `status` reads the live reply shape (#2629). A definitional question such as "What does EISV stand for?" gets a glossary answer instead of the caller's own status (#2637).
- **lease plane:** a governed `file_write` now releases its lease with reason `normal`. The previous reason was rejected by the database constraint and the error was discarded, so every such lease stayed active until the TTL reaper closed it. An effect on a surface its proposer already holds is now refused rather than piggybacking on that lease. (#2618)
- **dialectic reviewer:** deeply nested JSON is treated as unparseable rather than crashing the reviewer before any fallback or abstention is recorded, and the verdict scanners no longer take quadratic time on it (#2619, #2622). When the Antigravity reviewer host exits nonzero, the stored error now includes its own reason (from its JSON error, stderr or log), capped at 300 characters (#2616).
- **dialectic_live:** on macOS, boot re-signs asset CLIs the OS kills on exec (#2600), and a failed boot-time asset build serves the build already on disk instead of crash-looping under launchd; assets are rebuilt only when the on-disk build is stale (#2601, #2613, #2602).

### Removed
- **37 of the last 38 legacy tool aliases** (compatibility: callable names removed).
  These were pre-consolidation tool names that each renamed one router call
  and injected its action. They now return `tool_not_found_error`, refused
  before any identity step runs. Use the router call instead: `agent`
  (`list_agents`, `get_agent_metadata`, `update_agent_metadata`,
  `archive_agent`, `delete_agent`), `observe` (`observe_agent`,
  `compare_agents`, `compare_me_to_similar`, `detect_anomalies`,
  `aggregate_metrics`), `dialectic` (`request_dialectic_review`,
  `submit_thesis`, `submit_antithesis`, `submit_synthesis`,
  `reassign_reviewer`, `get_dialectic_session`, `list_dialectic_sessions`),
  `knowledge` (`store_knowledge_graph`, `get_knowledge_graph`,
  `list_knowledge_graph`, `update_discovery_status_graph`,
  `get_discovery_details`, `cleanup_knowledge_graph`, `get_lifecycle_stats`),
  `calibration` (`check_calibration`, `update_calibration_ground_truth`,
  `backfill_calibration_from_dialectic`, `rebuild_calibration`), `export`
  (`get_system_history`, `export_to_file`) and `admin` (`get_connection_status`, `get_tool_usage_stats`, `get_telemetry_metrics`,
  `debug_request_context`, `validate_file_path`, `reset_monitor`,
  `cleanup_stale_locks`), each with the action its old name implied. No
  router, action, identity requirement or read/write class changes, and the
  advertised surface digest does not move. Server hints and tool
  descriptions that named the old tools now show the router call, and the
  router descriptions no longer list the tools they replaced. Only the
  eight workflow aliases remain, plus `get_server_info`, which stays because
  the Wave 3a BEAM route is keyed on that name. The read/write classes some of these
  aliases carried now live in `tool_meta.ACTION_OPERATIONS`, so timeout
  recovery for router reads is unchanged. (#2593)

## [3.1.0] - 2026-09-28

### Added
- **dialectic:** `dialectic(action="consult")` files an outside verdict on a review (a Codex or other-model consult, a subagent council seat) as a governed transcript record stamped `reviewer_kind: "external_consult"`, with the filer's role (paused agent, reviewer or third party) and the phase at filing. It needs no reviewer slot, so any bound agent can file one on any session, open or closed. It has no authority: it never advances a phase, never counts as a verdict (`agrees` stays NULL; the stated position is kept in `observed_metrics.consult.position`), and never refreshes the session's liveness clock, so it cannot overturn a standing rejection or hold a stalled session open. A session keeps at most 8. The previously documented route, an antithesis with `reviewer_kind="external_consult"`, needed the reviewer slot, which the orchestrated reviewer takes within about a minute, and had never been used (0 rows). What it does not claim: a consult is not an independent review of the session's thesis unless the model that produced it was independent of the author, and the server cannot verify that; `reviewer_provenance` is descriptive, never identity proof. Multi-seat review (a council as one record with several seats) is not built. Interface contract 1.24.0 (#2540).
- **knowledge search:** `recency_half_life_days` on `knowledge(action="search")` and `search_shared_memory` is an opt-in recency weight, off by default (operator decision, 2026-09-27). With a query, each result's relevance is multiplied by `0.5 ** (age_days / recency_half_life_days)` before authority ranking, so a result that many days old keeps half its score and a fresh match can overtake an older, slightly stronger one. It is a true half-life. The AGE backend's own blend on the semantic leg is `1 / (1 + age / 90)`, which is also 0.5 at 90 days but decays more slowly after that, and it still applies underneath. The weight reads the same score authority ranking reads (cross-encoder, then RRF, then semantic similarity, then a full-text row's rank, then position). RRF is rank-derived and nearly flat, so on a hybrid search the weight dominates the order. Authority ranking still applies on top, and the candidate pool widens to the same 50 rows so a fresh match just below the first page can be retrieved to be lifted. It is refused with `sort_by="created_at"`, which already orders by time, and without a query, where results are already newest first. The response echoes it. Through `search_shared_memory` the echo now reaches the envelope's `state_summary`, together with #2517's `sort_by`, `created_after` and `created_before`: #2517 echoed those in `knowledge`'s response and lean digest, but the alias's envelope lifts a fixed list of search fields and dropped them. They appear only when they differ from the default. Accepted values are above 0 and at most 36500 days. Interface contract 1.25.0; to stay inside the progressive tool-listing ratchet, `search_shared_memory`'s `include_details` brief and `knowledge`'s `sort_by` brief are shortened (full descriptions unchanged). What it does not claim: no default half-life is set or recommended, and no retrieval evaluation backs any particular value; the retrieval eval's labels have decayed and cannot currently score one (#2564).
- **dialectic:** Wave 3 §7 step 1 observability instrumentation (`instrument_version: wave3-instrument-v2`). Every periodic dialectic sweep cycle and every guarded sweeper write now records a balanced audit row (read state, decision/early-check/commit times, outcome, winner, attempt id), every Python session-write chokepoint records an attempt/response pair with the database-clock `effect_ts` the write itself returned, and a post-write saga probe is time-correlated. Emit failures are counted in-process and appended to a durable, fsynced ledger (`data/dialectic/instrument_emit_failures.jsonl`) so a stalled audit sink cannot silently under-count. `scripts/ops/wave3_writer_inventory.py` and the read-only `scripts/ops/wave3_collision_report.py` (a pre-registered completeness and harm/ambiguous/contention classifier) ship alongside it. Python only: no Elixir change, no migration, and refusal semantics are unchanged — this is observability, not a new gate. New environment variables are in `FLAGS.md` (#2558).

### Changed
- **knowledge search:** agent-to-agent channel messages rank below native findings in close relevance contests (operator decision, 2026-09-27). A discovery is `authority.tier="channel_message"` when it carries a `channel-<topic>` tag together with a `to-<agent>` tag, or a `[channel:<topic>]` summary prefix: the convention cross-harness coordination used on the KG before the lease-plane message transport replaced it (#1960). It gets the 0.55 multiplier imported memory already gets, so a channel note still ranks first when it is the only strong match, and it remains searchable. A `channel-` tag alone does not qualify (`channel-detection` is an ordinary topic). Imported memory keeps its own tier when a row carries both markers. A `channel-*` tag filter lifts the down-rank only from channel messages on that lane; every other row keeps its authority order, because a `channel-` tag can also be an ordinary topic. A filter made only of source-memory tags still reads that lane in raw order, but a mixed filter (tags match any-of, so it also returns ordinary findings) now keeps the authority order, which narrows the exemption source-memory tags had. Authority ranking also sees a pool of max(limit, 50) candidates before the page is cut: with hybrid and the reranker off it saw only `limit`, so a native finding just below a page of down-ranked rows could never be lifted, and `authority_mode="all"` still returns raw relevance order. Full and compact responses name the tier in `authority_policy` when a channel message is among the results. Measured 2026-09-27 on the relational table, 78 rows match the rule, 61 of them open. On the live KG, "PR merged review" at limit 10 went from channel notes in positions 1-3 and 6 of 10 to native findings in the top five and three channel notes at 6-8. Nothing is excluded, archived or retired (#2537).
- **metrics catalog:** the shipped catalog behind `GET /v1/metrics/catalog` and the `POST /v1/metrics` write gate now names only product metrics. The seven series about this operator's own deployment (`tokei.unitares.src.code`, `tests.unitares.count`, `governance.sentinel.findings.7d` and the four `github.cirwel.traffic.*` series) moved next to their producer in `agents/chronicler/metrics_catalog.json`, which the server registers, `.error` twins included, only when `UNITARES_METRICS_CATALOG_EXTRA` names it. Unset (the default) lists and accepts none of them; a missing, unreadable or malformed file or entry is skipped with a `WARNING`, and an extra entry never overrides a core one. A deployment running the reference Chronicler must set the variable before upgrading, or its next scrape of those seven names is refused with 404. Stored points stay readable through `/v1/metrics/series` either way (#2526).
- **resident progress:** the `metrics_series` progress source no longer carries a hardcoded list that named one deployment's repository metrics. It counts the core catalog's product series (`agents.active.7d`, `kg.entries.count`, `checkins.7d`) plus every series a deployment's extra metrics catalog (`UNITARES_METRICS_CATALOG_EXTRA`) marks `"progress": true`. The reference Chronicler's `agents/chronicler/metrics_catalog.json` marks its repository-size and test-count series, so a deployment that points the variable at that file counts exactly the five series it counted before and its progress verdict does not change; the GitHub-traffic series stay unmarked because they measure the repository rather than the resident. An install with no extra catalog counts only the three product series. Deployments that copied the reference catalog into their own file should add `"progress": true` to the series that stand for their metrics resident's work; without it, those rows stop counting toward progress and a resident whose only output is those series can read as flat. A non-boolean `progress` value skips that entry with a warning (#2574).
- **review gate:** a review is no longer carried across a base merge when the PR touches a second-family path (the paths `scripts/dev/review_policy.json` lists, read from the base branch). A base merge that moves the diff key of such a PR now needs a fresh review on the new key rather than carrying the old one, as before the carry. A base merge that leaves the PR's files untouched keeps the key, and its records still count. A PR whose changed paths cannot be read is treated the same way. Every other PR keeps the carry across clean automatic base merges (operator decision, 2026-09-28) (#2581).
- **review gate:** the review round cap is switched off. Every round gets a full review however many came before, and the `review` check shows only the round count. The mechanism is kept behind `ROUND_CAP_ENABLED` in `scripts/dev/review_gate.py`, and its tests still run, so it can be switched back on without being rebuilt.
- **`/mcp/` tool results are compact JSON text** (no API change). The MCP
  transport parsed each handler's JSON into a dict and returned it to
  FastMCP, which rendered it back to text with `indent=2`. Every `/mcp/`
  result therefore reached the agent's context indented, one array element
  per line, while REST (`/v1/tools/call`) already answered compactly. The
  registered tools now hand FastMCP one text block rendered by the same
  serializer with the same fallback and no indent
  (`src/tool_registration.py`, `_mcp_wire_result`), so the text differs from
  before only in whitespace outside strings and parses to the same value.
  Nothing else changes on the wire: every tool registers with
  `structured_output=False`, so no tool advertises an outputSchema and no
  result carries structuredContent, on mcp 1.x or 2.x; the text block was
  and remains the whole result. The non-JSON fallback
  (`{"success": true, "text": ...}`) and the wrapper's error envelope keep
  their shape. `get_tool_wrapper` still returns the dict that `use_tool`'s
  nested invoker reads. Measured basis: over 14 days of one deployment's
  local Claude Code transcripts, indentation was 13.5% of governance
  tool-result bytes (about 19.5 KB a day), 11.7% since 2026-09-26, and
  largest on `describe_tool` (23%), `list_tools` (20%) and
  `check_working_state` (15%). Tokens saved are fewer than bytes, because
  runs of spaces tokenize cheaply. The SDK's check-in and metrics readers
  are pinned to resolve the verdict, coherence, risk and E/I/S/V from the
  compact text (`tests/test_mcp_text_compact.py`) (#2590).

### Fixed
- **agent archive and delete act only on the agent a call names (#2532):** `agent(action='delete', confirm=true)` sent without `agent_id` deleted the caller's own agent, and `agent(action='archive')` without one archived it. Dispatch's identity step wrote the session's id into `agent_id` for every call that is not a browse, and `describe_tool(lite=true)` hid `agent_id` on both actions, so a caller that followed the lite view sent exactly that call. A second route led to the same place: an `agent_id` that resolves to no registered agent, such as a typo or a label the server does not hold, fell back to the caller's bound agent inside `require_registered_agent`, so `archive(agent_id='<typo>')` archived the caller. Now dispatch no longer injects the session's id for `archive` or `delete`, including through the legacy `archive_agent` and `delete_agent` names and the `op` selector. A call with no `agent_id` (or a blank one) is refused with `TARGET_AGENT_REQUIRED`, whose recovery says to pass `agent_id` and how to find it. Only the target's own UUID selects it: a UUID no agent holds is refused with `TARGET_AGENT_NOT_FOUND`, and anything that is not a UUID (a label, a public id, or a legacy row's non-UUID key) is refused with `TARGET_AGENT_UUID_REQUIRED`. A public id is shared by most identities that carry one, and review of this change found that `delete(agent_id='<shared handle>')` would otherwise delete whichever holder the metadata cache listed first. Naming yourself by your own UUID still works. The lite view of `archive` and `delete` lists `agent_id` as required at call time, and `delete`'s `confirm` too. The lite view of `get` and `resume` lists `agent_id` as their optional target. `get`, `update`, `resume` and `release_presence` keep their session default. Interface contract 1.23.0 (after #2517's 1.22.0): `agent`'s `agent_id` description changes, which moves its input digest and the surface digest. No parameter is added, removed, retyped or renamed.
- **fresh install:** first-run rough edges found by walking a new Docker stack. `/health/deep` no longer calls a default install unhealthy: with no embedder installed and no `UNITARES_EMBEDDING_MODEL` named, `knowledge_graph` reports `not_configured` (search runs on Postgres full-text search), and an audit log no event has written yet reports `no_data_yet`. Both are counted in `status_breakdown`, listed in the new `operator_summary.not_configured_checks`, and leave `overall_status` alone; a requested embedder that is missing is still `degraded`, and an audit log whose directory cannot be written is still a `warning`. `make coordination-demo` now refuses, before contacting anything, a run whose environment moves one half of the stack off its default port and says nothing about the other, which had let it register participants on an unrelated server; its failure hint names both ports. The dashboard's Security tab shows the passkey setup hint instead of a red 403 when passkeys are not configured, the Overview labels verdicts in the server's proceed / guide / pause vocabulary with risk ticks at the real default tier edges (0.45, 0.70), and residentless, host-specific and development wording is gone from the EISV, Risk and Activity tabs, the footer and the enrollment page (#2533).
- **fresh-install leakage, third pass:** `.env.example` now ships `UNITARES_KNOWLEDGE_BACKEND=postgres`, the default the code and `docker-compose.yml` already use; it said `age` "(recommended)", so a non-Docker install copying it needed the Apache AGE extension it was never told to install. `verify_trajectory_identity`'s missing-signature recovery says to include the signature the caller's runtime computed instead of pointing every caller at one operator's embodied runtime (#2552).
- **identity: the identity surface's events reach the broadcaster again, without holding up identity resolution:** `_broadcaster()` in `src/mcp_handlers/identity/handlers.py` and `persistence.py` imported `broadcaster` from `src.broadcaster`, a name the module never defined (the singleton is `broadcaster_instance`). The accessors swallowed the ImportError and returned None, so every event they gate was skipped since those accessors landed in April 2026 (#70, #78): `identity_hijack_suspected` from the PATH 0 continuity-token gate (handler and middleware), the PATH 1 fingerprint and token-mismatch checks, the synchronous fingerprint check and the PATH 2 IP:UA pin check, plus `pg_session_collision` and `resident_fork_detected`. The warning log lines were written all along, but the WebSocket stream, `audit.events` and anything subscribed to them saw nothing. Every emission test patched the accessor with a mock, so none of them noticed. Both accessors now import `broadcaster_instance` and hand it out behind a thin wrapper whose `broadcast_event` schedules the broadcast as a tracked background task and returns at once. The broadcaster awaits its WebSocket fan-out (up to 2 s per stalled client plus 2 s to close it), and the REST `client_session_id` corroboration lookup gives identity resolution 0.5 s, so an awaited event could have turned a stalled dashboard tab into a failed corroboration; a telemetry event now never decides whether a resolution finishes. The synchronous fingerprint check, which cannot await, used a bare `loop.create_task` that the loop references only weakly; it now schedules through `create_tracked_task` as well. New tests call the accessors unpatched, hold every `from src.broadcaster import ...` under `src/` to a name the module defines, and time a fingerprint mismatch against a stalled fan-out. What changes for an operator: these events now appear on `/ws/eisv`, in `/api/events` and in `audit.events`. `identity_hijack_suspected` fires once per fingerprint mismatch in the default `log` mode, most of which come from benign co-resident twins, so a relay that posts every broadcaster event to chat (the Discord bridge does, with no repeat suppression for this type) posts one message per mismatch unless it suppresses or de-duplicates the type. No gate decision, tool description, input schema or interface contract changed (#2559).
- **the knowledge-graph lifecycle counts only the moves the backend made:** `KnowledgeGraphLifecycle._batch_update_status` awaited each `update_discovery` and dropped its result. Both backends return False for an update they did not make (the Postgres backend when no row has the id, the AGE backend when its query fails and rolls back), so a refused archive or cold move was still counted in the cleanup summary, logged as done, and returned as moved. Before migration 071 this is what happened to every classified resolved row: the AGE update refused the archive on each run, and each run reported the row archived. Each pass now returns only the ids the backend confirmed, and `ephemeral_archived`, `discoveries_archived`, `discoveries_to_cold` and `tags_canonicalized` count only those; the tag-canonicalization pass had the same dropped result and is fixed the same way. A refused or raising update is one row's failure: the rest of the pass still runs, the row keeps its status and is a candidate again next run, one warning per pass names up to 20 of the ids with the exact count, and the summary's new `failed_updates` map gives, per affected count, the number that failed and the same bounded sample of ids. It is empty when nothing failed and always empty in a dry run. The in-process lifecycle health record reads `degraded` with the per-pass numbers after a run with failed updates, where it read `healthy`. Existing summary keys and their types are unchanged, so the cleanup tool response, the SDK's `CleanupResult.cleaned_total` and the startup and periodic sweeps read the same fields, now with the real counts (#2567).
- **knowledge updates bound the details value they store; the lifecycle's dead PG-sync block is gone:** `resolution_notes` are appended to a finding's `details` as a timestamped block, and nothing bounded the result, so repeated closing notes (or one long log excerpt) grew the field without limit. On the AGE backend `details` is a Cypher-interpolated node property and `GraphMixin._sanitize_cypher_param` refuses any string over 128 KiB; the whole update, status included, rolled back and the caller was told `Discovery '<id>' not found` for a finding that exists. The update handler now measures the value it would store (the stored details, or the details the call sends, earlier notes included, plus the new block) against `MAX_UPDATED_DETAILS_LEN` (96 KiB, in `src/mcp_handlers/knowledge/limits.py`) and refuses an update over it with `INVALID_PARAM` before anything is written. The recovery gives the number of characters of notes that would fit, or, when the stored details leave no room, says to close without notes and record them as a new finding that responds to this one. A `details` replacement sent without notes is held to the same bound, since it is the same stored field. A finding stored at the 64 KiB details cap still takes about 32 KiB of notes; status-only updates are not measured. `KnowledgeGraphLifecycle._batch_update_status` no longer carries its second "PG sync" write: it imported `get_postgres_backend` from `src.db.postgres_backend`, which never existed, and swallowed the `ImportError` at debug level, so it never ran. Both backends' `update_discovery` already write `knowledge.discoveries` (the AGE backend in the same transaction as the graph node), so lifecycle behaviour is unchanged (#2530).
- **knowledge writes bound the rest of the text they send to the graph; supersede applies its `resolution_notes`:** on the AGE backend every value a write stores goes through `GraphMixin._sanitize_cypher_param`, which refuses a string, or a dict serialized with `json.dumps`, over 128 KiB. #2530 bounded `details` on update; the other caller-controlled values were still unbounded. An update's `summary` or `tags` over that limit rolled the whole update back and the caller was told `Discovery '<id>' not found`; a store's tags or node metadata (`related_files`, the provenance fields a call sends such as `memory_context`, a batch item's `provenance`) failed with the sanitizer's own "Cypher dict param too long" text. Each is now refused with `INVALID_PARAM` before anything is written, with a recovery naming what to shorten: an update's `summary` above 4,003 characters (`MAX_SUMMARY_LEN` plus the `...` a store appends when it truncates, so a stored summary sent back unchanged still passes); more than 50 tags or a tag over 128 characters, counted after normalization, on store, batch store, note, promote and update; and node metadata over 32 KiB as the AGE store serializes it, on store, batch store (as that item's error), note and promote. The limits live in `src/mcp_handlers/knowledge/limits.py`. `knowledge(action='supersede')` listed `resolution_notes` but never read them, so the notes were dropped. They are now appended to the older finding's details in the same update that marks it superseded, exactly as an update appends them, within the same 96 KiB details bound; on a high or critical finding only its owner may send them, checked again on the read taken just before the flip so a finding raised to high or critical meanwhile is held to it, matching update's rule that a non-owner adds notes there only while closing it as resolved, closed or wont_fix. A refused non-owner is pointed to a finding that responds to the older one, not to a supersede without the notes. A supersede without notes behaves as before; whether a non-owner may mark another agent's high or critical finding superseded at all is left open (#2569).
- **identity: a claimed name that a collision renames never becomes the label the server assigned:** `set_agent_label_resolved` renames a claim of a label another agent holds to `<name>_<uuid8>`, appending this agent's own uuid8. That is also how the mint builds its label (`claude_code-opus_<uuid8>`) and how a knowledge write names an agent with no meaningful label (`Agent_<uuid8>`). So a claim of the bare mint stem (`claude_code-opus`) while another agent held it became this agent's own mint label, byte-identical to the recorded `auto_label`, and `agent_signature` and the knowledge display payload reported `label_source: "auto"` for a name the caller claimed; `docs/ontology/identity.md` listed this as the one exception (#2490). When `<name>_<uuid8>` equals a label the server recorded for this agent, in memory or in `core.identities.metadata` (which the cold-start loader restores), the rename now takes the uuid's first two groups (`<name>_<uuid8>-<4 hex>`), or the whole uuid if another agent holds even that. Every other collision rename keeps the `<name>_<uuid8>` form, and the exception is gone from the ontology. For a knowledge write's `Agent_<uuid8>`, a claim of `Agent` that collides now lands on `Agent_<uuid8>-<4 hex>` instead of the auto-name's own string; it already read `claimed`, because the claim clears the in-memory auto-name, and a claim of the exact string `Agent_<uuid8>` still relies on that clear. Stored labels are not rewritten: an agent whose claim was renamed to its own mint label before this change keeps that label, and it reads `auto` until the agent claims a name again. No tool description, input schema or interface contract changed (#2528). The rename also avoids the agent's structured id (`{interface}_{model}_{date}_{uuid8}`, the same shape), which a fresh mint gets only when the label setter runs: it is now generated before the rename is chosen, so claiming its date-stamped stem while another agent holds it no longer reproduces it (#2557). One residual remains: `identity(name=..., model_type=...)` can regenerate the structured id with the model after the label is set, so on an agent whose structured id lacked the model, a claim of the model-aware stem can still read `auto` (noted in `docs/ontology/identity.md`).
- **fleet-identity guard:** tool input schemas are now checked as the prose they are. `scripts/dev/check_fleet_identity_leak.py` flagged a string literal in shipped Python only when the literal *was* a resident name, and #2493 held the served files (skills, `tool_descriptions.json`, the dashboard) to a stricter word-level rule. The schema text fell between the two: a Pydantic `Field`'s `description=` and its `json_schema_extra` `"brief"`, a schema model's docstring (served as the schema's own description) and the alias overrides in `src/alias_schema.py` are Python literals that every connecting agent is served, and a resident name inside a longer description passed. A review of #2489 caught one in `observe`'s `target_agent_id` that the guard had passed. In `src/mcp_handlers/schemas/*.py` and `src/alias_schema.py` those literals now get the served-prose rule: a resident name anywhere, as a whole word in any case, fails the build ("Sentinels" and "vigilant" still pass), and neither `NOT_IDENTITIES` nor `KNOWN_COUPLINGS` defers it. A description built from a module-level constant is read through the constant; other literals in those modules keep the literal rule. The rule is static, because the guard runs where the project's dependencies are not installed, and a test cross-checks it against the schemas Pydantic builds. Current master and the open PR that edits schema text pass with no new exemption. Tool-description text assembled in `src/tool_descriptions.py` is still checked by the literal rule only (#2536).
- **agent archive and delete need a named target over REST too:** #2532 made `agent(action='archive')` and `agent(action='delete')` refuse a call that names no `agent_id` instead of acting on the caller, but only on `/mcp/`. Every REST prebind path (`/v1/tools/call`, and `use_tool` nested under it: the operator token, the sticky binding and the session binding) wrote the resolved caller into an omitted `agent_id` before the handler ran, so over REST such a call still archived or deleted the caller. The prebind now reads whether the call named a target before any path writes one, and for the calls `/mcp/`'s identity step leaves alone (`_EXPLICIT_TARGET_CALLS`, including the `archive_agent` and `delete_agent` aliases and `op=`) it takes the stamp off again once the caller is bound. The decision reads the call as dispatch will see it after unwrapping a `kwargs` wrapper (a dict or a JSON string), since the stamp lands on the outer arguments and an action inside `kwargs` only surfaces later, so the handler refuses with `TARGET_AGENT_REQUIRED`. Other calls keep the session default. A REST caller that archived or deleted itself by leaving `agent_id` out now gets `TARGET_AGENT_REQUIRED` and passes its own UUID, as on `/mcp/`. Whether `agent(action='resume')` should follow the same rule is an open decision; because the REST prebind now reads the shared call set, adding resume there would apply it on both transports (#2579).
- **review gate:** a base merge no longer voids a finished review when the PR's own lines are unchanged. The diff key hashes each changed file's blob, so a merge from master that edited text next to a PR's lines moved the key and left the `review` check UNREVIEWED. That covers GitHub's "update branch" for auto-merge and a plain merge of the base. It happened twice on #2499, and on #2519 while it was armed for auto-merge. The gate now treats the earlier keys as the same diff when two things hold. Every merge since must be a clean automatic merge of the base: `git merge-tree` reproduces its tree with no conflict. And the fingerprint of the PR's added and removed lines must be unchanged. Records on those keys, and native Codex reviews of those earlier heads, then count as the head's: the review, its findings, its dispositions and its model families. An open finding stays open until disposed. The check says "carried across a base merge from `<sha>`". A conflict resolution, any edit made in a merge, a new commit, or any change to an added or removed line stops the carry. The local `review`, `record` and `dispose` commands read records the same way (#2568).
- **session cleanup:** the six-hourly `session_cleanup_task` no longer deletes a Redis session binding that is still in use. A `core.sessions` row expires 24 hours after bind unless a lookup reaches PostgreSQL, but a lookup served from Redis slides only the Redis key's TTL. The cleanup deleted `session:<key>` for every expired row, so a long-lived client that kept resolving through Redis lost its binding at the next pass. Every later call then missed both stores and was refused as `pg_session_missing` until the client re-onboarded. From 2026-09-01 to 09-07 this stranded three discord-bridge keys. Each flood of misses began right after a pass that deleted one Redis key and lasted until the bridge restarted: 74,750, 22,298 and 128,200 miss rows. The cleanup now reads each key's TTL first. It deletes only a key with no expiry (TTL -1), which is the orphan the delete was added for. It leaves a key with a positive TTL for Redis to expire once its client goes idle, and a key whose TTL cannot be read. Its Redis awaits are now bounded. The PG rows are deleted as before, and the log line adds the number of live bindings kept. This does not cover the 09-16 to 09-24 flood (994,848 rows). That one began when Redis restarted from a snapshot older than the bridge's binding, and durability of Redis-only sessions is a separate question (#2531).
- **skills (freshness stamps):** an agent whose harness gives its command a pseudo-terminal can no longer stamp a skill under the git user's name. #2529 refused `scripts/client/check-skill-freshness.sh --stamp` without `SKILL_ATTESTATION_VERIFIER` when stdin was not a terminal, and kept the git `user.name` fallback for a person at one. A TTY on stdin was the only test for a person, and it does not hold for every agent: Codex's `exec_command` takes `tty: true` and Hermes' terminal tool takes `pty`, so an agent command run either way still recorded the operator's git name as its verifier. The stamp now also takes the caller for an agent or a job when a variable an agent harness or CI runner sets is present: `CLAUDECODE` (Claude Code), `CODEX_THREAD_ID` and `CODEX_CI` (Codex), `AI_AGENT` (Claude Code and Hermes) and `CI` (CI runners). Each was seen set before it was listed: in a Claude Code shell, in the `env` output recorded in Codex session transcripts, in the command wrapper of Hermes' terminal tool, and in this repository's Tests workflow log. An empty, `0` or `false` value does not count, so a person who exports `CI=false` keeps the fallback. The refusal names the variable it found, and says separately when stdin is not a terminal. A person at a terminal with none of these variables set is unaffected, and a caller that sets `SKILL_ATTESTATION_VERIFIER` is accepted as before, marker or not. A harness that sets none of these variables and gives its command a terminal still reaches the fallback; other harnesses (Cursor, Gemini CLI, Antigravity) were not checked. Existing attestations are not rewritten (#2555).
- **check-ins:** a bounded `sync_state` response (the default compact or mirror check-in, and the `compact`, `mirror`, `standard` and `minimal` modes) no longer sets `raw_governance_available: true`. That flag promises a re-call that fetches the omitted payload by reading, which is what it means on `check_working_state` and `search_shared_memory`, whose hint names the same read at a fuller tier. A check-in has no such read. Its omitted payload is that check-in's decision (reason, margin, policy gates, enforcement, `prediction_id`, warnings), and `check_working_state(verbosity="full")` and `get_governance_metrics` report the agent's current state rather than what one check-in decided. The only route to that payload was `response_mode="full"` on the next `sync_state`, which records another check-in, so an agent that trusted the flag and re-called to read it would write again. The flag is now set only by the two read aliases, as a routine `start_session` and the `store_finding`, `update_finding` and `record_result` acknowledgements already did not set it. `raw_governance_hint` is unchanged and still names `response_mode="full"` on the next `sync_state`. A `sync_state` with `response_mode="full"` still carries the payload under `raw_governance`. The integration guide and the manual say so. No tool description or input schema changes, so the interface contract version and digests are unchanged (#2527).
- **REST trusted networks:** the local-posture auth bypass no longer trusts `100.64.0.0/10` by default. That range is where Tailscale assigns addresses, and some ISPs also use it for carrier-grade NAT. Building it in meant every install trusted one operator's tailnet layout. Loopback and the RFC1918 private ranges stay built in. An operator whose callers come over a tailnet or another overlay adds it with `UNITARES_TRUSTED_NETWORKS` (comma-separated CIDRs). The LaunchAgent template and `.env.example` document the setting. **Upgrade note:** a deployment that relied on the implicit Tailscale trust must set `UNITARES_TRUSTED_NETWORKS=100.64.0.0/10`, or its tailnet callers will get 401 on REST and the dashboard WebSocket. On a dual-stack bind, an IPv4 caller's IPv4-mapped address (`::ffff:a.b.c.d`) is now matched against the IPv4 ranges too, built-in and listed, so the same setting works there. uvicorn now applies `X-Forwarded-For` from every loopback address in every form (`127.0.0.0/8`, `::1/128`, `::ffff:127.0.0.0/104`), so a reverse proxy on the same host that sets that header passes on its caller's address instead of its own loopback trust. A forwarder that sets no header (socat, an SSH tunnel), or a proxy on a trusted non-loopback address, still passes its callers through with its own trust; front those with `UNITARES_REST_STRICT` or a bearer (#2560).
- **an unknown tool name no longer mints an identity before it is refused:**
  the dispatch pipeline ran its pre-steps, identity resolution among them,
  before it looked the tool up. Identity resolution treats a name it cannot
  classify as identity-required, which is the fail-closed default, and on a
  session miss outside strict identity it takes the auto-mint retry
  (`spawn_reason="dispatch_auto_mint"`). A REST or `use_tool` caller that
  guessed a name with no handler therefore got a fresh, persisted identity
  and then `TOOL_NOT_FOUND`, one ghost per call. `run_tool_dispatch_pipeline`
  now refuses a name that neither a handler nor an alias answers before any
  pre-step runs, so the refusal leaves no identity behind. Names a handler or alias does answer are unaffected
  and run the same steps in the same order. MCP clients were mostly spared,
  because a name missing from `tools/list` is refused before dispatch; the
  path mattered more once the 24 unadvertised aliases were removed, since
  names such as `status` and `hello` are plausible first-call guesses.
- **inference:** a provider's usage cooldown now survives a governance-MCP restart. `host_availability` kept each provider's cooldown window in-process only, so every restart forgot a provider at its limit and spent a failed call rediscovering it. Each cooldown is now written through to Redis (key `unitares:host_cooldown:<host_id>`, TTL matching the remaining window) and refilled into the in-process cache at startup; reads stay synchronous and in-process. Existing window rules are unchanged (a guess inside a live window changes nothing, a stated reset wins, 12h cap), Redis writes for one host are serialized against its own recovery deletes, and every Redis await is bounded — Redis absent, raising or hanging falls back to in-process behavior only, never an exception (#2503).
- **observability:** `GET /v1/api/incidents` now says how each incident type is produced instead of only whether rows exist. `anomaly_detected` is written only when a caller runs `detect_anomalies`, and nothing schedules that call, so after the dashboard's Anomalies card was removed (#2494) an empty feed for that type read as a quiet fleet rather than an unscheduled producer. Each type's entry in the response now carries a `producers` block (cadence — `on_demand` or `scheduled` — the writer, what an absence means, and the newest row); an empty feed is reported `undetermined` rather than implying either a clean run or a failed one, since neither producer records its own runs here. `limit=0` is clamped to at least 1 (#2535).
- **inference:** a classified model-call failure now logs one `WARNING` line instead of a chained traceback at `ERROR`. Since #2495 declared the `openai` SDK, a consult on an install with no model configured reaches the connection attempt every time, so each expected, already-reported failure (provider unreachable, model not pulled, timeout, rate limit) wrote a multi-frame traceback. Only an unclassified failure still logs at `ERROR` with the traceback. What the caller receives is unchanged (#2545).
- **write timeouts:** a tool call that can write no longer answers its timeout with advice to try again. The `@mcp_tool` timeout ends the wait for the reply, not the work. On 2026-09-27 an `update_finding` on the AGE backend returned `TIMEOUT` with "Try again with simpler parameters" after its row had committed, and because `resolution_notes` append, following that advice writes them twice. The timeout reply for a write, admin or unclassified call now says the outcome is unknown and the change may have been saved. It carries `outcome: "unknown"`, `operation`, `call_started_at` and `settled_by`: the reply time plus the pool's 30 s command timeout, after which a database statement still running at the timeout has finished. `settled_by` bounds nothing else. The recovery offers a resend only where the work is bounded by `settled_by` and the read it names covers everything the call would have written, and then only on a read after `settled_by`. That holds for a knowledge store, note or update, each of which commits one database transaction per row. Every other write, for example `export(action='file')`, whose file write runs on an executor thread that outlives the cancelled await, gets no resend step: its reply says the outcome cannot be settled by reading, because a tool can also write files, start background tasks or call other services that finish later, and tells the caller to inspect the effect with a read-only tool (`describe_tool` lists them) and not to send the call again blind. For a knowledge update, every field it sends is set, not added to, except `resolution_notes`, so the check is `knowledge(action='details', discovery_id=..., length=98304)`, which reads all of details up to the largest value an update stores, paging on while `has_more` says a row is longer. The caller looks anywhere in details, not only at its end, since another writer can append after it, for the block this call would have written: a `Resolution notes (<time>):` line stamped at or after `call_started_at`, followed by its text. The text alone proves nothing, because an earlier block can hold the same words. The update writes all its fields in one statement, so a found block, when every other field sent also shows the value sent, shows the whole update landed and nothing of it is sent again (a block alone can be another call's that sent the same notes, so the caller does not resend blind), except that an update with `superseded_by` records its edge in a separate write afterwards, so the reply adds a step to run the idempotent `knowledge(action='supersede')` once the fields are confirmed; the caller resends only if the block is absent from the whole of details. An update without `resolution_notes` only sets fields, so it can be sent again after `settled_by`. `updated_at` settles nothing, because any writer's update moves it. For a store or note the check is a queryless `knowledge(action='search')` filtered to the author the handler records (the bound identity, the anonymous `anonkg_*` pseudonym, or the `agent_id` the call passed) and to rows created between `call_started_at` and `settled_by`, newest first, archived and cold rows included, limit 100. It needs no bound identity, and `check_arguments` carries it as arguments. A relevance search could lose the row off its ranked page and match another writer's row with the same summary. The window lookup reads `knowledge.discoveries` on both backends, so a list below the limit is every row that author created in the window, each with its full details; a single store is matched on summary and details together, both for finding its row and for deciding it may be resent, since the same writer can store two rows with one summary in the window; a note up to the summary limit is matched exactly, since it is stored as sent, and a longer one is matched when its text is the row's summary, nothing or only whitespace, then its details, since it is split between the two and the split drops the whitespace it falls on; one over the combined limit is first cut to it and marked `... [truncated]`. The reply says when the author id is not the caller's alone, and then only an absent row is proof. A store with `supersedes` marks the old row superseded in a second write the list does not show, so its reply adds a step to run `knowledge(action='supersede')` once the new row is found, whatever the old row shows: it sets a status and merges an edge, so it adds nothing twice and repairs an edge a timeout between the two writes left missing. A batch store (`discoveries`) commits each item on its own, so a timed-out batch can be partly saved. Its reply says how many items the batch held and never has the caller resend the whole batch. Its lookup includes each row's full details, and the caller compares each item with the rows by its summary and details together, not by summary alone, and resends only the items no row matches. Those two fields are stored as sent (cut short when long); `discovery_type`, severity and tags are normalized when stored, so the caller does not match on them. Items that agree on summary and details cannot be told apart, so once a row matches them the reply says resending cannot be made safe for them. `onboard` (and `start_session`) and `identity` are listed as reads but can create an identity, so their timeouts get this reply too, with a recovery that says a repeat creates another identity. A call that names its binding with `client_session_id` is told to read that binding instead, unless it asked for a new identity: `force_new=true`, or `resume=false` on `onboard`, read as the handler reads it, which mints a new identity beside the named binding. On `identity`, `resume=false` is the schema default and the call reads the binding. The class comes from the `operation` metadata that `describe_tool` already reports, resolved per router action. `error_code` stays `TIMEOUT`, `error_category` stays `system_error`, and read tools keep the reply they had. On the AGE backend a windowed query whose SQL read fails now raises instead of returning no rows, as the postgres backend already did, so a failed read is not taken for an empty window; semantic search's in-memory fallback, which only ranks candidates, still degrades to none, so a hybrid search keeps its full-text results. Separately, the AGE update no longer waits for the embedding refresh it runs after committing. The first embedding in a process imports sentence-transformers and loads the model, and that load is what outlasted the 10 s timeout in the incident; the refresh now runs as a tracked background task, one pass at a time per discovery so an older pass cannot store stale text last (#2543).
- **coordination events:** an install's own agents can record coordination events under their own service id. Migration 035 locked `audit.coordination_events.service` to six emitter ids, four of them the names of one deployment's resident agents, and the sync emitter rewrote any other id to `governance_mcp`. Migration 072 replaces that list, under the same constraint name, with a format rule: a lowercase identifier (a letter, then letters, digits or underscores, at most 63 characters). `src/coordination_events.py` checks the same pattern before the INSERT (`is_valid_service`), and the sync emitter keeps a well-formed id as given and falls back to `governance_mcp` only for a malformed one. Every id 035 admitted still matches, so no row changes (#2582).
- **fresh installs stop trusting one deployment's runtime vocabulary:** outcome corroboration built in two substrate markers from the reference deployment's embodied runtime (`pi_anima_eisv`, `get_lumen_context`), so every install graded evidence that named them as substrate-observed. They are no longer built in; an operator lists their own runtime's markers in `UNITARES_TRUSTED_SUBSTRATE_MARKERS` (comma-separated, case-insensitive, entries under three characters ignored), and the generic markers such as `sensor_sync` still ship. **Upgrade note:** a deployment whose runtime writes those two markers sets `UNITARES_TRUSTED_SUBSTRATE_MARKERS=pi_anima_eisv,get_lumen_context`, or that evidence grades lower.
- **one non-reasoning tag set:** the stuck sweep, auto-triggered dialectic and the dialectic reviewer pool each carried their own copy of `{"autonomous", "embodied", "anima"}`. They now read one `NON_REASONING_TAGS` in `src/grounding/class_indicator.py`, where `anima` is documented as a deprecated alias of `embodied`, kept only so an agent registered with it alone is still skipped; it stays privileged (not self-assignable) while it confers anything. The `detect_stuck_agents` description no longer names it, and the server port comment no longer describes one host's port layout (#2582).
- **MCP server instructions fit a 1,400-byte budget** (agent-facing text only;
  no tool, schema or digest changes). The `instructions` string every client
  receives at initialize was 3,438 UTF-8 bytes in progressive mode and 3,144
  in full mode. Fourteen days of one operator's local Claude Code and Codex
  transcripts showed Claude Code delivering only about the first 2,088 bytes
  (read from `mcp_instructions_delta.addedBlocks`), so every Claude Code
  session lost the tail: the discovery route (`list_tools(lite=true)`, then
  `describe_tool`, then `use_tool`) and the note that advertised parameter
  descriptions are abridged. The same transcripts showed the text arriving
  twice per session when an agent connects through both a local server and
  the hosted connector, about 297 KB a day of repeated orientation. The
  rewrite is 1,393 bytes (progressive) and 1,386 bytes (full). It keeps the
  product sentence, every positioning phrase the doc-drift lint pins, reading
  before binding, `start_session(force_new=true)` with `client_session_id`
  threading, declared lineage, the full list of pause exits, the workflow
  names the tests pin (`start_session`, `sync_state`, `record_result`,
  `check_working_state`), the discovery route and the abridged note. It no
  longer mentions `request_review`, `consult` or the `dialectic` action list,
  the reconstruction-sources paragraph, or the core-versus-advanced
  reading-paths paragraph; `request_review` and `consult` stay in the initial
  progressive listing with their own descriptions. Operator configuration
  (`UNITARES_TOOL_ADVERTISEMENT=full`, the ignored `GOVERNANCE_TOOL_MODE`)
  left the string, because an agent cannot set it; the interface contract's
  advertisement section documents it and now says the instructions string
  does not. Tests pin the byte budget in both modes and check that each
  required phrase ends inside it. The interface contract does not hash the
  instructions, so its version and surface digest do not move. This measures
  bytes delivered, not any change in agent behavior (#2588).
- **`audit.tool_usage` now counts a call made through `use_tool`:** `use_tool` re-enters its target's own transport path, so a gateway call and a direct call wrote the same row, under the target's name, and `use_tool` itself read as zero uses in the table (verified: a live `use_tool(health_check)` wrote one `health_check` row with an empty payload). The row a gateway call writes now carries a fixed `via=use_tool` payload key so the two are distinguishable; `via` is on the payload's key allowlist and its only value is a gateway name. A direct call's row is unmarked, as before (#2587).
- **knowledge search:** a compact (non-expanded) search result's `summary` is now cut to one line of 200 characters. A search list is a list of leads, and an untruncated summary made a page of results read as a wall of text; `knowledge(action="search", include_details=true)` and every other expanded read keep the full summary (#2586).

### Removed
- **findings:** the HTTP adjudication surface is removed: `GET /v1/sentinel/adjudication-queue`, `POST /v1/sentinel/adjudicate` and `POST /v1/sentinel/model-adjudicate`, the `scripts/ops/model_adjudicator.py` job and its launchd template, and the doctor's `adjudication_feedstock` check with its `UNITARES_OPERATOR_ADJUDICATION` switch. The Sentinel CLI paths (`--resolve`/`--dismiss` via `agents/sentinel/agent.py`) are unchanged and remain the supported way for an operator to record external-truth outcomes. This is a design decision by the maintainer, not a reading of usage: asking a human to judge each finding is performative, because the human would in practice ask a model to verify it, and a model verdict here was only ever telemetry, never an anchor. Removing it loses no evidence: operator verdicts were 2.4% of the external_signal channel, every one `is_bad=false`, and excluding them moved nothing the EISV falsifier reads. Historical `*_confirmed` / `*_dismissed` outcome rows are kept, and the `anchor_all_positive_generator` doctor check still audits them alongside Watcher's own resolve/dismiss labels. Finding intake, the backlog and forced-release evidence are unchanged.
  Deployments that ran the model adjudicator should unload its launchd job (`com.unitares.model-adjudicator`) when they deploy this change; the job would otherwise call routes that now answer 404 every cycle. `UNITARES_MODEL_ADJUDICATOR_TOKEN` in the governance environment and `UNITARES_OPERATOR_ADJUDICATION` on the doctor-findings job are no longer read and can be deleted. A dashboard extension that rendered the queue needs its Adjudication tab removed, since its data route is gone (#2546).
- **24 unadvertised tool aliases** (compatibility: callable names removed).
  Names that only redirected a guessed or pre-consolidation name to a tool
  that keeps its canonical name now return `tool_not_found_error`. Its
  suggestions are fuzzy name matches and usually do not name the
  replacement, so the replacements are listed here: `status`, `my_status`, `check_status`, `metrics`, `state`
  (use `check_working_state` or `get_governance_metrics`); `start`, `init`,
  `register`, `login` (use `start_session` or `onboard`); `checkin`, `log`,
  `update` (use `sync_state` or `process_agent_update`); `authenticate`,
  `session`, `quick_start`, `recall_identity`, `bind_identity`, `hello`,
  `get_agent_api_key` (API keys are deprecated: use
  `start_session(force_new=true)`, pass its `client_session_id` on later
  calls, and inspect the result with `identity(client_session_id=...)`);
  `request_exploration_session` (use
  `dialectic(action="get")`); `find_similar_discoveries_graph`,
  `get_related_discoveries_graph`, `get_response_chain_graph`,
  `reply_to_question` (use `knowledge` with the matching action). No
  capability is removed, the eight advertised workflow aliases are unchanged,
  and the advertised surface digest does not move. Hints that still named
  `get_agent_api_key` now point at `client_session_id` as the proof, and
  those that named `reply_to_question` show the complete
  `knowledge(action="store", summary=..., response_to={...})` call.
  The 38 other legacy aliases, such as `list_agents`, `observe_agent` and
  `store_knowledge_graph`, are unchanged: each shares its name with an
  internal handler, and several still have callers to repoint before they
  can go. The complexity validation error and the `complexity` field text
  now name `sync_state` as the alias that accepts named levels and scale
  objects (#2576).
- **dashboard:** `PLAN.md`, the last dashboard file the server still served that described the operator's own fleet (it named this deployment's residents seven times), is deleted rather than merely auth-gated. Its auth-gate entry, its two token-injection tests, its dashboard README links, its mention in the dashboard skill, and its `SERVED_KNOWN_COUPLINGS` entry go with it; the fleet-identity ratchet tests' example entry moves to `src/tool_descriptions.json`, the one served file the list still names (#2538).

### Security
- **tool text:** shipped tool descriptions no longer name a maintainer, a resident, or a vendor. `tool_descriptions.json` named a resident in a drawing-event example and an `observe()` example, and called the notification bridge by its vendor name; all three now use neutral wording, so the fleet-identity leak guard's `SERVED_KNOWN_COUPLINGS` exemption list is empty (the domain test keeps its coverage with a fixture coupling instead). `docs/dev/TOOL_REFERENCE.md` is regenerated (#2541).
- **auth:** the dashboard's `/ws/eisv` break-glass bearer is no longer sent as a URL query parameter, and uvicorn no longer writes it to the server log. The dashboard sent `/ws/eisv?token=...`, and uvicorn logs every WebSocket handshake request line, query string included, on `uvicorn.error` — so every dashboard connection wrote the live `UNITARES_HTTP_API_TOKEN` into `mcp_server_error.log`, and a tunnel edge saw it in the URL too. The socket now takes the bearer as a `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` entry instead (the `Authorization` header still works); a logging filter on `uvicorn.error`/`uvicorn.access` redacts any query parameter whose name ends in `token`/`key`/`secret`/`password`/`auth`, including a percent-encoded name, and also redacts credential values inside logged tracebacks. A `?token=` page URL is a one-time handoff, persisted to `localStorage` and scrubbed from the address bar, matching `?operator_token=`. `docs/manual` and the dashboard skill are corrected to stop describing the old query-string flow (#2577).
- **fresh install (Docker):** two credential/scope leaks in the Docker install path are hardened. `scripts/unitares`'s CLI onboard flow wrote `~/.unitares_session.json` (`client_session_id`, `continuity_token`, `uuid`) with default file permissions — group/other-readable on at least one machine — and now creates it `0600`, correcting a pre-existing file too. `.dockerignore` did not exclude `.unitares/` or `secrets/`, so a local session/continuity cache was sent to the Docker build context (a real risk with a remote builder) even though neither is ever `COPY`ed into the image. A proposed third change, narrowing the image's `agents/` copy to `agents/sdk/` alone, was reverted after two review rounds each found a live regression it missed by static analysis (the opt-in adjudication route pack and `UNITARES_METRICS_CATALOG_EXTRA` both import outside `agents/sdk/`); shipping the full reference-fleet tree with its operator-specific defaults stays an accepted tradeoff, not a credential leak (#2578).
- **Compose hardening:** every service in `docker-compose.yml` now runs with `no-new-privileges` and `cap_drop: ALL` (Redis gets `SETUID`/`SETGID`/`CHOWN`/`FOWNER` back: the first two so its entrypoint can drop from root to the `redis` user, matching `/data`'s baked-in ownership, and `FOWNER` so its perm-fixup step can `chmod` a file after `chown`ing it away from root, which a volume carrying stale ownership from a prior run can otherwise hit). Postgres and Redis stop publishing to the host by default — Postgres ships a default credential and Redis none, and `127.0.0.1` is a machine boundary, not a user boundary — while `governance-mcp` and the lease plane keep publishing on `127.0.0.1` unchanged. **Upgrade note:** a deployment that reached Postgres or Redis directly from the host (local debugging, an external client) needs the new `docker-compose.admin.yml` overlay to republish those ports. `docs/DEPLOYMENT_SECURITY.md` records what this pass covers and what it does not: `governance-mcp` and the lease plane still run as root (no `USER` directive), the dev-only default secrets are still defaults, and there is no production-mode boot guard (#2580).

## [3.0.0] - 2026-09-27

A major release from master, following v2.22.1. It is major because the
changes listed under Compatibility break existing installs and callers. It
carries source changes merged since v2.22.0 on master, including the v2.22.1
maintenance history through its forward-merge. Deploy is continuous from master, so this tag is a
citation and rollback anchor rather than the first appearance of these
changes on a running server.

**Compatibility.** Several changes break existing callers or installs, and
[`COMPATIBILITY.md`](COMPATIBILITY.md) carries the full upgrade and rollback
policy:

- **Python 3.14.** The server package now requires Python 3.14 (the container
  already ran it); a bare-metal install on 3.12 or 3.13 no longer installs it.
  The SDK keeps `>=3.12`.
- **Compose upgrade command.** `scripts/unitares update` now upgrades the
  install instead of posting a check-in (check-ins move to
  `unitares call process_agent_update`). From a v2.22.x checkout, follow the
  one-time step in the install manual ("Releases before this command
  existed") first; the v2.22.x script's `update` migrates nothing.
- **Tool discovery.** Profiles are gone. Any `GOVERNANCE_TOOL_MODE` value,
  `full` included, is accepted and ignored, and the initial `tools/list` is
  progressive: the workflow tools plus `list_tools`, `describe_tool` and
  `use_tool`, which reach the rest. Every registered tool stays callable, and
  `UNITARES_TOOL_ADVERTISEMENT=full` advertises every schema up front. The
  v2.22.x advice to set `lite` in `.env` no longer has any effect.
- **Removed.** The `direct_resume_if_safe` tool (use `self_recovery`), the
  unused `dialectic` `vote` parameter, the server's coupling to the retired Pi
  plugin, new v2 party-HMAC signatures on dialectic resolutions (existing ones
  stay readable), and the served `discord-bridge` skill.
- **Contract.** The interface contract moves from 1.1.0 to 1.22.0 and
  input-schema digests move; a client that pins hashes must re-pin. The server
  reports `serverInfo.name` as `unitares`, not `governance-monitor-v1`.
- **Deployment settings.** Deployment-specific HTTP routes are mounted only
  when `UNITARES_ROUTE_PACKS` names them, and dashboard passkeys need
  `UNITARES_DASHBOARD_RP_ID`; see their entries' upgrade notes. The production
  `mcp` pin moves to 2.2.0.

**Migrations.** Two schema migrations, which must be applied before the new
code starts: `070_outcome_prediction_bindings` (a ledger table for
exactly-once prediction-bound outcomes, a cleanup function, and a replaced
outcome-partition drop function) and
`071_knowledge_closure_class_survives_tiering` (widens a CHECK constraint so a
classified finding can be archived). Until 070 lands, `record_result` with a
`prediction_id` fails with `DB_ERROR`; until 071 lands, a classified finding
cannot be archived. A Docker Compose install applies them with
`scripts/unitares update`, which backs up the database first; an operator
install runs `scripts/dev/apply_migrations.py --apply`. Rolling back to
v2.22.1 with 071 applied is safe only while no finding carries a
`closure_class`: `COMPATIBILITY.md` gives the check and the statement that
clears the classes.

**Artifacts.** The server image, and from this release a separate
`unitares-lease-plane` image pinned by `docker-compose.yml`, are published for
`linux/amd64` and `linux/arm64` with SBOMs and build provenance.
`unitares-sdk` 0.4.0 ships alongside (tag `sdk-v0.4.0`). Its behavior changes:
`checkin` reads the verdict from the response envelope, so
`GovernanceAgent`'s pause and reject handling (`VerdictError`) can now fire
where every verdict used to parse as proceed; `get_metrics().action` is the
policy action and `MetricsResult` gains `verdict`, `action`, `coherence` and
`risk`; `audit_knowledge` no longer requests a model by default; and the
async `GovernanceClient` raises `GovernanceToolRefused` (a subclass of
`GovernanceConnectionError`) when a tool answers `success: false`. It also
adds an optional NeMo Relay integration. The skills bundle changed, and the
governance plugin carries it as v0.4.18.

<!-- plugin-bundle-recut: v0.4.18 -->

### Added

- **inference:** Antigravity (`agy`, Google subscription) is the third strong-model host adapter, `antigravity:host-adapter`, callable through `delegate_inference` and as a `consult(effort="thorough")` peer. It runs in plan mode and its sandbox, with a fixed first prompt line so caller text can never be expanded as a slash command, from an empty temporary workspace, an allowlisted environment and a fresh temporary HOME holding only a link to the login keychain, and a turn that stalls on a denied command or the output limit is resumed in the same conversation; usage is summed across resumed turns. `consult` now chooses among three peers: a caller whose model family can be detected is never sent to its own family (the reported model or provider decides first, then the harness, client and user agent; an undetected caller may get any peer), and the first available peer wins. `UNITARES_HOST_ADAPTER_DISABLED_HOSTS` switches single host-adapter lanes off while the rest stay on; it does not change the dialectic reviewer, whose backend `UNITARES_DIALECTIC_REVIEWER_HOST` chooses. All three `agy` launchers (this lane, the dialectic reviewer's Antigravity backend and the PR review gate) now use that isolated HOME: the operator's `~/.gemini` holds standing permission grants and MCP servers, which headless mode does not deny, so a prompt (a caller's brief, a paused agent's thesis, a PR diff) could otherwise have read home-directory secrets or called governance tools. Google API credentials (`GEMINI_API_KEY`, `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT`, `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS`) are no longer passed to `agy` in any lane, including the dialectic reviewer's Antigravity backend and the PR review gate, so these stay subscription lanes by construction; an `agy` that authenticates with an API key instead of its keyring login no longer works there. (#2470)
- **consult:** every consultation that reaches the router now writes one `audit.events` row, `event_type='consultation'` (`unitares.consultation_record.v1`), recording the requester, policy, outcome, the route when a well-formed result came back, and HMAC-SHA256 hashes of the brief, prompt and advice. The brief and advice are never stored as text. The hash key is per-consultation, returned only to the caller in the new `record.hash_key` response field, and never stored, so readers of the row cannot test a guessed brief while the caller can prove which row is theirs. Any bound agent can read the row through `observe(action='audit_events')`. It is written to Postgres only, with `confidence=0.0`, so it stays out of the JSONL readers and confidence readers that take an agent's recent rows without an event-type filter. What it does not claim: it does not cover `call_model` or `delegate_inference`; it does not replace or mark the `auto_attest` decision that Energy accounting already writes for each successful call; an upstream failure records no route (after #2483 the audit row keeps the attempted host's route under `failure.upstream.route`); and an identifier-shaped piece of the brief that a backend echoes into a model name or execution id is stored as reported (#2477).
- **inference:** a strong-model host whose provider account is at its usage limit, or whose CLI is logged out, now takes itself out of rotation and comes back on its own. A delegated call that fails that way (the provider ran and said so; the server's own orchestrator rejecting a spawn does not count) starts a cooldown for that host: until the provider's stated reset time when it gives one (Codex: "try again at Sep 29th, 2026 9:10 PM"; Claude's epoch and "resets 3pm" forms), capped at 12 hours so a limit lifted early is noticed, otherwise an exponential backoff from 30 minutes to 6 hours. An error that is not recognisably a quota or auth failure never starts one. While a host cools down, `host_adapter_available` reports it unavailable, `list_inference_hosts` shows the reason and retry time under `cooldown`, and `delegate_inference` to it is refused before any spawn with the retry time. A thorough `consult` whose chosen peer fails that way, with nothing left running, tries the next eligible peer from a different family in the same call while a full attempt still fits in the tool timeout; the response lists each hop under `failover`, and the route and audit record name the host that actually answered. Cooldowns are per process: a restart forgets them and costs at most one failed attempt, which the failover routes around (#2486).
- **ci(skills):** the plugin skill mirror no longer falls behind silently. `.github/workflows/plugin-skill-sync.yml` runs on every push to master that touches `skills/` and calls `scripts/dev/open-plugin-skill-sync-pr.sh`. When the mirror is in sync it does nothing. When it is behind, it commits the mirror to one automation branch (`auto/plugin-skill-sync`) in the plugin repo and opens a PR there, or updates the one already open. It never merges. It steps aside when a hand-made sync PR is open, and its push is a compare-and-swap, so a concurrent run is never overwritten. It needs a `PLUGIN_SYNC_TOKEN` secret (fine-grained, plugin repo, Contents and Pull requests read/write); without one it warns and does nothing. An operator's local deploy wrapper can call the same script. Before this, the plugin's CI checked the mirror only against its own manifest, so a stale mirror passed, and it fell behind twice on 2026-09-25/26 (#2468).
- **identity:** `agent(action="release_presence")` releases the caller's own presence lease at a clean exit, so a successor that declares it as parent is no longer refused as co-located for up to ten minutes. It needs the releasing session's `client_session_id`; a crash still falls back to the lease TTL. The interface contract advances to 1.14.0. The same change fixes presence renewal: the producer read the lease id at `result.lease_id` while the SDK nests it at `result.lease.lease_id`, so every presence heartbeat was a fresh acquire. A release also retires the identity's live process bindings when the exiting session is its last live holder, and never frees a lease another session took over (#2387).
