- **review gate:** a diff that touches a security-sensitive path now needs a passing full review from two different model families before the `review` check goes green. The paths live in `scripts/dev/review_policy.json`: the OAuth provider, identity and auth modules and schema, the strict-identity gates, the MCP authorization gate and its wiring, lease and effect authorization, the strong-model host adapter and Antigravity client, the dialectic reviewer's backends, and the review gate, its policy and its workflow; `_boundary` in the file names what is deliberately left off. Families are OpenAI (Codex, native or local), Anthropic (Claude) and Google; an Antigravity review counts by the model it ran (recorded in its marker), since agy can run Gemini, Claude or GPT-OSS models; a recorded reviewer counts only when its name carries its family as a word, and a fix-verification receipt never counts. CI enforces the rule, reading the policy as merged on the pull request's base ref, never from its head, and requires the second family if the changed paths cannot be read. `review.sh` does not start the second review itself: on a sensitive diff with one family it exits 3 (distinct from exit 2, reviewers unavailable) and prints the exact `--fresh --reviewer` commands for eligible providers, so the round cap and the scheduled sweep are unchanged. The motivating cases: on PR #2486 the first reviewer returned a bare CLEAN and a second family then found two P2 defects, and the Antigravity isolation hole (standing permission grants loaded from `~/.gemini`) was caught by one family's review (#2504).
- **sentinel:** an audit-volume check (`UnitaresSentinel.AuditVolumeWatch`) files a `sentinel_finding` of type `audit_volume_anomaly` when one writer (agent_id, else session_id) produces far more `audit.events` rows of one type than anything legitimate does. There are two rules. At 1,000 or more rows in the trailing hour the finding is `high`. At 300 or more rows that are also 10 or more times the writer's own mean over the prior 23 hours, it is `medium`. Rows carrying `suppressed_since_last` count for 1 + that number, so a server-side write throttle cannot hide a storm. A `throttle_flush` row contributes only its suppressed count, placed at `suppressed_last_at` rather than at the row's own timestamp, so old misses flushed late neither page as current traffic nor vanish. `suppressed_last_at` now carries the writer's UTC offset, so it reads as the same instant in any reader's time zone. An alert that fails to reach governance is queued in memory, up to 200, and resent on the next tick, even if the condition behind it has since cleared. Each tick runs its query before any delivery and attempts at most 5 POSTs, new findings first, so a stalled endpoint cannot starve the check. The same applies to forced-release and conflict alarms, whose cursor moves past the rows it read. Their queue is attempted by severity, with this tick's alarms ahead of older ones. Retries rotate through the queue rather than repeating its head. Once a queue is full, lower-severity items are evicted first, then the longest-queued (`Findings.cap_queue/2`), so a fresh failure is never the one dropped. The thresholds were calibrated on 30 days of data. The busiest legitimate writer peaked at 484 rows in an hour. Four runaway clients each wrote about 5,850 an hour, including one that ran from 2026-09-16 to 09-24 without being flagged; replayed, this check pages `high` on each within 14 minutes of onset. The check is a single read-only query every 5 minutes. `elixir/sentinel/scripts/start.sh` turns it on; config leaves it off by default. An opt-in launchd crash-loop check (`UnitaresSentinel.LaunchdWatch`) flags a job whose last exit was non-zero and whose run count rose by 3 or more between checks. It runs only when `UNITARES_SENTINEL_LAUNCHD_LABEL_PREFIXES` names label prefixes and `launchctl` exists, and it needs no privileges. Both checks re-alert through a shared backoff (`UnitaresSentinel.ReAlert`): once, then 1h, 2h, 4h up to a 24h cap while the condition holds. An escalation re-alerts immediately. Once a condition has been quiet for a full interval, the next occurrence starts a new episode (#2405).
- **tool discovery:** add progressive advertisement without shrinking the callable contract. MCP, REST, and stdio initially list 13 workflow/discovery tools by default; `list_tools(lite=true)` still negotiates all public capability names, `describe_tool` supplies the selected schema, and the new `use_tool(tool_name, arguments)` gateway invokes an omitted target through its normal identity, validation, authorization, timeout, response, and telemetry paths without adding a shorter gateway timeout. `UNITARES_TOOL_ADVERTISEMENT=full` restores every schema up front; legacy `GOVERNANCE_TOOL_MODE` settings remain ignored. The transport-neutral interface contract advances to 1.13.0 and fingerprints the complete catalog independently of advertisement size (#2328).
- **sdk:** `unitares_sdk.integrations.nemo_relay`, an optional extra (`pip install "unitares-sdk[nemo-relay]"`) that attaches UNITARES to NVIDIA's NeMo Relay runtime as a plugin. Each root Relay scope mints a fresh identity; tool and LLM activity is filed as `substrate_interpretation` check-ins with no confidence; tool errors and foreign guardrail rejections become `task_failed` / `tool_rejected` outcome events under the server's default in-process provenance; and a conditional-execution guardrail refuses tool calls for a run whose latest policy action is `pause` or `reject`, naming the verdict, guidance and review path in the refusal. The gate never touches the network and the subscriber only enqueues to a worker thread. Rejections by the integration's own gate are never fed back as outcomes. Fail-open by default (`fail_closed` refuses instead). Sixteen tests drive the exporter with Relay 0.8.4's exact event shapes; the end-to-end test against the real runtime skips without the extra. Companion decision packet: `docs/proposals/active/relay-substrate-relayering-v0.md`. (#2255)
- **tool-surface diagnostics:** `scripts/diagnostics/tool_surface_cost.py` measures what a profile's `tools/list` costs on the wire, per profile, per tool and per parameter (`--boilerplate`, `--check-ladder`), and `scripts/diagnostics/hint_target_advertisement.py` lists tools named in caller-facing hints that a profile does not advertise. `docs/dev/TOOL_REGISTRATION.md` records the first measurements: cost tracks parameter breadth rather than tool count, and generated property titles were a flat share of every profile. Token figures are a bytes/4 estimate; no schema, profile or tool changed (#2113).
- **glama image:** `Dockerfile.glama` and `docker/glama/` build a standalone image for the Glama registry that runs PostgreSQL 18 with AGE and pgvector, Redis, the lease plane and UNITARES under a supervisor on private loopback addresses, with persistent `/data` and generated identity keys. Bootstrap reuses the canonical schema order and fails closed on incomplete initialization or a schema-changing upgrade, and MCP stdout stays protocol-only; before, Glama's plain `uv sync` build listed tools but failed real calls. The Compose installation is unchanged. Platform behaviour (persistence entitlement, memory limits, startup deadline, redeploy volumes) is not verified; `docs/deployment/glama.md` lists the prerequisites (#2127).
- **dialectic attestation descriptor:** every response that serves a resolution carries a derived `attestation` sibling from `describe_attestation()` classifying it as `bilateral`, `single_signer`, `unsigned` or `legacy_v1`, so a reader no longer infers "unsigned" from two empty signature strings under a version-2 label. It is never stored, so `resolution_hash` and the `drr.v1` receipt bytes do not move, and it is not on `dialectic(action='list')`. `scripts/ops/dialectic_canary.py` records `surface_ok` and a four-valued `outcome` and exits non-zero only when the review surface fails, not when a reviewer declines or has not yet resolved a synthetic probe; `ok` keeps its old meaning in the JSONL (#2140).
- **dialectic unresolved reviews:** `scripts/ops/dialectic_unresolved.py` lists unresolved reviews with their standing reviewer conditions, excluding probe sessions by the existing probe-family rule, and prints nothing when there are none; `--session-id` renders one review as a paste-ready PR comment. It is report-only, with no `--apply`. `classify_outcome` gains `standing_rejection`, supplied by `get_outcome_breakdown`, and the `dialectic_outcomes` header no longer claims `awaiting_facilitation` is exact or set by the self-clear guard (#2153).
- **dialectic:** a session read now carries `wait_assessment` (`elapsed_s`, `expected_by_s`, `assessment`, `note`), which says how long the session has waited on a reviewer and whether that is yet unusual; `whose_move` only said who owed the move. It is classified only when an identified orchestrated reviewer owes a verdict or a reconsideration and the transcript clock can be read: the budget is the reviewer's own model-call ceiling (`UNITARES_DIALECTIC_CODEX_TIMEOUT_S`, 420 s by default) plus fixed overhead, and `assessment` is `too_early` or `overdue`. A human or unmanaged reviewer, an unreadable clock, a terminal session or the caller's own turn is reported unclassified (`assessment: null`). `expected_by_s` is not a liveness reading, and `overdue` is a reason to look, not proof the reviewer is gone. Responses now suggest reassignment or facilitation only when the wait is overdue. The `dialectic-reasoning` skill documents the block (#2191).
- **EISV telemetry:** telemetry now says how each reported value was built, without changing any computed value (checked bit-for-bit against the previous code on 10,000 random input sets). The envelope's `derivation.components` (`behavioral_sensor.components.v1`) records the weighted terms and ordered post-blend adjustments the behavioral sensor applied, with sources, defaults and compatibility inputs named. `derivation.calibration_signal` separates the deployed fleet calibration from a new agent-scoped candidate that needs five samples in a bin and activity within seven days; legacy fleet bins carry no timestamps, so their freshness reads `unknown`. Both blocks are `mode: measurement_only`, `policy_applied: false`: the candidate is shadow telemetry and the deployed fleet calibration remains the only live input. `GET /v1/agents/{agent_id}/history` accepts `include_telemetry=latest` (newest envelope only) and returns `trajectory_context` (elapsed wall-clock time, cadence gaps, source and maturity transitions, descriptive least-squares slopes per hour); the telemetry metrics read gains `measurement_context` (subject, window, freshness, coverage, unavailable inputs); the dashboard qualifies policy wording by source instead of presenting it as safety or health. No policy path reads any of it, and the registered outcome experiment was not re-run (#2194).
- **ci/release:** a `Promote Release` workflow (`promote-release.yml`, run by hand with a tag) replaces the step-8 prose checklist. A read-only verify job checks that the tag is the newest server tag and ahead of `PUBLISHED_VERSION`, that its release page is published, that the image index carries `linux/amd64` and `linux/arm64` each with an SPDX SBOM, and that `gh attestation verify` passes against `publish-container.yml` at that tag. After `release-promotion` environment approval, it moves `latest` to the verified digest with `imagetools create` (never a rebuild), then pushes a `publish/<tag>` branch that advances `PUBLISHED_VERSION` for a normal PR. It refuses to run when the environment has no real User or Team required reviewer, and it re-verifies the peeled tag SHA, tag digest, source attestation, current `latest` digest, release state and branch state immediately before each mutation, so a rerun cannot act on stale evidence. A weekly `server-release-sync.yml` report compares `VERSION`, server tags and their ancestry on master, published releases, `PUBLISHED_VERSION` and the GHCR `latest` digest, and keeps one `ci-finding` issue open while they disagree; it never gates or publishes (#2210, #2228).
- **doctor:** `scripts/dev/unitares_doctor.py` gains an advisory `host_binary_currency` check that reports outdated Homebrew installs of `cloudflared`, `postgresql@17`, `redis` and `ollama`. It skips hosts without Homebrew, never updates anything, and never fails: an outdated binary, a `brew` error or an unexpected or malformed response is a WARN, not a healthy result (#2243).
- **constraint pressure (research primitive, not wired):** `src/constraint_pressure.py` adds a pure, exact-time leaky constraint-pressure transition with explicit missing-input and resolution semantics, and `docs/proposals/pcalm-primal-dual-governance-v0.md` sets out a replay-first design around it. Nothing calls it: it reads no outcomes, emits no audit event and changes no response, EISV value or policy. It is registered KEEP-DORMANT in the dormant-capability registry, with the wake condition that a separately approved, preregistered offline replay over an operator-selected constraint clears its bar first (#2250, #2262).
- **operations:** `scripts/ops/provision_resident_anchor.py` writes a resume anchor for a rostered identity that was minted through `start_session` rather than the SDK and so never got one. Without an anchor such an identity survives only in a process-local session binding, and once strict identity is on, the first process boundary refuses a binding-only resume (`lineage_declaration_required`) while both remedies it names mint a successor. The tool reads the identity back (it must be active, `persistent` and `autonomous`), mints a continuity token with the server's own signing secret, proves the token resumes the same UUID through `identity(agent_uuid, continuity_token, resume=true)`, and only then writes the anchor at mode 0600. It is a dry run unless `--apply` is given; `--no-verify` writes an anchor reported as unproven. `docs/operations/resident-roster.md` explains when to use it (#2253).
- **ci findings:** `scripts/dev/ingest_ci_findings.py`, an operator-side, stdlib-only producer that reads `ci-finding` issues through the `gh` CLI and stores each finding the knowledge graph does not already hold, with fingerprints collected from the issue body and every comment (the guards report recurrences as comments). It is read-only on GitHub, defaults to a dry run (`--apply` writes), and runs where the credential and server already are rather than in CI, so the guard workflows stay `GITHUB_TOKEN`-only. It fails loudly: a handler refusal wrapped in a success body, the success-shaped identity refusal, or a store with no `discovery_id` is an error, not a stored finding. Nothing schedules it; wiring it is an operator decision described in `docs/operations/ci-issue-surfacing.md` (#2283).
- **review gate:** pull-request review is a checked status like tests. `scripts/dev/review.sh` has the other model family review the PR diff (it refuses a branch's own model) and posts a review record, `ship.sh` starts it on every push (`SHIP_NO_REVIEW=1` skips it), and `.github/workflows/review-gate.yml` sets a `review` commit status from the latest record for the PR's current diff, keyed on a sha256 of `git diff --raw` against the merge base: clean, or findings each answered by a numbered disposition, is green; unanswered findings or no record is pending; a crashed or timed-out reviewer fails; `review.sh record` accepts any review (a council, a person, another model), and CI calls no model. `review_gate.py sweep` reviews at most one ready, owner- or dependabot-authored PR per run that has been quiet for 15 minutes, using its own trusted copy of the script against a detached worktree and a per-diff lock shared with `ship.sh`; its schedule lives outside this repository. A green status records that a review happened and was answered, not that it was sound (#2318, #2319).
- **hosted deployment and cloud sessions:** Compose and `.env.example` pass through the settings a governance server behind a public tunnel needs: `UNITARES_MCP_BEARER_TOKENS` (the MCP and strict-REST bearer allowlist), `UNITARES_MCP_ALLOWED_HOSTS`, `UNITARES_MCP_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, `UNITARES_HTTP_CORS_EXTRA_ORIGINS`, `UNITARES_REST_STRICT`, and `UNITARES_DASHBOARD_RP_ID` / `UNITARES_DASHBOARD_ORIGIN`, which replace a hardcoded passkey relying party (the default is unchanged, and the enrollment page shows the configured one). The lease plane prefers `UNITARES_MCP_BEARER_TOKEN` over `UNITARES_HTTP_API_TOKEN` for its governance calls, the container installs `webauthn` for dashboard passkeys, the Compose healthcheck probes the unauthenticated `/health/ready`, and `/health` reports `rest_strict`, `mcp_bearer_required` and per-transport auth headers. `cloud-deploy-runbook.md` documents each setting and the first-passkey bootstrap. `docs/operations/cloud-session-plugin.md` explains why a hosted Claude Code session gets governance tools but no hooks (install state does not travel, and container egress is allowlisted), and `scripts/dev/cloud-session-setup.sh` installs the plugin and verifies the configured MCP endpoint without ever failing the session; it is not a browser and does not prove CORS configuration (#2323).
- **knowledge authority:** search ranks by retrieval authority as well as relevance. Rows tagged as imported memory are `imported_context` (ranking multiplier 0.55), native findings `native_finding` (1.0) and promoted claims `governed_claim` (1.15), so an imported memory no longer wins a close relevance contest against native knowledge but can still rank first on a substantially stronger match, and `authority_mode="all"` restores raw backend order. The router-only `knowledge(action="promote")` creates a new, immutable, server-receipted claim from an imported-memory row plus at least one non-memory evidence id, an explicit `verification_basis` and a `decision_standard`, leaving the source untouched; it requires caller ownership, is classified high-stakes, and records a promotion receipt, not verified truth. Default mixed-tier ordering changes by design, and `knowledge`'s input-schema digest and the surface digest move (#2330).
- **hermes agent:** `integrations/hermes/` is a portable Agent Plugins v1 package for Hermes Agent: a `plugin.json`, an `mcp.json` pointing at the local Streamable HTTP endpoint `http://127.0.0.1:8767/mcp/`, and one bundled `unitares-governance` skill. It connects over the transport instead of embedding UNITARES in Hermes' Python environment (Hermes declares `<3.14`, the server `>=3.14`), does not install, update or start UNITARES, and carries no credentials (#2340).
- **hermes plugin:** the Hermes plugin package (0.2.0) declares its harness in static, non-secret MCP headers (`X-Unitares-Harness-Type: hermes`, `X-Unitares-Harness-Source: caller_declared`, `X-Unitares-Adapter-Type`, `X-Unitares-Adapter-Version`), so a Hermes agent running on a Claude or OpenAI model is no longer filed under Claude Code or Codex CLI when its provider id or User-Agent collides with those harness aliases. Knowledge-graph writes now honour a transport-declared harness too, after the same `_safe_identifier` sanitizer the check-in envelope uses (header, then body claim, then User-Agent hint); before, KG writes took the harness from the User-Agent hint only. The bundled skill tells the agent to report `model_provider` and `model` in `provenance_context` and never to put a provider name in `harness_type`. The headers are caller-declared attribution, never identity proof or verdict authority (#2356).
- **behavioral floor (default off):** two flags add a baselined floor-breach caution floor. `UNITARES_FLOOR_BREACH_CAUTION_SHADOW` records what the floor would do; `UNITARES_FLOOR_BREACH_CAUTION_APPLY` turns a `safe` behavioral verdict into `caution` when the agent is baselined and an absolute floor is breached (escalate-only, one step, never before warm-up), with the record in the existing `absolute_floor_observation` audit row as `floor_breach_caution` only when a flag is on. Before this, a breached absolute floor bounded only its own risk component, below the safe threshold, so it could never move the verdict off `safe`. With neither flag set nothing in the verdict path changes; applied, `caution` maps to `guide` after warm-up, and enabling it is left to an operator reading the shadow rows (#2362).
- **model adjudicator:** for a deployment with no human judge, `POST /v1/sentinel/model-adjudicate` and `scripts/ops/model_adjudicator.py` (with a 6-hourly launchd template) let a model confirm, dismiss or abstain on adjudication-queue findings. Model verdicts are telemetry only: they are written as `finding_model_adjudicated` audit events, never as outcome events, so they never reach the operator label channel, `is_bad` or the registered outcome read. The route needs its own secret (`UNITARES_MODEL_ADJUDICATOR_TOKEN` in `X-Unitares-Adjudicator`, 503 when unset), an operator verdict wins (409), a model confirm or dismiss hides the item for `UNITARES_MODEL_ADJUDICATION_COOLDOWN_H` (default 168h) while an abstain does not, and the judge runs as a Claude CLI with no tools, MCP, hooks or plugins because finding text is untrusted. `/api/findings` now replaces a fingerprint over 256 characters with `sha256:<hex>` (keeping `fingerprint_original`) instead of storing one the verdict route would later refuse, and `?postable_only=1` keeps legacy over-long rows out of the adjudicator's window (#2378, #2390).
- **dialectic backlog:** `scripts/ops/dialectic_unresolved.py`, which feeds the SessionStart "N dialectic review(s) unresolved" line, gains an operator acknowledgement ledger. `ack ID [ID ...] --disposition {superseded,stale,test} --reason TEXT` appends one row per session to a local append-only JSONL ledger (`DIALECTIC_ACK_LEDGER` overrides its location); acknowledged sessions are then hidden, with one line counting how many were hidden by disposition, and `--all` or `--json` show them. A batch is all-or-nothing: an ambiguous, unknown, too-short or not-in-backlog id refuses the whole batch. A missing or corrupt ledger hides nothing. The database is never written, since an acknowledgement is neither a protocol status nor a verdict, and both queries now open read-only connections (#2428).
- **oauth:** one confidential OAuth client can be pre-registered from the environment (`UNITARES_OAUTH_STATIC_CLIENT_ID`, `UNITARES_OAUTH_STATIC_CLIENT_SECRET`, `UNITARES_OAUTH_STATIC_REDIRECT_URIS`; setting only some of them fails OAuth setup closed), for connectors that ask for a client id and secret and cannot self-register. `UNITARES_OAUTH_PUBLIC_PORT` starts a second, loopback-only listener over the same app, and the `/mcp` OAuth gate applies only there, so a tunnel can point at it while local callers on the main listener are not gated; REST routes reached through the public listener never get the trusted-network bypass, and a busy, duplicate or unusable public port falls back to gating every request. `UNITARES_OAUTH_REQUIRED=1` refuses to serve with an issuer and a public port but no bearer allowlist. Issued tokens and dynamically registered clients now persist in Redis (keys are SHA-256 digests, no raw token is stored, and Redis failures fall back to memory), refresh grants that returned 500 now work and are single-use, `UNITARES_OAUTH_DYNAMIC_REGISTRATION=false` admits only pre-registered clients, and the doctor's `mcp_route_gate` probes the public listener when one is configured. For the pre-registered client only, `/authorize` without PKCE gets a challenge derived from a server-held verifier (redeeming still requires the client secret) and requested scopes are narrowed to `mcp:tools` instead of refused; every `/authorize` and `/token` request writes one rate-limited `[OAUTH]` log line that never records a secret, code, verifier or token (#2429, #2458).
- **review and dialectic reviewer:** Antigravity (`agy`, on a Google subscription login) is available as a second model family for the PR review gate (`review.sh`) and as a dialectic reviewer host (`UNITARES_DIALECTIC_REVIEWER_HOST=antigravity`, alias `agy`). Reviewer choice prefers a family other than the author's (codex, then antigravity, then claude), and antigravity is only considered when `agy` is installed. It runs in plan mode and its sandbox from an empty temporary workspace that is refused if any parent holds `.git` or `.agents`, with an allowlisted environment carrying no `UNITARES_*` or GitHub tokens; its review material comes from committed git objects only. On a dialectic timeout the whole process group is killed, and if the host fails the local model takes over and can object but never approve. The dialectic backend now tells `agy` the prompt is self-contained and to answer in text, and names an empty reply after a denied tool use as such (before this, every orchestrated Antigravity review returned an empty reply and fell back to the local model). The review gate resumes the same `agy` conversation after a denied-command stall (up to two resumes as merged; #2476 raises that to three and catches a denied call to any tool) or an output-limit stall (one), within the remaining budget, and an unrecovered stall is a failure, never an accepted review. `docs/UNIFIED_ARCHITECTURE.md` now presents long-lived agents as an optional capability rather than a fleet that ships with UNITARES: `UNITARES_RESIDENTS` is empty by default and `agents/` is outside the shipped package (#2436, #2446, #2459, #2476).
- **cli:** `scripts/unitares` is the one command for a Docker Compose install. `update [--check] [--to REF] [--yes]` fetches the newest published release (or `REF`), stops the server and lease plane, writes a private `pg_dump` backup when migrations are pending, applies that release's migrations with the canonical runner inside the database container (before this, nothing applied a new release's migrations to an existing Docker volume), then rebuilds and restarts and reports deep health. It asks first, refuses on a launchd install, with tracked local edits or untracked files under `db/postgres`, and when the database schema is newer than the target; a failed migration or rebuild checks the previous code back out and leaves the forward-only schema migrated. `--check` is read-only. `model` lists the models a local Ollama has pulled, writes `UNITARES_OLLAMA_BASE` and `UNITARES_LLM_MODEL` to `.env` keeping every other line, rebuilds, and checks from inside the container that the server reaches the model (`--model`/`--yes`, `--no-rebuild`, `--no-docker` and `--clear` are supported); with no Ollama or no pulled model it writes nothing. `start`, `stop` and `logs` wrap Compose for the checkout. Breaking: `unitares update "text"` no longer posts a check-in (use `unitares call process_agent_update`), and releases that shipped the old command need one manual step to move, which the install manual gives (#2496, #2501).
- **knowledge search:** `knowledge(action="search")` and `search_shared_memory` take `sort_by`, `created_after` and `created_before`. `sort_by="created_at"` returns the query's full-text matches newest first: the database orders the match set by `created_at`, so an entry written a minute ago comes first however weakly it ranks, where re-sorting a relevance page would only reorder rows that already ranked in. Status, archived/cold exclusion, type, severity and author filters sit inside that ordered query too, so newer ineligible matches cannot fill the page. It uses no semantic leg, reranker or authority reorder, and it refuses `search_mode="semantic"` or `"hybrid"`, because similarity has no match boundary to order within. `created_after` / `created_before` are exclusive ISO 8601 bounds (UTC when no offset is given), applied inside the query on every retrieval path (full-text, filter reads, and the pgvector similarity search, which joins the discovery row as the tag filter already did); an unparseable or inverted window is refused, not ignored. With no query, `created_after` is the "what is new since T" read, newest first. Responses echo `sort_by` and the window when they differ from the default, the lean digest included. The default order is unchanged. `exclude_agent_labels` is now checked while candidates are collected rather than after the page is cut, so an excluded writer no longer leaves a page short in either order. `search_shared_memory` pays for the three fields with shorter advertised briefs on nine existing search parameters (full descriptions unchanged through `describe_tool`), so the progressive tool listing is smaller than before. Interface contract 1.22.0. All three fields were previously stripped by the unified `knowledge` schema; the legacy `search_knowledge_graph` schema declared `sort_by` (default `created_at`) and both bounds, and no handler read any of them. That default is now unset, which reads as `relevance` (a materialized default would defeat the queryless time order after validation), and its never-implemented `score` and `related_count` values are refused. What it does not claim: newest-first recall is the full-text match set, so an entry that shares no query terms (after stemming) is not returned, and the AND-then-OR fallback applies as it does for relevance search (#2517).

### Changed

- **docker quickstart:** a release checkout no longer has to compile the coordination lease plane. The `Publish Container` workflow now builds a second image from the same release tag, `ghcr.io/cirwel/unitares-lease-plane:vX.Y.Z`, from `elixir/lease_plane/Dockerfile`, with the server image's hardening: pinned action SHAs, `linux/amd64` and `linux/arm64`, SPDX SBOMs, max-mode provenance and a build-provenance attestation. The release commit is baked in as `UNITARES_BUILD_SHA`, so the image's `/health` reports a build sha instead of `unknown`. `Promote Release` verifies that image's digest, platforms, SBOMs and tag-bound provenance next to the server's, re-reads it before promoting and before advancing the install pins, and records it in the release evidence. It never tags the lease plane `latest`. The `lease-plane` service in `docker-compose.yml` names the image with `pull_policy: missing` and keeps its `build:` block. `make version-bump` moves that tag with `VERSION`, so each release tag's tree names the image built from that tag, and promotion refuses a tag whose Compose file names anything else. Until a release publishes the image, the pinned tag does not exist, so Compose prints a pull warning and builds from source as before. A `master` checkout should use `docker compose up --build`, or it runs the last release's lease plane; CONTRIBUTING and the install manual now say so, and the Docker quickstart CI job still builds from source. A new `Lease Plane Image` workflow builds the image for both platforms without pushing whenever the lease plane, `elixir/unitares_sdk` or `publish-container.yml` changes, so the QEMU-emulated arm64 build is exercised on pull requests and a break cannot first appear at release, where it would stop promotion. This changes install time, not lease-plane behavior (#2522).
- **tool discovery (compatibility):** discovery profiles are gone. `GOVERNANCE_TOOL_MODE` values (`minimal`, `standard`, `lite`, `full`) no longer select a surface on MCP, REST or stdio; they are accepted and ignored, so old configuration cannot silently pick a different catalog, and the v2.22.x advice to set `lite` in `.env` no longer has any effect. Every registered tool, raw entry point and workflow alias stays callable, and plugin tools loaded from entry points are now mounted before MCP accepts clients (previously introspection and REST could see plugin tools MCP could not). What a client is shown first is now governed by progressive advertisement (#2328, under Added): a short workflow and discovery listing, with `list_tools`, `describe_tool` and `use_tool` reaching the rest, and `UNITARES_TOOL_ADVERTISEMENT=full` for clients that need every schema up front. The profile entries below (#2102, #2106) describe intermediate states that did not survive to this release (#2137).
- **review gate:** the Antigravity (`agy`) reviewer reads the diff and the changed files from its temporary workspace (`diff.patch`, and `files/<repository path>` for each changed file's committed text) instead of one inlined command-line argument, so the old ~120 KB cap is gone: PR #2470's 143 KB diff could not be reviewed by Antigravity at all, and every round fell back to Claude. `agy` reads these with its own file tool, which needs no permission grant inside its workspace; a shell command it reaches for is still denied, and the resume prompt now says file reads work. Content still comes only from committed git objects, never the working tree, symlinks or paths outside the repository. Changed files named `AGENTS.md`, `GEMINI.md`, `CLAUDE.md` or under `.agents/`/`.gemini/` are written with a `.review-copy` suffix so a PR cannot configure its own reviewer. The default model is Gemini 3.1 Pro (High) at full effort, overridable with `REVIEW_AGY_MODEL` (`default` leaves the choice to `agy`); it was tried on two PRs: on #2458 it was the only model that found real (pre-existing) defects, and on #2470 it returned clean, so it is a starting point rather than a measured result. The shared review budget rises from 1,800 to 2,400 seconds, since Pro needed 983 s for #2470 and the first of two attempts used to get only 900 (#2476).
- **changelog:** entries now arrive as one new file per pull request under `docs/changelog.d/` (`<section>-<slug>.md`, body = the entry) instead of an edit to `## [Unreleased]`. Every PR used to write into the same subsection, so each merge conflicted every other open PR with an entry, and resolving the conflict re-keyed its review. The release cut folds the fragments in with `scripts/dev/changelog_assemble.py` before the version header is written, so `changelog_coverage.py` still checks the assembled entry; it now also fails a release tree that still holds a fragment. The Release Seams workflow validates fragments on every PR and fails an ordinary PR that edits `## [Unreleased]` directly (release trees and dependabot exempt; released entries stay editable for errata and maintenance forward-merges). Existing `[Unreleased]` entries are unchanged (#2412).
- **governance:** a risk-only verdict from the Phi cold-start prior no longer hard-pauses an agent's own report either. The non-authored cold-start guard now covers `agent_report` rows under the same provenance, maturity and no-override conditions (`GOVERNANCE_COLD_START_GUARD_INCLUDE_AUTHORED`, default on; rollback by setting it false), with its own enforcement basis. Measured 2026-08-30 to 2026-09-24: 0 of ~19,300 rows at behavioral confidence ≥ 0.3 produced a pause verdict. Structural and runtime-safety stops and independent verification still pause. This does not establish that cold-start pauses were false positives. The doctor's cold-start canary now counts authored cold-start pauses like non-authored ones, including structural stops the guard never evaluates; only an authored pause whose row shows the guard evaluated it without the extension (before this change, or with the flag off) is reported uncounted (#2419).
- **inference:** the Codex, Claude and Antigravity host adapters are labelled as what they are on a default install: an operator extension that needs the agent orchestrator, which no Compose service starts. `list_inference_hosts` returns `extensions.agent_orchestrator` (`configured`, `host_ids`, `serves`, `note`), and each adapter record carries `extension: "agent_orchestrator"`. An adapter's `configured` now needs both `UNITARES_HOST_ADAPTER_ENABLED` and `AGENT_ORCHESTRATOR_BEARER_TOKEN`; the flag alone used to read as configured. On a server without the extension, `delegate_inference` and `consult(effort='thorough')` fail with the same `INFERENCE_HOST_UNAVAILABLE` code, but the message and recovery now say the extension is not configured and point at `consult(effort='standard')` or `call_model` instead of listing flags. `allow_degraded=true` still falls back to a standard local answer. The `consult`, `delegate_inference`, `list_inference_hosts`, `call_model` and `dialectic` descriptions say the same; `delegate_inference` moves to the `advanced` tier. No tool is hidden, no schema changes, and the interface contract digest does not move. Servers that run the orchestrator see no change in dispatch. The progressive `tools/list` shrinks by 80 B (#2507).
- **knowledge search:** the staleness warning's version clause is retired (operator decision, 2026-09-26). It is the "2+ minor versions behind current" rule under *Staleness warnings* in the 2026-03 Knowledge Graph entry. It compared two VERSION-file values, so it counted release cuts, not code change: it fired on entries 22-57 days old depending on when releases were cut, and never on a `leave_note`, which records no version. `staleness_warning` is now the last-write age clause alone (more than 60 days, open entries, threshold unchanged), and its wording follows the entry: a durable entry (the lifecycle's permanent types and tags, plus `insight`) reads "Last written N days ago; durable entries can carry volatile details (ports, paths, refs) — verify before acting on them" instead of "still open". Because that classification is the retention one, an open bug tagged `architecture` also gets the neutral wording. `system_version` stays on results as store-time provenance, and the dashboard's stale flag still reads `staleness_warning`, so it now counts fewer entries. Search rows gain `last_activity_days` beside the warning: days since the last write in UTC, the name `knowledge(action="audit")` uses for its own last-activity count (the audit computes it in server-local time, so the two can differ by a day near a day boundary). The `include_details` disclosure is protected from the attribution yield only; over-budget truncation still trims retrieval options as before. The default `search_shared_memory` digest carries `last_activity_days` instead of the warning sentence and explains it once in `state_summary.staleness_note`. Where the AGE supersession edge exists, it names a superseded row's replacement as `superseded_by` (first successor id), and the single digest kept by budget compaction keeps `status`, `superseded_by` and `last_activity_days`. It states `confidence_note` once, at the top level; the note was also copied into `state_summary`. `knowledge(action="details")` on a superseded entry now carries `superseded_by` and `superseded_warning` where the AGE edge exists. Inside the 3,000-byte digest budget, optional coaching gives way before any attribution is withheld: the tier ladder (`response_options`) first, then the extra retrieval options, then a truncated digest's `expand_with`, which repeats `raw_governance_hint`. Each piece yields only if attribution still needs the room, and comes back if it fits once attribution is restored. The `include_details` override disclosure (`requested_tier`, `details_omitted_by`) never yields to attribution. Withheld attribution now always carries `digest_attribution_omitted` when a digest is shown, and the truncation markers are measured inside the budget instead of added after it (#2474).
- **tool surface:** the progressive `tools/list` every session loads before its first call shrinks from 44,199 to 40,455 bytes (about 940 tokens by the bytes÷4 estimate). `search_shared_memory` stops advertising six fields that only other `knowledge` actions read (`offset`, `epoch_scope`, `scope`, `evidence_ids`, `verification_basis`, `decision_standard`); search never read them and `knowledge` keeps them. The EISV field contract rides once on the advertised surface, on `check_working_state`; `sync_state` and `record_result` point at `describe_tool(tool_name='check_working_state')`, and `describe_tool`'s full view of either still appends the contract (its explicit `lite=true` view now shows the pointer instead). The identity briefs are shorter: `agent_id` reads "UUID; leave unset for yourself." Corrections: `store_finding`'s `agent_id` tells callers to leave it unset (it said "Filter by agent"); six model docstrings that reached the wire as `inputSchema.description` no longer invite an argless `identity()`, promise "supportive feedback", or name a canonical tool on an alias; `list_tools`' `verbose` says it is ignored and `lite` says what it returns. A two-sided ratchet test holds the progressive surface between 39,000 and 41,000 bytes under the default schema policy. Interface contract 1.17.0 (#2444).
- **review gate:** a verdict with no reasoning is no longer recorded as a review. Antigravity twice replied with nothing but `VERDICT: CLEAN` on PR #2486: once after reading about 97,000 tokens of the diff and changed files, and once on a five-line fix. After the first, an independent Claude review of the same diff found two P2 and two P3 defects, so a bare CLEAN was hiding real problems, not summarising a clean change. A verdict now needs at least 120 non-whitespace characters (about 25 words) of reasoning before the `VERDICT` line; that floor is a judgement call, set low on purpose, because it exists to catch an empty verdict, not to grade a short one (200 was tried first and rejected a terse but genuine review). When Antigravity replies with a bare verdict, the conversation is resumed once and asked what it examined; a local reviewer that stays bare is recorded as a failed attempt, so the next reviewer runs instead; and `review.sh record` refuses a bare verdict outright. Every reviewer prompt now says so. Native Codex review is unaffected: its records are built directly from GitHub's review, not parsed from reviewer text (#2500).
- **responses:** routine responses drop what restates the normal case and mark it with `response_shape: "routine"`; anything unusual keeps its full shape. A plain fresh `start_session` (outcome `minted_force_new`/`minted_fresh`, no resume, reactivation, lineage or trajectory) no longer repeats the onboard record under `raw_governance`; it lifts `identity_resolution_outcome`, a compact `identity_assurance` (tier, session_source, caller_proven, baseline), and `continuity_token` grouped under `rebind` with its use caveat. A clean `sync_state` proceed (positive evidence of healthy status, a comfortable margin and a safe verdict) drops the repeated approve/safe/healthy values; the margin and any unassessed edges stay, and it keeps `action_summary.action`, `reason` and `risk_score` plus every field the SDK reads. On responses that carry `agent_signature` (canonical tools and non-envelope tools such as `list_tools`), a caller-proven strong signature with a routine continuity claim keeps its role declarations, assurance and `continuity_claim` but drops the nested blocks restating its own flat fields (`detail: "compact"`); weak, unproven, UUID-direct, discontinuous or rejected-provenance signatures keep the full record. `tests/test_response_budgets.py` pins byte budgets on the normal case. #2473 later widens which fresh `start_session` counts as routine (see its entry) (#2437).
- **grounding:** `REGISTERED_READ_MANIFEST` now also records, for the stop-rule read (`eisv-outcome-grounding-2026-12-01` and its `-retry-<n>` forms), the seven-name `EISV_PRIOR_STATE_MODELS` tuple as written and `DISPERSION_FEATURE`, as they stood on `master` when the stop rule's PASS condition 4 ("same family") was clarified on 2026-09-23. A `--read-protocol registered` read under that protocol refuses, inside `validate_read_protocol` and therefore before any receipt is written, any read id consumed or any database touched, if the live `eisv_skeptic_report` constants differ from the recorded values; the same read id is reusable once the checkout is corrected or the manifest amended, and the `-retry-<n>` rule is not triggered. The guard compares source constants, so it detects a drifted checkout; it records the tuple as written and the feature name only, compared by exact equality (so a reordering also refuses, conservatively: the tuple is a membership filter in `score_deltas_vs_baseline`, and a tie on the selection key is broken by `build_model_scores`' construction order, which the manifest does not pin; the stop rule records it and a CI canary fails if `master` changes it), not what any candidate computes (constructors, binning, `min_feature_rows`, the dispersion window and snapshot minimum stay free), and it is not a runtime guarantee. Exploratory, reproduction (including the pre-declared sensitivity cohort) and operator-cohort reads are unaffected, and no registered read, estimator, threshold or cohort moves. Alongside it, the registered stop-rule document gains a dated pre-read clarification defining condition 4 as winner-name identity of the `Best EISV/prior model` cell between the two lead slices (`-` in either cell = unmet), and the independent-operator preregistration's "candidate family" wording is aligned with it while its enrollment ledger is empty. The same change records a separate, dated declaration (chosen under the operator's delegation, ratified on merge) that no smallest relevant effect is set for the 2026-12-01 read: on any non-PASS branch the scientific inference is `INCONCLUSIVE`, `REFUTED` is unreachable for that read, and the power probe is reported descriptively; PASS and FAIL are decided unchanged. (#2154, #2373)
- **grounding:** the registered EISV outcome stop rule gains a dated pre-read clarification of its reopening clause, drafted and decided 2026-09-25 under the operator's recorded delegation, before any 2026-12-01 data exists, and ratified on merge: if the read FAILs, an estimator fitted or learned on the same trusted task/test label channel (fitted blend weights, a learned readout, any fitted function of measurements the deployed producers already record) does not by itself constitute the "materially different label channel or measurement process" the clause requires, while a fitted readout whose inputs come from a genuinely new measurement process, or which is scored against a genuinely different label channel, remains a candidate new premise. The label channel is fixed by the labels' kind and provenance (the task outcome types from the verification sources the trusted anchor scope admits), not by flag values: re-scoring or re-filtering those rows is not by itself a new channel, while labels from a newly admitted verification source can be. A refit of the deployed producers' parameters against these labels is a fitted estimator under the block whatever it is called; a correction that changes what is measured is judged on that change, under the operator-judgement clause when condition 3 fails and on its merits otherwise. The operator-judgement clause itself is unchanged. No PASS condition, threshold, cohort, cutoff, date, command, pinned candidate or `REGISTERED_READ_MANIFEST` entry changes, no read is run, and the outcome question stays unresolved. The EISV contract, `docs/ROADMAP.md` and the maths roadmap now point to the registered block as the single authority. (#2440)
- **grounding:** a pre-data, narrowing-only correction to the 2026-09-25 reopening-clause clarification in the registered EISV outcome stop rule, made the same day after an independent re-read of the merged block and before any 2026-12-01 data exists. The block's definition had let labels from any "verification source the registered read did not admit" count as a candidate new label channel, which a wider, self-attested anchor scope (`--anchor-scope soft`, which admits the gameable `agent_reported_tool_result`) or the withdrawn `all` cohort would also satisfy. A genuinely different label channel of the same outcome types is now only a new exogenous, trusted-tier verification source: one absent from the anchor registry at the read, with no outcome row before its cutoff, that the amended registry maps to `TRUSTED_EXTERNAL`. Re-scoring under a wider scope, re-tiering an existing or excluded source, or dropping the joinable-snapshot requirement widens the label definition and is not a new channel, and the trusted predicate is now described with that snapshot requirement. The block now states that its fixture-rule example settles only the label-channel half, that the operator-judgement clause is unchanged in what it reserves for a real change to what is measured, and that a pure refit is not such a change. It also records that the operator gave a general direction and an agent selected and drafted the reading under that delegation, after an adversarial design review, ratified on merge. The superseded sentences are quoted in a dated correction note. The EISV contract and `docs/ROADMAP.md` pointers are corrected to match. No PASS condition, threshold, cohort, cutoff, date, command, pinned candidate or `REGISTERED_READ_MANIFEST` entry changes, no read is run, and the outcome question stays unresolved (#2455).
- **agent response envelopes:** the write aliases `store_finding`, `update_finding` and `record_result` no longer repeat their canonical payload under `raw_governance` by default, extending the omission the read aliases and routine `sync_state` already had. Each acknowledgement keeps what a caller needs next: `success`, `next_action`, `agent_uuid` (taken from the response's `agent_signature`) with a compact `written_as` giving the writer's `agent_id`, `display_name` and assurance tier, `caller_proven` and `proof_origin` (on `record_result` only when its binding was not server-inferred, because `outcome_event` signs nothing for a server-inferred one); `discovery_id`, write-time warnings (failed supersession, truncation, anonymous writer, unclassified closure) and `consolidation_hint` for the finding writes, plus on `store_finding` a bounded `related_discoveries` store-time similarity snapshot (at most five rows, each an id and a summary preview of at most 120 characters), since a later details read returns the stored record but not the warnings or those previews (the snapshot's ids are the stored record's `related_to`, so a `store_finding` ack that carries the snapshot does not repeat them as `state_summary.related_to`; `update_finding`, and a `store_finding` with no snapshot, keep the record's `related_to` ids); and `outcome_id`, `is_bad`, `idempotent_replay`, `unverified_fields` and any handler `identity_warnings` for `record_result`. `raw_governance_hint` names what is still reachable and never suggests a repeat that would write again: for `record_result`, which has no read by outcome id, an outcome recorded with a `prediction_id` names the identical call repeated with `response_mode="full"`, which replays the stored outcome in full (`idempotent_replay: true`) and records no second outcome while the binding is retained, provided the same agent repeats it with its `client_session_id` (#2487), and an outcome without one names `response_mode="full"` on a later `record_result` and warns that a repeat records a second outcome (the older `include_semantics` spelling still works, including over `/mcp/`); and a `knowledge(action="details", discovery_id=...)` read for the finding writes, which returns the stored record rather than the ack's payload (the canonical `knowledge` tool also returns their payload directly; `response_mode` is not a full request on these two aliases, because canonical validation defaults `knowledge`'s `response_mode` to `"full"` and the envelope cannot tell a caller's request from that default, and their `/mcp/` schemas do not declare it). Since no read returns the omitted payload (the outcome route is a repeat of the write, safe only under the conditions the hint states), these acks do not set `raw_governance_available`, as a routine `start_session` does not. A write whose record id the envelope cannot lift keeps the canonical payload. `start_session` and `request_review` are unchanged. Identity refusals and error payloads still pass through whole. Measured on recorded acknowledgements re-rendered locally from their canonical payloads (2026-09-25, p50, n=5/4/5): `store_finding` 5,412 B to 1,222 B, `update_finding` 5,678 B to 1,663 B, `record_result` 5,505 B to 1,579 B. None of the recorded `store_finding` payloads carried a `related_discoveries` snapshot; one adds at most about 1 KB to the ack (five rows at the preview bound). No input schema changes, so the interface contract version and digests are unchanged (#2439, #2462).
- **ci(skills):** `skills/SKILLS_MANIFEST.sha256` is no longer committed. It is derived data, and its one `# aggregate:` line made any two pull requests that edited any two skills conflict (#2361 went conflicting on the manifest alone when #2363 merged); GitHub runs no custom merge drivers, so `.gitattributes` could not help. `scripts/dev/sync-plugin-skills.sh` now writes the fingerprint into the plugin mirror, where the plugin's CI parity gate reads it, and `--check` reports a missing or mismatched one as drift. `scripts/dev/skills_manifest.py` prints the manifest, its aggregate, writes it to a path, or verifies a file against a tree; its `--check` mode and the CI step that ran it are removed, and the path is gitignored. The skill freshness checker now treats a cited source as fresh when its current digest appears in any attestation that certified the current `SKILL.md` (new stamps record a `skill_digest`), falling back to the newest attestation only when none certified this text; the legacy frontmatter block still counts: a branch cut before a source changed could stamp later with the old digest and, once merged, mask a correct older attestation, which read `unitares-governance` STALE after #2363 merged. Attestations written before `skill_digest` existed vouch only as the newest, as before, so the fix applies to records stamped from now on. AGING takes its date from the same records that vouch (the current-text attestations, else the newest), so a newer stamp of different skill text cannot reset it; the server's `skills` tool serves `last_verified`, `version` and `stale` from the same records, through one shared rule in `src/skill_attestations.py`. A stamp made where a cited source is absent carries that source's digest only from an attestation for the current skill text, and otherwise leaves it unrecorded (#2402).
- **vigil:** an unchanged stale-opens set is now re-reported at most once a day, not every 30-minute cycle (measured 2026-09-24: the same 2 entries in 167 of 167 cycles). Groundskeeper's archive candidates now reach the operator as one low-severity `vigil_finding` a week (`finding_type=kg_archive_candidates`), not a KG note; 91 Vigil KG notes in 30 days drew 0 detail reads. The digest names `python3 agents/vigil/agent.py --archive-candidates`, which lists the candidates under Vigil's own identity and archives only on `--apply` (entries with activity 0, or exactly `--ids`), through the same archive call and high-severity `closed` fallback the auto-archiver uses. Auto-archive criteria are unchanged, and nothing is archived without that command (#2404).
- **chronicler:** the per-run KG digest is now opt-in (`CHRONICLER_KG_DIGEST=1`). Measured 2026-09-24: 20 digests in 30 days, 0 detail reads, and each one joined the stale backlog Vigil sweeps. The check-in now carries the movement instead (`Chronicler: 14/14 scrapers ok, 11 moved (a, b, …)`, first five names), so a healthy run still says what it did on the channel `/v1/residents` surfaces. Scrapers, `metrics.series` and the daily check-in are unchanged (#2404).
- **sentinel:** `held-by-other` conflict alarms now back off per surface on the shared re-alert schedule instead of paging every 30-second cycle; 111 of 280 in the 30 days to 2026-09-24 were one surface. An emitted repeat carries `suppressed_cycles_since_last`. Alarm fingerprints are unchanged. `entropy_outlier` and `correlated_events` are no longer POSTed as findings, by operator decision after the 2026-09-24 resident review. They were 466 `medium` findings in 30 days. `medium` is outside the adjudication queue, so no path ever surfaced them for a verdict, and that zero says nothing about their value. The analysis is kept: it still runs, is logged, and shapes the cycle check-in text and complexity, so Sentinel's own state estimate is unaffected. The set is configurable (`:log_only_finding_types`), and an empty list restores posting (#2405).
- **ci(skills):** re-verifying a skill now writes a new attestation file under `skills/.attestations/<skill>/` instead of rewriting `last_verified` and a `source_digests` block inside `SKILL.md`. The skills manifest excluded attestations, and since #2402 it is no longer committed at all. Before this, any two open pull requests that stamped a skill conflicted on the same lines, and every merge put the others back into conflict. The freshness checker, the plugin's copy of it, and the server's `skills` tool read the newest attestation; `--migrate` and `--prune` manage the files. Cited `unitares/...` sources now resolve against the checkout itself, so the source check no longer passes vacuously in a worktree with a different directory name. The attestation history is pruned with `scripts/client/check-skill-freshness.sh --prune 3` (with `UNITARES_PROJECTS_ROOT` set when run from a worktree, #2524) at every release cut (`RELEASE_PROCESS.md` step 2); a prune keeps every record that vouches for the current text or carries a cited source forward within the AGING window, so each skill's served `last_verified` is unchanged by it (#2394, #2516).
- **doctor findings:** an open doctor condition no longer re-posts every 6 hours. The first repeat now waits 24h and each later repeat doubles the wait, capped at 7 days, so a stuck condition still resurfaces about weekly. Measured 2026-09-24: 356 `doctor_check_finding` rows in 30 days from 8 fingerprints (a 44.5x repeat), consecutive posts exactly 6.01h apart, `signal_degeneracy` open for 46 days. New conditions and warn-to-fail escalations still post on the first tick, because the status is part of the fingerprint. Suppression is keyed on `fingerprint`, as before. The resolve path and the adjudication gate are unchanged. `DOCTOR_FINDINGS_COOLDOWN` is now the base interval; `DOCTOR_FINDINGS_MAX_COOLDOWN` sets the cap (#2397).
- **tool discovery:** `list_tools(lite=true)` is now the compact handshake its name promised instead of a second rich catalog. It keeps the complete advertised `tools[*].name` index required by federation negotiation, but each entry is name-only and the response no longer repeats per-tool hints, tier/operation/stability/category metadata, category membership, workflow recipes, signatures, or onboarding blocks. On the current 50-tool surface the direct JSON response falls from 22,981 B to 2,998 B (-19,983 B, 87.0%) while `list_tools(lite=false)`, category/tier filtering, progressive ordering, `describe_tool` behavior, the MCP `tools/list` roster and input schemas, dispatch, and the negotiated interface contract remain unchanged. The top-level `list_tools` and `describe_tool` descriptions now teach the split accurately; the older `lite`/`verbose` parameter-help wording remains byte-stable because changing it participates in the versioned input-schema digest and is deferred to the next negotiated contract release. A first-party regression caps the default lite response at 4 KiB and requires every lite tool record to contain exactly `name` (#2327).
- **tool schemas:** an operator can drop `default: null` annotations from the tool listing with `UNITARES_TOOL_SCHEMA_NULL_DEFAULTS=strip`. The default is `keep`, so the advertised listing is unchanged unless an operator opts in. JSON Schema treats `default` as an annotation rather than a validation rule: requiredness stays in `required`, explicit-null acceptance stays in each nullable type, and every concrete default stays advertised, so stripping leaves every capability, parameter, type, required field, runtime default, validation result and dispatch path intact. With `strip`, 404 repeated annotations go and the compact listing (brief descriptions, generated titles stripped) falls from 145,384 B to 139,324 B (-6,060 B, 4.2%). The registrar keeps the annotations and `src/tool_mode_listing.py` applies the policy to copied Tool objects on each listing, so registration and validation schemas never change; a catalog invariant requires every removed annotation to be optional and explicitly nullable. Under the default this moves no input-schema digest and no interface contract version (contract 1.12.0 is the `dialectic` `judgment_formed` change, #2322). `tool_surface_cost.py --boilerplate` reports null-default annotations separately (#2326, #2327).
- **agent response envelopes:** routine `sync_state` calls no longer repeat their formatted canonical payload under `raw_governance`; bounded modes keep an explicit full-response escape hatch, while actionable auto responses expand to mirror mode for weak identity, enforcement/recovery signals, critical margins, or warnings. Lean shared-memory search now bounds historical summary previews and tags, exposes one relevance score with its basis, marks preview and result-set truncation separately, and enforces a 3 KB whole-envelope budget before returning lower-ranked digests (#2332).
- **packaging:** `sentence-transformers` moves out of the `full` extra and out of `requirements-full.txt` into a new opt-in `embeddings` extra. A fresh `pip install -e ".[full]"` or `-r requirements-full.txt` therefore no longer carries semantic search; `.[full,embeddings]` restores it, and the commented re-enable line in `requirements-full.txt` mirrors the one the container has carried since it made this same call. The dependency is not optional by preference: it requires `torch`, whose Linux wheel metadata pulls `cuda-toolkit`, `cudnn`, `nccl`, `nvshmem` and `triton` under a bare `platform_system == "Linux"` marker, with no GPU check and no opt-out. Measured on a clean 3.12 venv, `.[full]` resolved to **5.9 GB** with it and **162 MB** without — 5.4 GB of that difference being NVIDIA wheels, for a server whose advertised install path is Docker Compose and whose shipped image already excludes the package ("saves ~1.3 GB", `requirements-docker.txt`). The cost fell on the contributor path (`requirements-full.txt`, which every CI shard installs) and the bare-metal path only. What this retires is a *default-install lever*, not a capability: semantic search stays wired, reachable and tested, `src/embeddings.py` and `src/reranker.py` already degrade cleanly, retrieval falls back to Postgres FTS and says so via `search_degraded`, and both availability warnings now name the extra rather than a bare `pip install sentence-transformers`. Any future usage zero measured against an install that lacks it reads "never reachable", not "unwanted". Two guards close the drift class that produced the divergence: `pyproject`'s `full` extra and `requirements-full.txt` are now asserted to agree as sets (directionally — the requirements file is a superset), and every package in an opt-in extra is asserted absent from both default install files. Also fixes the stale `<6.0.0` bound on the container's commented line, which a Dependabot widening missed, and moves the package out of Dependabot's `runtime-deps` group, where a bump would now be mislabelled (#2303).
- **mcp server:** `list_tools` now advertises `lite`, `include_advanced`, and `tier`, the three parameters `handle_list_tools` has read since the tool existed. FastMCP builds `/mcp/`'s transport argument model from the registered schema and drops any key it does not name before dispatch, so `list_tools(lite=false)` still returned the compact response over that transport — the same defect class as the `observe`/`describe_tool` gap fixed 2026-09-07 and the `cirs_protocol` gap fixed 2026-09-13 (#2183), just missed for `list_tools` in that sweep. REST and stdio callers were unaffected, since undeclared keys merge back on those paths; only `/mcp/` silently ignored them. No advertised parameter is removed or renamed; only `list_tools`' `input_schema_sha256` moves, and the negotiated interface release advances to 1.10.0. `tests/test_wire_declares_handler_params.py` gains a `list_tools` case. `tier` is normalized for case and whitespace (null or blank means all), and an unknown `tier` is now refused with `INVALID_TIER` (allowed: `all`, `essential`, `common`, `advanced`) instead of returning an empty catalog; `lite` and `include_advanced` coerce null and unrecognized strings to their default `true`, as the handler does (#2240).
- **tool schemas:** `complexity` and `confidence` on `process_agent_update`, `simulate_update` and `sync_state` now advertise what the server accepts. The 0-1 bound had been emitted as Pydantic `ge`/`le`, which are not JSON Schema keywords, so every client validator ignored it and 99 was advertised as legal; it is now `minimum`/`maximum`. The string branch had allowed any string, so `"x"` looked legal too; it is now a regex of numeric strings in [0, 1]. `sync_state`'s `complexity` also lists the named levels its normalizer maps to numbers, nested inside its one string branch. The string branch was kept rather than dropped because the MCP transport builds its argument model from the advertised schema: without one, `sync_state(complexity="medium")` is refused before the normalizer runs, and a bad value on the canonical tools gets a generic parse error instead of the message naming the accepted forms. Only the advertised schema changes. The fields keep their runtime declaration and `WithJsonSchema` overrides what is emitted, so every value is accepted or refused as before, with the same validation errors down to each error's location, on every transport. Moving the bound onto the Union members instead emits the same schema, but reports `complexity=2` as two errors at new locations; a second top-level string branch for the named levels repeats the transport's string error. Three schema digests move; the negotiated interface release advances to 1.9.0 (1.8.0 is taken by a concurrent change). New guards: an invariant test that every boundary value an advertised schema calls legal passes both the transport and the handler path dispatch runs, alias normalizers included; a ban on Pydantic-only constraint keys in any advertised schema; a `format`/`pattern` policy under which a construct the test cannot model fails loudly instead of being skipped; and comparisons of whole validation error lists, on the handler and on the transport, against the fields' declaration. (#2181)
- **README and brand assets:** the README header now carries a wordless mark, the CIRWEL flat-top hexagon holding a didone U cut from Bodoni Moda, with the wordmark beside it behind a light/dark `<picture>` element; the tagline stays markdown text so copy can change without a new image. The three hero banners and the phosphor flow diagram are removed, badges drop the phosphor palette, and the three call-to-action badges become a text nav line. `docs/assets/social-preview.png` is regenerated in the same register with no counts on it (the previously served card promised prevention the claim table marks Untested), and `scripts/dev/gen_social_preview.py` is replaced by the generators under `scripts/dev/brand/`. `docs/public-site/favicon.svg` follows the new mark. (#2174)

- **mcp server:** `/mcp/` now advertises the catalog schema byte for byte. FastMCP derives each tool's `tools/list` schema from the typed wrapper's signature, and that derivation is lossy: at `be117c2` the mount showed 106 non-null catalog defaults as `null` (`sync_state.complexity` 0.5, `sync_state.task_type` "mixed", `check_working_state.lite` true, `start_session.resume` true, ...), dropped 21 constraint keywords (`consult.brief` length 1–32000, `delegate_inference.timeout_s` 5–420, `dashboard.limit` 1–100, `record_progress_pulse.value` ≥ 0, the `items` of the `parameters` / `ethical_drift` arrays, three `additionalProperties`) and lost the `$defs` of the four nested-model tools, so a direct `/mcp/` client saw a different parameter contract than a stdio or REST client (finding F12 of the 2026-09-12 tool-surface audit, #2167). Both registrars now replace the registered tool's `parameters` with the catalog schema after registration; on both supported mcp majors that dict is read only by `list_tools()`, so what the transport accepts is unchanged -- the wrapper's argument model still gates dispatch, extra-argument passthrough still applies to it, and the handler's Pydantic model still enforces the bounds it now advertises. The registrar keeps generated titles at registration and the listing strips them per call, so `UNITARES_TOOL_SCHEMA_PROPERTY_TITLES=keep` stays reversible; the `search_shared_memory` property overrides are applied once, in the resolved description mode, instead of twice. `tests/test_mcp_schema_parity.py` diffs the mounted listing against the catalog per tool and per property across every description and title mode. `tool_surface_cost.py --surface mcp` and `--surface catalog` report the same bytes, the eight alias wire-schema hashes in `docs/dev/TOOL_EDGE_INDEX.md` moved to the catalog's, and `docs/interface-contract.v1.json` is unchanged. (#2178)
- **tool schemas:** parameter descriptions are advertised abridged. A description is paid for on every `tools/list`, in every session, by every client, before the agent has decided it wants the tool: measured on the FastMCP wire, `sync_state` alone cost 8,076 characters (~2,000 tokens) and the five-tool `minimal` profile ~5,470, roughly half of it parameter prose. A profile cut does not touch that -- it removes names from the list, not words from the names that remain. `src/schema_brief.py` now serves an authored short form where a field carries one (`Field(json_schema_extra={"brief": ...})`, which always wins and is not held to the budget, because some parameters are their enum list), the description whole where it already fits the 140-character budget, and its first sentence where it does not; abbreviations and single-letter initials are not sentence ends, and a first sentence still over budget is cut on a word boundary and marked. Nothing is removed from the system: `describe_tool(tool_name=..., action=...)` reads the Pydantic models and still serves every word, and the MCP `instructions` string says the catalog is abridged once at initialize rather than paying for a pointer on every parameter. Twenty-three short forms are authored by hand where the first sentence alone would have left a parameter unusable -- the identity trio on every tool, `epistemic_class`, `provenance_context`, `logprobs`, `knowledge.discovery_type`, `config.thresholds` -- and no advertised description on the surface is now cut mid-sentence. Measured after: `sync_state` 8,076 to 6,283 chars (-22%), `start_session` -33%, the `minimal` profile ~5,470 to ~4,290 tokens (-22%), `standard` (eleven names since #2106) ~10,950 to ~9,380 (-14%), `lite` -12%. `tool_schemas.advertised_input_schema` is the one place the mode is applied, so the wire catalog, `describe_tool` and the tool-surface audit cannot drift; workflow-alias property overrides in `src/alias_schema.py` observe the same mode and carry a `brief` of their own. `UNITARES_TOOL_SCHEMA_FIELD_DESCRIPTIONS=full` restores the previous surface byte for byte, `=off` is the old `UNITARES_TOOL_SCHEMA_STRIP_FIELD_DESCRIPTIONS=1`, and `UNITARES_TOOL_SCHEMA_BRIEF_BUDGET` moves the cap. Descriptions live inside the hashed input schema, so every `input_schema_sha256` moves without a single parameter name, type, default or requiredness changing; the negotiated interface release advances to 1.4.0 and the contract artifact is regenerated. `tests/test_schema_brief.py` holds the trim to being a trim. The structural half of the schema is untouched and reported rather than taken: Pydantic/FastMCP emit a `title` per property and an `anyOf: [{type}, {null}]` per optional, 663 and 396 characters respectively on `sync_state` after this change. #2115 later strips the generated titles (see its entry). (#2107)
- **tool modes:** `self_recovery` joins the default `standard` profile, which now advertises eleven names. A mode filters `tools/list` and never dispatch, but the server names this tool to the agent it just paused -- `mcp_handlers/updates/phases.py` on the pause path, `support/agent_auth.py` on an auth refusal, and `dialectic/responses.py` in `related_tools` -- and a schema-driven client is offered only what discovery returned. Under the ten-name default that instruction named a tool the model had never been shown, in the one state where it can least improvise. `self_recovery` is a router, which the standard-profile rule otherwise excludes, but the exclusion is for "a router over actions these already cover" and no other advertised name reaches recovery; the rationale is written into the mode set so the rule is not read as forbidding it. `tests/test_lite_wire_surface.py` already required that a tool named in another tool's recovery hints be advertised and only happened to scope it to `lite`; a standard-mode sibling now enforces the same invariant on the profile adopters actually run. Superseded within this release: #2137 removed discovery profiles, and #2328 made the initial listing progressive (see the tool discovery entries). (#2106).
- **describe_tool / routers:** a consolidated router now answers for one action. `describe_tool(tool_name="knowledge", action="search")` returns the parameters that action uses (20 of the 51 the router advertises) with `action` pinned to the requested value; without an action the response lists the actions the tool routes. The wire schema stays flat, because the MCP wrapper builds a tool's argument model from top-level properties and a per-action `oneOf` would not survive registration -- so the narrowed schema describes one call and the router still accepts and ignores the rest. Which parameter belongs to which action is declared as `ACTION_FIELDS` on each router's parameter model, beside the fields; most fields already said so in prose, which nothing could check against the routing table. `tests/test_router_action_fields.py` now does: the declared actions must be exactly the routed actions, every named parameter must exist on the wire, and every parameter must be classified or centrally common. `describe_tool` declares `action` on its own schema, so the negotiated interface release advances to 1.3.0 and the contract artifact is regenerated. (#2103)
- **tool modes:** the default advertised surface is a new ten-tool `standard` profile: the five-name checkpoint loop plus `search_shared_memory`, `store_finding`, `update_finding`, `request_review`, and `consult`. A profile filters `tools/list` and never dispatch, but a schema-driven client (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor) offers the model only the names discovery returned, so under the five-tool default of v2.22.0 shared memory, structured review, and advisory inference were registered, reachable, and unreachable from such a client at once. The line is drawn at capability rather than at count: each added name is the only advertised route to something the server does, and the consolidated routers, the discovery tools, and the operator surface stay behind `lite` / `full`. `minimal` is unchanged and keeps the v2.22.0 surface for a context-scarce client. The profiles nest, `minimal` through `lite`, and a test holds them to it. Superseded within this release: #2137 removed discovery profiles, and #2328 made the initial listing progressive (see the tool discovery entries). (#2102)
- **mcp server:** the server now returns an `instructions` string in the initialize response, built from the static mode sets by `tool_modes.build_server_instructions()`. It names the workflow, states which profile is running and how many tools it advertises, and names the capabilities that profile withholds together with the flag that lists them. Before this, the only in-band notice that an unadvertised surface existed was the onboard `tool_mode` block, which the lean envelope drops unless a caller passes `verbose=true`, so a default client had no way to learn what it had not been told about. The string is orientation, not contract: it is outside the interface-contract surface hash. The Compose discovery check now asserts it over the real HTTP mount and covers `standard` and `minimal` as well as `lite`. Superseded within this release: #2137 removed the profiles this string described, and the shipped instructions describe progressive advertisement (#2328). (#2102)
- **tool registry:** one record per advertised tool. `src/tool_meta.py` holds a `ToolMeta` for each of the 50 names on the advertised roster (42 registered tools + 8 workflow aliases) with its category, tier, operation, stability, and relationships; `TOOL_TIERS`, `TOOL_OPERATIONS`, `TOOL_CATEGORIES`, `_TOOL_STABILITY`, and `TOOL_RELATIONSHIPS` derive from it and keep their names, and building the table refuses a duplicate record or an unknown category / tier / operation. The wire list `TOOL_ORDER` derives from the registered records in the same order, so `get_tool_definitions()` returns the 42 registered tools instead of 65 names that included 23 `register=False` delegates every wire consumer filtered out again; the interface contract's surface hash is unchanged. `TOOL_OPERATIONS` is keyed by the roster only, and a legacy alias that pins an action narrower than its router's class declares `operation` on its `ToolAlias` entry (15 of them), which `describe_tool` reports. `TOOL_RELATIONSHIPS` drops 35 legacy-alias entries no surface read and the duplicate `dialectic` key whose second entry silently won. `describe_tool` and `list_tools` now report `stability`. `tests/test_mcp_startup.py`, which returned 1 instead of asserting and so passed with 23 schemas that had no handler, is a real test. `check_doc_health.py` reads tool names from the record table. (#2099)
- **tool aliases:** the 49 `consolidated` and 1 `deprecated` entries of the alias table now carry `deprecated_since`, the date the old name stopped being canonical, read from the commit that added each alias: 2026-01-13 for the 15 that predate the repository's full-state import (a floor, not a day), 2026-02-04 for the 25 from the action-router consolidation, 2026-04-01 for `reassign_reviewer`, 2026-06-29 for the 8 admin-router names, and 2026-01-29 for `request_dialectic_review`, matching `DEPRECATION_REGISTRY`. The 20 intuitive aliases (`start`, `status`, the eight workflow names) stay undated because those names were never canonical. `describe_tool`'s `alias` block reports the date and reports role `compatibility_alias` for a non-workflow alias instead of `primary_agent_workflow`; a bookkeeping test holds the table to the rule. (#2098)
- **describe_tool:** no longer advertises `agent_id` / `agent_name` for `process_agent_update`, `get_governance_metrics`, `sync_state`, and `check_working_state`, the session-injected parameters the registered schema already hid (the two standing `DESCRIBE_SCHEMA_WIDER_THAN_WIRE` warnings). The hiding has one definition, `tool_schemas.advertised_input_schema`, applied by the wire catalog, `describe_tool`, and the tool-surface audit, whose rule now covers every wire name rather than workflow aliases only. (#2098)
- **ci(skills):** the skill freshness gate now reads source drift from recorded content digests. Each `SKILL.md` frontmatter carries `source_digests`, a sha256 prefix of every cited source taken when the skill was last verified; a source whose content no longer matches, or has no record, marks the skill STALE, and a source absent from the checkout (another repository, the plugin mirror) is counted and reported rather than guessed.
