The previous signal, filesystem mtime, is rewritten by every checkout, so CI ran the calendar-age check only and five of seven skills sat stale behind a green gate. Commit dates were rejected because the repository squash-merges, which stamps a landed change with its merge time. `check-skill-freshness.sh --stamp NAME` re-records a skill after its claims were re-checked and rewrites only the date line and the digest block; `SKILL_FRESHNESS_AGE_ONLY=1` remains as an explicit age-only switch. (#2097)
- **tool registry:** the five hand-maintained bookkeeping maps now describe the advertised roster, exactly. The roster is every `register=True` dispatch tool plus the eight workflow aliases (`tool_modes.advertised_tool_names_full()`), and `GOVERNANCE_TOOL_MODE=full` now returns it instead of the schema-definition list, which also counted the 23 `register=False` delegates that only validate router actions and put `full` fifteen names wider than anything the registrar advertises (the two standing edge-index warnings on `full` clear). `TOOL_CATEGORIES` and `TOOL_TIERS` partition the roster, one bucket per advertised name: 23 registered tools had no category or tier before (every action router among them) and 26 pre-consolidation names were still listed; categories agree with the introspection catalog that `list_tools(category=...)` filters on, which gains a `workspace` category the catalog already had. `TOOL_OPERATIONS` covers every advertised name (22 were missing and defaulted to `read` in `list_tools` / `describe_tool` output, `knowledge` and `agent` included), and `dialectic` is classified `write` (request / thesis / antithesis / synthesis / reassign mutate). The stability map is keyed by every registered tool with no silent defaults, drops four names that no longer exist (`who_am_i`, `quick_resume`, `self_recovery_review`, `check_recovery_options`), and an alias reports its canonical tool's tier; `knowledge` and `self_recovery` carry the STABLE tier their flat predecessors all declared, every other router stays BETA. The onboard `tool_mode` block counts the roster. `validate_tool_modes.py` fails on an uncategorized advertised tool (it warned) and on a category entry off the roster; `tests/test_tool_registry_bookkeeping.py` holds every map to the rule. (#2093)
- **ci/release:** `changelog_coverage.py` no longer requires a citation or a hand-written exemption for a dependabot `build(deps)` / `build(deps-dev)` merge that its own merge proves inert: the diff touches nothing but dependency manifests, lockfiles, image digests and workflow action pins, and a `build(deps)` bump that moves a runtime manifest shows a same-major move in its subject. A major move of a runtime pin (the shape `mcp` 1.x to 2.x arrived under), a group subject over a runtime manifest, or a bump whose diff reaches into source is held to a citation and printed with the reason. The provenance line counts excluded and held bumps separately. `RELEASE_PROCESS.md` records the tagging order that avoids the freeze-window race: tag immediately after the release PR merges, arming no other auto-merge until the tag exists. (#2096)
- **tool schemas:** advertised input schemas no longer carry Pydantic's generated `title` keywords (the model class name at the root and a title-cased echo of each property key), which JSON Schema does not validate against; this is the structural half #2107 left in place. The strip is applied in `advertised_input_schema`, so the wire, `describe_tool` and the surface audit agree, and it reached the final `/mcp/` listing, after FastMCP regenerates schemas, only with #2120. Every `input_schema_sha256` moves while no parameter name, type, default or requiredness changes, and `UNITARES_TOOL_SCHEMA_PROPERTY_TITLES=keep` restores the titles (#2115, #2120).
- **agent-facing hints:** hints written as calls now name the workflow alias the caller holds (`start_session` / `sync_state` rather than `onboard` / `process_agent_update`) and are judged against the surface of the caller that can receive them, and `hint_target_advertisement.py` requires an adjacent parenthesis so English prose no longer parses as a call. The same change added `dialectic` to the then-default `standard` profile, because `request_review` pins `action="request"` while its own refusal pointed at `dialectic(action='get')`; profiles were later removed by #2137, which advertises the complete catalog. Bare tool names in `related_tools` lists were not covered here (#2119).
- **search_shared_memory schema:** the alias's advertised schema narrows from 36 to 22 parameters, dropping 14 controls that only other `knowledge` actions read; `discovery_type`, `severity` and `include_provenance` stay because the search parser reads them, and the action-field map now says so. `tool_surface_cost.py` measures the final MCP `ListToolsResult` by default (`--surface catalog` selects the source layer), and `hint_target_advertisement.py` also scans `next_call`, nested safe options and structured `related_tools`. The negotiated interface contract advances to 1.5.0 (#2120).
- **gap recovery:** the absence threshold that arms gap recovery is now a named constant, `GAP_RECOVERY_ARM_SECONDS`, instead of a side effect of the Euler stability cap (`scaled_dt > DT_MAX`). It is derived as `DT_MAX * DT_EXPECTED_INTERVAL / DT`, exactly 150 s, the same boundary as before, so no verdict, suppression or pause changes at the default; `dt_saturated` and `gap_recovery_armed` are logged separately, and the constant is inventoried as governance-sensitive. `docs/proposals/active/gap-recovery-arming-semantics-v0.md` records the arming semantics (#2122).
- **orchestrator deploy script:** `deploy-orchestrator.sh` defaults its deploy checkout to a directory named `unitares-orchestrator-deploy`, matching the other `<repo>-deploy` pins, and `deploy-status.sh` follows. `UNITARES_ORCHESTRATOR_DEPLOY` still overrides, and the plist preflight still refuses to deploy until the LaunchAgent's working directory matches (#2161).
- **stakes table and tool counts:** `stakes_table.export_table()` no longer emits thirteen alias names (`reset_monitor`, `submit_thesis`, `get_server_info` and others) that the resolver canonicalizes before lookup and so never consulted; each canonical entry carries the same level, so no gate decision changes. `count_tools.py` buckets tools by declaring module, so the eight consolidated routers report as `consolidated` instead of `decorators` and a plugin router is excluded by its module rather than a hardcoded allowlist; the total is unchanged (#2173).
- **sdk metadata:** the `unitares-sdk` package summary names what the client exposes (identity binding, the check-in loop, shared memory, self-recovery and the lease-plane client) instead of "UNITARES governance", EISV state and "identity anchors", which the SDK does not expose. The PyPI page changes at the next SDK release (#2180).
- **knowledge responses:** the `agent` block in `knowledge(action="store")`, `knowledge(action="note")` and full-mode `knowledge(action="get")` responses no longer copies the caller's `identity_context`. The same block was serialized twice, once under `agent` and once under `agent_signature`, about 16% of one measured `store_finding` response (970 of 6,112 bytes). `agent_signature` keeps the canonical copy; `agent` keeps `uuid`, `agent_id`, `structured_agent_id`, `display_name`, `label_source` and `identity_assurance`. Response shape only; stored provenance is unchanged (#2192).
- **watcher:** Watcher findings now carry delivery receipts scoped to the receiving host and worktree, so one host or checkout surfacing a finding no longer consumes it for the others (previously a single global open-to-surfaced transition let the first host to see a finding hide it from the rest). New fingerprints keep the absolute repository and worktree identity, so identical code in two checkouts no longer collapses into one lifecycle row that one branch's resolution could close for another. Existing exact-provenance fingerprints are bridged into the new dedup key without replaying active rows, relative legacy rows are kept as unattributed evidence, and receipt, scan, status, dedup and migration writes are serialized under one bounded local lock. Legacy global surfacing still works; per-host delivery needs the matching governance-plugin release (#2205).
- **dependencies:** the production `mcp` pin in `constraints.txt` moves from 2.1.1 to 2.2.0. The server's streamable HTTP transport runs stateless, so 2.2.0's idle-session expiry and 10,000-session cap do not apply to `/mcp/`. Its client-side changes do apply to MCP client code on this pin, such as the stdio proxy: an HTTP redirect is followed only within the endpoint's origin, and the OAuth client checks the authorization server's `issuer` on the legacy path too (#2229).
- **compose:** the Compose `redis` service moves from `redis:7-alpine` to `redis:8-alpine`. Redis 8 reads the existing Redis 7 RDB/AOF data on the named volume, but that volume holds session and identity state, so `cloud-deploy-runbook.md` gains a Redis 7 to 8 rollout: run the backup and restore drill while still on Redis 7, recreate only the Redis service, confirm an existing client still resolves its identity, and keep the pre-upgrade snapshot for a full session-TTL cycle. Rollback means restoring that snapshot before starting a Redis 7 image again; the change does not upgrade an existing deployment by itself (#2272).
- **python:** the server now requires Python 3.14 (`requires-python = ">=3.14"`, the classifiers, ruff's target, `.python-version`, the doctor's version check and the install docs). The container and the service templates already ran 3.14; a bare-metal install on 3.12 or 3.13 no longer installs the server package. The SDK keeps its `>=3.12` floor and is still tested on 3.12. CI runs the server suite on 3.14 only, in four coverage shards, and installs dependencies with uv instead of pip; the required status keeps its historical name `test (3.12)` (#2287).
- **identity guidance:** server hints, the tool catalog, refusal `safe_options`, the startup contract, commands, the manual and operator docs now recommend `spawn_reason='explicit'` for a deliberate handoff instead of `new_session`, which `lineage_semantics.py` records as non-intentional co-location; an agent following the old hints recorded a different lineage edge from one following the lifecycle skill. The catalog's step 1 no longer says "if continuing prior work", and its `sync_state` example drops `confidence=0.9`, since any value creates a scored prediction. Four skill passages no longer tell agents to call `identity()` with no arguments when continuity is unclear, because with no session proof that lookup can return a neighbour's identity; they say to pass `client_session_id` and to trust the answer only when `identity_assurance.caller_proven` is true (#2320).
- **review gate, author workflow:** the author now owns one command that starts or joins review and returns its findings. `ship.sh` joins review in the foreground (deferral stays available with `SHIP_NO_REVIEW=1` or `review.sh --background`), a caller joining a running review waits for its result instead of reporting success when a process starts, and `review_gate.py` joins native Codex review evidence for the current commit, then falls back to a fresh local Claude or Codex session within one shared budget with per-provider cooldowns and retry limits. When no reviewer finishes, or the `gh` CLI cannot be launched, the result is UNREVIEWED (exit 2) and CI shows a neutral `review` warning; a missing `gh` used to exit 1 with a traceback, which read as findings. An opt-in scheduled sweep (`scripts/ops/install-review-sweep.py`) includes quiet drafts and respects `no-auto-review` but never edits branches, marks ready or merges, and the gate reads currently published GitHub evidence, not an archive of deleted review comments (#2349, #2352, #2382).
- **review gate, native review evidence:** a joined native Codex clean result is persisted once as a receipt comment, so the `review` check turns green even when the bot's reaction arrived after the last workflow run; local review commands stop on closed or merged PRs and recheck before publishing; and the final handoff validates the freshly fetched diff, so an equivalent amend or base merge keeps the review while a push during publication returns UNREVIEWED. Bot-command mentions copied from a native review footer are rendered as plain names, so publishing evidence no longer launches an unintended cloud review. A disposition that cites a native review for the same finding count now survives a base merge instead of reopening as an open finding that nothing could clear (#2353, #2354, #2407).
- **review gate, quota and cloud sessions:** a review can be requested without local tooling by commenting `@codex review` on the PR (documented in `docs/operations/github-workflow-conventions.md`), and a Codex review whose only comments are thread replies with an empty body no longer counts as findings. Codex fix loops are capped at three full native rounds per PR, shown on the `review` check as "review round N of 3"; past the cap with only P2 findings open, `review.sh` starts no model run, and the findings are answered by `review.sh dispose` or, when `git config review.verifier` is set, by a verifier model that checks each finding against the fix commits and posts a `fix-verify:<model>` record, which checks the fixes but not new lines. P0 or P1 findings in the last round always get another full run, and `--reviewer` still spends a round past the cap on purpose. On 139 labelled pairs from this repository's own findings, a local `gemma4` verifier caught 59 of 66 non-fixes and passed 56 of 73 real fixes, so roughly one unaddressed P2 in nine past the cap would be accepted as fixed (#2369, #2401).
- **watcher:** pattern P016 moves to experimental (`EXP-P016`) and leaves `violation_taxonomy.yaml`, and P006 no longer fires when the governing `except` carries `# noqa: BLE001` or a bare `# noqa`, when the handler re-raises, or on files under `/tests/`; a 30-day audit (to 2026-09-24) found no confirmed catch from either pattern. `UNITARES_WATCHER_LOG_FILE` overrides the Watcher log path and the test suites set it, so pytest no longer writes into the operator's real Watcher log. Security patterns (P012) still fire on tests, the model pin, timeout and worktree scanning are unchanged, and #2424 later narrowed P006 further (#2396).
- **paused refusals:** the refusal a paused agent reads now says why, what is refused and how the pause ends, replacing "Circuit breaker triggered due to governance threshold violation". It gives the reason recorded with this pause (matched to `paused_at`, otherwise it says none is recorded), says check-ins and new shared-memory entries are refused and not queued while dialectic moves still work, explains that `self_recovery` rarely applies because a paused agent's risk stays frozen at the reading that paused it, and names the other exits: the pause's dialectic, operator resume, and expiry, which is a re-evaluation where a check-in that still trips pauses again. The glossary `pause` entry, the governance-lifecycle skill and the manual follow; the text written into an auto-opened dialectic still says "Circuit breaker triggered" (#2414).
- **dialectic resumption conditions:** a `risk_target` overshoot of a dialectic resumption condition now yields a guided proceed (`action: proceed`, `sub_action: guide`) and never a pause. The escalation ran after the circuit breaker had read the decision, so the agent was told "Paused" while its status stayed active and its writes were accepted. Actuating that pause was rejected: it would bypass gap suppression and the cold-start guard, its 1.5x overshoot rule is uncalibrated, and as of 2026-09-24 no recorded reviewer condition parsed to a `risk_target`. The dialectic-reasoning skill now says an unmet condition only guides (#2415).
- **identity handler logging:** four exception handlers in the identity handlers that swallowed errors silently or at debug level now log a warning when they fail (the resident-registration annotation on mint, SPAWNED lineage edge creation, `seed_genesis_from_parent` scheduling, and the `get_trajectory_status` trust-tier enrichment). A fifth, the R2 parent-UUID lookup, logs at debug because it currently raises on every explicit handoff and fails open to the same value. Logging only: control flow, return values and fail-open posture are unchanged (#2422).
- **watcher:** P006 (swallowed exception) findings now pass a deterministic AST post-filter, `p006_actually_fires` in `agents/watcher/agent.py`, after the model reports them. A Python finding is dropped only when every handler on the path from the cited line outward shows positive evidence that it reacts to the failure: a `raise`, a logging call at info level or above on a logger-named receiver (so `task.exception()` or `parser.error(...)` does not count), or a `return` of a non-None value, fallbacks such as `return []` included. `pass`, a bare `return` or `return None`, an assigned fallback, collecting the error and debug-only logging keep the finding, and so does anything the filter cannot check (no handler on the path, no line number, a file that is not Python or does not parse). Code in a nested `def` or `lambda`, and the handlers of a nested `try`, do not count as the outer handler reacting; a silent handler of a `try` nested below or around the cited line, including one inside a class body or later in the same `else` or `finally`, keeps the finding. `patterns.md` states the same rule, and the rule is a chosen standard rather than a measured threshold: no P006 finding has a recorded confirmed verdict (#2424, #2442, #2447).
- **watcher:** the unresolved listing (`--print-unresolved` at SessionStart and the `--surface-pending` chime) groups copies of one finding into one entry. Watcher state is shared across worktrees and the fingerprint keeps the absolute path and line number, so the same flagged code appeared once per worktree and once per line shift. Rows group when they share the repository (git common dir), the repo-relative path, the pattern, the flagged line's content hash and the displayed severity; each location keeps its own line, fingerprint and status and stays individually resolvable. Only the flagged line is hashed, so distinct handlers with the same line text also group, and the label says "locations, same line text" rather than "copies". Display only: `findings.jsonl`, the fingerprint, dedup, dismissal, resolve and the precision maths are unchanged, `Total unresolved: N` still starts with the row count, and the block never shows more rows than the ungrouped listing (#2427).
- **ci(skills):** `scripts/dev/check_plugin_attestation_rule.py` compares the plugin's port of the attestation rule (`attested_date` in its `_check_freshness.py`) with canonical's `vouching_date` over a fixed matrix of attestation directories and the plugin's real synced skills. `sync-plugin-skills.sh` runs it wherever it would report success and exits 5 on rule drift, which re-syncing cannot fix; `ship.sh` treats exit 5 as a warning. A crash or a comparison that never ran is reported as "not checked", never as drift or agreement. The check is advisory, needs a local plugin checkout, and is run by no CI in either repository (#2445).
- **dashboard passkeys:** with `UNITARES_DASHBOARD_RP_ID` unset, passkey sign-in is now off instead of defaulting the WebAuthn relying party to the original operator's domain, which `docker-compose.yml` also copied. A passkey is bound to one domain, so on every other install (a plain `docker compose up` on localhost included) passkey setup could never complete and nothing reported why. All six passkey endpoints now answer `503` with a `fix` field naming the settings to set, and with no relying party configured the WebSocket origin check refuses every cookie session. Upgrade note: a deployment that used passkeys through the old fallback must set `UNITARES_DASHBOARD_RP_ID` and `UNITARES_DASHBOARD_ORIGIN` before upgrading. The fleet-identity guard also refuses that domain in shipped string literals (#2456).
- **server name:** the server reports itself to MCP clients as `unitares` (`serverInfo.name`) instead of `governance-monitor-v1`, a name from before the project was called UNITARES that some clients displayed. Both transports (streamable HTTP and stdio), the `http_api` default and the client-config snippets `scripts/mcp` prints change. Clients connect by URL, so existing connections do not break; a client or script that matched on the old name needs updating (the cloud-session setup script accepts both) (#2469).
- **dashboard:** the core dashboard is cut to what any install can read: eight tabs (Overview, Agents, Discoveries, Dialectic, Activity, EISV, Risk, Security). The Residents, Automations, Adjudication, Telemetry, Metrics and Enforcement views leave the repository; this change left the server endpoints behind them in place, and #2510 later mounts the deployment-specific ones only through `UNITARES_ROUTE_PACKS`. The Overview drops from eleven panels to five cards plus Pulse, and its default page no longer calls `detect_stuck_agents`, `calibration(check)`, `detect_anomalies` or `/v1/agents/tier_distribution`. A deployment can bring its own tabs through `UNITARES_DASHBOARD_EXT_DIR`: `GET /dashboard/ext/{file}` serves `.js`, `.json` and `.css` from that directory, path-confined, `no-store` and only to an authenticated caller (404 when unset); `app.html` loads its `manifest.json`, an entry can never shadow a core tab, and a malformed or unloadable entry is skipped. The contract is in `dashboard/EXTENSIONS.md` (#2494).
- **dashboard Overview and Risk:** the Overview leads with agents rather than residents: cards for agents checked in within the hour, check-ins by verdict, dialectic (led by its recent failure rate), discoveries and system health, plus the latest check-in from any agent. The resident strip appears only when residents are configured. The Fleet Coherence card is removed, since its between-agent spread was too small to carry information. The offline `snapshot.js` is now a synthetic fixture instead of a capture of one deployment's fleet, and `preview.html` is deleted. The Risk trend no longer depends on a resident's scrape: a new gated `GET /v1/governance/trend?days=N` computes fleet-mean risk, guide and pause from `core.agent_state` over seven-UTC-calendar-day windows (capped at 60 days, cached 10 minutes). New gated `GET /v1/eisv/agents` returns one compact row per agent from the in-memory EISV ring, compact `/v1/eisv/recent` carries `agent_name` and `decision.action`, and it and `/api/activity` return `coverage_start` so an in-memory "last hour" count says how much history it covers. It also fixes `/api/activity`, which counted guide and `risk_pause` check-ins as proceed (including on the existing Activity histogram), and adds exact window `totals` (#2502).
- **http route packs:** deployment-specific HTTP routes are now mounted only when `UNITARES_ROUTE_PACKS` names their pack, and the shipped default mounts none. `reference-residents` holds `/v1/sentinel/{backlog,summary,adjudication-queue,adjudicate,model-adjudicate}`, `/v1/watcher/summary` and `/v1/vigil/summary`; `automation-census` holds `/api/automations`. Handlers are unchanged, a misspelled pack name is logged at startup, and routes any install can read (findings, events, incidents, `/v1/residents`, `/v1/metrics*`, telemetry health, enforcement divergence, `/v1/eisv/agents`, `/v1/governance/trend`) stay in core. Upgrade note: a deployment that runs the reference residents' backlog or adjudication tooling, or the automation census, must set `UNITARES_ROUTE_PACKS` to keep those routes (#2510).

### Removed

- **dialectic:** new resolutions no longer receive v2 party-HMAC signatures, on any finalize path: peer-review synthesis, the synthetic reviewer in `submit_thesis`, and LLM-assisted dialectic. The scheme was symmetric and keyed on api_keys the server stores, so a party HMAC the server computed attested only that the server wrote the row, which the attributed row already says; restoring key issuance would also have revived a deprecated credential. Minting stops inside `DialecticSession.finalize_resolution`, which now takes no api_key at all, rather than by relying on keys being absent, and no handler looks a key up for it. The stored shape is unchanged: `signature_a` and `signature_b` stay present and empty, so the record's key set, the `Resolution.hash()` formula served as `resolution_hash`, and the field set a `drr.v1` receipt covers are the same, and tests pin the hash of an unchanged record. New rows carry `signature_version` 3, so a record read on its own says why it is unsigned. `describe_attestation` reports such a row as `unsigned` with a new `unsigned_by_design` flag, which is false on every historical row. History is never stripped or backfilled, no migration runs, and historical rows classify exactly as before; `compute_signature` and `verify_signatures` stay for reading them. The offline receipt verifier now distinguishes an unsigned record from a single-signer one instead of warning "single-signer" for both. Party-level non-repudiation remains a goal whose lever is agent-held asymmetric keys, which is not scheduled. Decision D5 in `docs/proposals/active/federation-trust-decisions-2026-09-25.md` (#2463, closes #2449).
- **pi plugin coupling:** the server no longer knows `unitares-pi-plugin` by name. The operator retired the plugin, which put a `pi` proxy (each action forwarding to a tool anima-mcp already serves), `pi_restart_service`, and the Steward Mac-to-Pi sync loop inside governance-mcp. Deep health no longer imports the plugin or reports `pi_connectivity`; that import was the path behind the 2026-09-09 50-vs-51 capability miscount. `tool_catalog` drops its `pi` / `pi_restart_service` relationship records, `AuditLogger` drops the five cross-device methods only the plugin called, and the deploy-status, deploy-drift-doctor and dependabot-triage surfaces stop tracking the plugin repository. Five health-check tests that skipped on any machine without the plugin, CI included, now run everywhere. The generic `governance_mcp.plugins` mechanism is unchanged. Lumen's sensor-derived EISV still arrives through its own check-ins. Steward, the retired plugin's sync loop, is also dropped from the reference progress manifest (`config/resident_progress.example.json`), the dashboard residents view and the example resident rosters, so the progress probe stops expecting `eisv_sync_rows` from an agent that no longer runs; the generic `eisv_sync_rows` source remains available to a deployment that names it (#2198, #2242).
- **dialectic:** the `vote` parameter is removed from the wire schema. It was documented "for action=vote" against a router whose actions are get / list / quick / request / thesis / antithesis / synthesis / reassign, no handler read it, and nothing routed to it, so no working call can change. It was found by the new per-action declaration, which had nowhere to put it. `conditions`, which sat beside it under the same wrong action, is live -- it is the accepted alias for `proposed_conditions` -- and now documents its real actions, thesis and synthesis. This narrows the `dialectic` capability hash; per `docs/INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md` a removal wants a major schema contract or a deprecation window, and the argument for treating this one as compatible is that the parameter was unreachable rather than merely unused. (#2103)
- **tools:** `direct_resume_if_safe`, deprecated 2026-01-29 in favour of `self_recovery` and annotated "will be removed in v2.0", is removed. It was a `full`-mode-only tool with no caller in the SDK, the governance plugin, or the reference residents. No alias is left behind: an alias to `self_recovery(action="quick")` would answer the old name with narrower behavior (quick resumes only at risk < 0.40; the removed handler resumed without reflection up to 0.60), and retiring that band is the substance of the removal, as the deprecation's own migration said: quick below 0.40, `review` with a reflection above. A caller of the old name now gets `tool_not_found_error` with a suggestion. The catalog's three recovery entries that named `register=False` delegates as if they were callable tools (`quick_resume`, `self_recovery_review`, `check_recovery_options`) are folded into `self_recovery`'s entry as its actions. Registered tools: 43 to 42 here; `use_tool` (#2328) brings the released count back to 43. (#2093)
- **skills:** the server no longer serves the `discord-bridge` skill. Every source it cited lived in the separate Discord bridge repository, so every install served one operator's Discord setup, fleet names included, to every connecting agent; the skill moves to that repository. The `skills` tool now serves six skills, `unitares-governance` no longer lists it, and the plugin mirror drops it at its next sync (#2518).

### Fixed

- **dialectic reviewer:** a reviewer waiting to answer a paused agent now survives a governance server restart. Its connection runs in a worker task of its own, so a lost transport fails the pending call with a transient `GovernanceLinkLost` instead of cancelling the reviewer; the next call reconnects and resumes the same identity with its same-process continuity token rather than onboarding again, and a filing interrupted mid-write is retried from session state without filing twice. Before this, a restart inside the reviewer's one-hour continuation window killed the reviewer, and the paused agent's answer went unread until a liveness reap. The async SDK client gains `GovernanceToolRefused`, a subclass of `GovernanceConnectionError` raised when a tool answers `success: false`, so a caller can tell a refusal from a transport failure; the synchronous client still raises `GovernanceConnectionError` (#2508).
- **agent-facing hints:** three gaps an external agent hit while dogfooding (2026-09-24). (1) `get_governance_metrics` / `check_working_state` now declare `verbosity` (`minimal` | `standard` | `full`). The handler has read it since the tiers existed, but only `lite` was declared, so `/mcp/` dropped `verbosity` and an agent wanting more than the minimum could only reach `full` (~15 KB), whose size warning then said `lite=true`. The catalog, the raw-payload hint, `response_options` and the oversized-response hint now name `verbosity='standard'`; `response_options.current` reports the tier actually served: the handler and the envelope now resolve it through one function, which changes two inputs. An explicit `lite: null` now serves the default `minimal` (it built `full` while the envelope reported `minimal`), and on REST `get_governance_metrics`, the one unvalidated route, a string the schema reads as false (`"no"`, `"0"`, `"off"`) now serves `full` instead of `minimal`, as every validated route already did. `verbosity` is matched exactly, as the handler always matched it, and validated routes (`/mcp/`, REST `check_working_state`) now refuse an off-list value such as `"Standard"` that they used to ignore. `response_options.current` for `check_working_state` now names the tier (`minimal` / `standard` / `full`) where it said `lite` / `full`. Interface contract 1.15.0. (2) `record_result` returns `corroboration_hint` when a self-attested claim grades `claim_only`: the reference keys that raise it to `self_report_with_refs` (one each from the grader's pr, commit, ci and test families; the command family is left out because its keys are tool-observation triggers; a test grades every advertised key alone and beside a partial tool description), and that self-attested detail is capped at `tool_observed` whatever it carries. It deliberately does not list the shapes that reach `tool_observed`: that grade is also the calibration admission weight, a caller can reach it by description alone, and whether it should is an open operator decision, so the hint only advertises grades below the calibration floor. Response-only, never persisted. (3) dialectic `wait_assessment` gains `poll_after_s`, the remaining reviewer budget as a number, set only while `too_early`; it is null when overdue or when no budget exists, rather than an invented interval (#2430).
- **agent locks (fcntl backend):** two writers could hold the same agent's lock at once. The stale-lock check released its probe lock and then unlinked the file, and acquirers never checked which inode they had locked, so an acquirer could lock the orphaned file while a newcomer locked a fresh one at the same path. It also removed files that were still held when the recorded pid looked dead or the timestamp looked old. A lock file is now stale only when no process holds it: removal (`state_locking.remove_lock_file_if_free`) probes with a non-blocking exclusive flock and unlinks while still holding it, and every fcntl acquirer (sync, async, and the `DistributedLock` file fallback) re-checks after `flock()` that it holds the file currently at the path, reopening if not. A test with four writer processes and a concurrent cleaner fails on the old code and passes now; it guards the acquirer re-check, and a deterministic test pins that the cleaner unlinks while its probe still holds the lock. `DistributedLock.force_release` keeps a held file lock instead of deleting it (deleting a held flock's file never released the holder; it only let a second one in), and its acquisition timeouts raise `LockTimeoutError` too. The `admin(cleanup_locks)` / `cleanup_stale_locks` tool, which derived its root as `src/` and so swept a directory nothing writes to while reporting success, now sweeps the lock writer's directory and reports the active lock backend. Lock-acquisition timeouts now raise `state_locking.LockTimeoutError` (a `TimeoutError` subclass), and the update path catches only that: it no longer sweeps (a lock timeout means a live holder) or advises the cleanup tool, and a `TimeoutError` from the work done under the lock is no longer reported as `LOCK_TIMEOUT`: `process_agent_update`'s fail-open branch reports it as an unexpected error. On the fcntl paths only the `flock()` call is treated as contention now, so an `OSError` raised by the caller's own code inside the lock (a `TimeoutError`, a `ConnectionError`) reaches the caller once with the lock released, instead of surfacing as a `TypeError`. The default advisory backend creates no lock files, so the lock-file changes do not reach it; the update-path timeout change above applies to every backend (#2433).
- **closure_class is stored; update_finding declares it; interface contract 1.20.0:** `closure_class` and `closure_evidence`, validated since #1752, are now written by every knowledge update path (the AGE SQL sync and graph node, the AGE SQL-only fallback, the Postgres backend) and by both add paths' SQL inserts, where the AGE vertex already carried them (no store handler sets a class, so adds behave as before; an upsert follows the incoming status, so a re-add that reopens a row clears its class), and read back by every converter; until now each path copied a fixed column set that left them out, and no live row carried a class. Migration 071 widens 064's `discoveries_closure_class_requires_closed` to admit a class on `archived` and `cold` as well as the closing statuses, so the KG lifecycle can still archive a classified finding and move it to cold (under 064 the AGE backend would have left such a row `resolved` forever, logging the refusal every run, and the Postgres backend would have lost the rest of the run). An update that reopens a finding (`open` or `disputed`) clears both fields on every path, including writers that know nothing about classes, such as dialectic's resolution, and the status-only `kg_update_status`. AGE node creation sets the pair explicitly, to NULL on an unclassified node, so a re-added id cannot keep a stale class on its vertex. A class sent without a status is judged against the stored status and refused on an open finding with `INVALID_PARAM` before storage (otherwise, with the class now written, the constraint would refuse it there and the AGE backend would report "Discovery not found"); a class alone now classifies a finding closed earlier; evidence without a class, evidence that is not an object, or evidence over 8 KiB as stored JSON is refused instead of dropped (on AGE an oversized property would have failed the whole update as "Discovery not found"); a non-owner of a high or critical finding may set a class only in the call that closes it, and the refusal says so and names that call instead of "Retry with status only", which dropped the class (it says the same for `resolution_notes`). The update response's `closure_class` is the value read back from the record, with a `closure_class_note` if it differs from what was sent, and the note on an unclassified close names `update_finding(discovery_id=..., status=..., closure_class='...')` with each class's evidence keys. `knowledge(action='details')` returns the class and its evidence (paginated or not), search results carry the class and expanded results its evidence, and the lean search digest is unchanged. The AGE SQL-only fallback, which called `db._pool.fetchval` on an ExecutorPool that has no such method and so failed every update it handled, now runs its UPDATE and tag sync in one transaction on the backend's connection, so a failed tag sync rolls the UPDATE back; reached from a missing AGE node, it uses the transaction the Cypher MATCH already holds rather than taking a second pool connection. Interface contract 1.20.0: `update_finding` declares `closure_class` and `closure_evidence` with the router's descriptions (before, `/mcp/` dropped both from an `update_finding` call); `knowledge`'s `discovery_type` says store defaults it to `note` (it said "Required"), promote to `insight`, update retypes and search filters, and on `update_finding` it says an omitted type keeps the stored one; `closure_class` says a class may also be set alone on a finding that is already closed, and `closure_evidence` states its bound; `observe`'s `target_agent_id` names the `audit_events` and `outcome_evidence` filters, which match the stored `agent_id` exactly and do not resolve labels (a UUID for most agents, but some audit writers record a name instead, such as the stuck sweep's `system`), `until` names `outcome_evidence` and `agent_ids` the `anomalies` and `aggregate` scope; `dialectic`'s `issue_description` names `action=quick`. Nothing is removed, retyped or renamed; five input digests and the surface digest move. `KnowledgeGraphAGE.update_discovery` now reports a graph result that carries an error as a failed update instead of routing it to the SQL-only orphan fallback, which would have written SQL and left the AGE node behind; no current query path returns such a row, and a missing node still comes back empty and takes the fallback. The migration is opt-in at deploy and must land before the code (#2489).
- **check-ins:** compact, standard and minimal `sync_state` responses, and mirror's actionable margin, now carry `unmeasurable_edges` and `margin_scope` beside `margin` when an edge went unassessed. The formatter used to drop them, so an agent whose coherence edge is unassessed (every agent today) read `margin: "comfortable"` with nothing saying which edges it covered. The envelope already lifted both fields into `state_summary`; they now arrive. Fully assessed margins add nothing (#2448).
- **response size:** three default calls returned far more than a caller asked for (measured on a live server, 2026-09-25). `dialectic(action="list")` was 173 KB because every row carried its full `topic` (up to ~12 KB). List rows now carry a 280-character preview with `topic_truncated` and `topic_chars`, while `get` and `include_transcript=true` still return the full text; the dashboard's Dialectic tab gets a "full topic" expander that fetches it, and the Elixir `dialectic_live` pane marks a previewed topic with its full length and points to `get`. A bare `skills()` returned every skill's full markdown (100 KB for seven skills). It now returns an index with `content_omitted: true`, and `name=` and `since_version=` still return content, which is how the plugin adapter fetches. `agent(action="list")` / `list_agents` never applied the lite defaults it documents (20 agents, last 7 days): the schema passes `limit` and `recent_days` as explicit `None`, which bypassed the handler's fallbacks, so every default call built the whole roster, the response serializer cut it to 100 arbitrary rows plus a `"... (N more items)"` string, and `shown` still reported the full roster size. The defaults now apply, with the caller's own row moved to the front so "always include the requesting agent" survives the page. Lite pages are capped at the serializer's 100, so `shown` matches the rows sent, and the overflow hint points to `lite=false` paging. Full mode with no `limit` returns one page of 100 rows in total, so `summary.returned` is true. For grouped calls (the default) that narrows the previous behaviour, where the serializer cut each status group to 100 separately. Explicit full-mode limits page as before. `summary.by_health` is now counted over the same pre-pagination population as `total` and `by_status` in every full-mode call; it used to count only the returned page. The `skills` `name` description now says a bare call returns the index (interface contract 1.16.0) (#2435).
- **update_finding / store_finding hints, describe_tool lite, SDK get_metrics:** hints on the finding write paths now name something that works as written (observed live, 2026-09-26). The note on a closing update without `closure_class` said `Pass closure_class=['duplicate', 'fix_verified', ...]`, a list value that fails validation for a string parameter, and did not say that `fix_verified` also needs `closure_evidence`. It now names the classes as prose and, for each class, the evidence keys the validator demands, read from the validator's own table. The follow-up call the note names repeats the status just set, `status=<the status just set>`, because a non-owner of a high or critical finding may add notes (and a class) only alongside a cross-agent closing status, and the status-less call was refused to exactly that caller. A closing update that already carried notes is told they are the record rather than that it declares nothing. `CLOSURE_CLASS_PERSISTED` is derived in tests from the three storage update paths, so the note cannot claim the class is stored when it is not, or the reverse. (Where and how the class is stored, and which call the note names, is in this release's entry on `closure_class` being stored; in between the two changes the note pointed at `resolution_notes`, because nothing stored the class then.) The anonymous-writer hint on `store_finding` names how to bind (the `client_session_id` `start_session` returned, else `start_session(force_new=true)`), and the display-name hint passes `client_session_id` to `identity`. `describe_tool(lite=true)` for an alias that pins a router action, or a router narrowed with `action=...`, now leads with what the action's handler requires at call time, marked `(required at call time)`, then the action's own parameters, primary first, and no longer fills from other actions' parameters: `update_finding` lists (after #2489) `discovery_id`, `status`, `resolution_notes`, `closure_class`, `closure_evidence` and `superseded_by` where it listed `content`, `details`, `summary`, `discovery_type` and `tags`, and `get_discovery_details` names `discovery_id` where it listed search parameters. A narrowed router view whose `action` has a default says to pass the action (`self_recovery(action='quick')` was described as `reason` alone, which runs `check`). `ACTION_REQUIRED_FIELDS` declares the requirements (`knowledge`; `dialectic` quick's `issue_description`), and `ACTION_FIELDS` gains reads the handlers already made (`knowledge` update, store, get and synthesize; `observe` anomalies, aggregate, audit_events, outcome_evidence and bridge; `dialectic` quick, request, thesis, synthesis and reassign); tests hold both to the handler source for every router that dispatches through an action table. No advertised description or input schema changes. **sdk:** `get_metrics()` returned `MetricsResult.metrics == {}` on every call, because the `check_working_state` envelope has no `metrics` key; both clients now read the reading from `state_summary` and `action_summary` (the canonical payload first when `raw_governance` is present), fill `metrics` with E, I, S, V, coherence, risk_score, verdict and primary_eisv_source, and add `verdict`, `action`, `coherence` and `risk` to `MetricsResult`. These SDK changes ship in `unitares-sdk` 0.4.0 (#2479).
- **identity check-in thread context:** the `process_agent_update` `thread_context.honest_message` for a `sibling_locus` fork no longer claims "you share a registry UUID … no child UUID minted". That text is false for a `force_new` identity minted onto a thread other processes have occupied, and the check-in path cannot tell that case from a resumed UUID. It now passes `minted_fresh=None` and gets wording true in both cases. The onboard response, which does know, keeps its specific variants. (#2385).
- **identity guidance for a dropped `client_session_id`:** a caller that omits its id now gets pointed back to that id instead of to a second mint or to `continuity_token` on every call. (1) The unbound `check_working_state` / `get_governance_metrics` payload and the glossary `unbound` entry lead with "if this process already called start_session, repeat this read with the client_session_id it returned", then `start_session(force_new=true)` for a process with no identity. The structured `next_action` follows the same order: with no `client_session_id` on the call (or only a transport-injected one), `tool`/`example` name `check_working_state(client_session_id=...)` and the mint moves to `otherwise`, so an agent acting on the structured fields is not sent to a second identity; with a caller-sent id that names no agent, the mint still leads, since repeating that id cannot help. The glossary meaning is now "No caller-proven identity on this call"; the `⚪ unbound` status and `unbound` verdict are unchanged, and the payload still names no uuid. (2) The strict (`STRICT_IDENTITY_REQUIRED`) `sync_state` / `process_agent_update` refusal for a server-inferred binding leads with retrying with the id `start_session` returned. `continuity_token` appears only inside `identity(agent_uuid=..., continuity_token=..., resume=true)`, followed by a retry with the id that call returns. The read-only option is `check_working_state(client_session_id=...)`, and `do_not` warns against minting a second identity to clear the refusal. A mint (`start_session(force_new=true)`) is offered last, only to a process that never called `start_session`, since the pin can resolve such a process to a co-located sibling. The hint quotes the same remedy text as the `identity_assurance.how_to_strengthen` it embeds; that breadcrumb, on both the write and identity paths, now says to pass the id the rebind returns. (3) A pre-onboard read on `/mcp/` now counts an `X-Session-ID` header as proof when `derive_session_key` keyed the call on it (step 4, `caller_asserted`). A header-only read therefore resolves on the same key a header-only write on the same connection already used, as REST reads already did. A header naming no session still reads `unbound`. `Mcp-Session-Id`, `X-Client-Id` and the OAuth client id are not counted. The typed wrapper's transport injection, which looked like the cause, never runs on a direct `/mcp/` call because FastMCP None-fills the argument first; it is now documented as unreachable and pinned by a test that goes through FastMCP validation. (4) The shared contract rule 2 and `docs/ontology/identity.md` say that when the minting call carries no session or client signal of its own (bearer-token `/mcp/` or plain REST), its weak / `caller_proven: false` assurance describes that call, not later threading, and that threading is confirmed on the next call with `check_working_state(client_session_id=...)` or `identity(client_session_id=...)`; rule 2 adds that a pin-resolved `sync_state` is refused under strict identity. `identity.md` also says that refusal is not a general write gate: a low- or medium-severity `store_finding` can still be attributed through the pin. `governance-lifecycle` says bearer-token `/mcp/` keeps no binding between calls (an OAuth client's binding is per client), gives an `X-Session-ID` header as an example of a transport session signal, and no longer tells a live process to mint again when a call resolved only by weak signals. No tool description or input schema changed (#2475).
- **identity: a target named through `use_tool` resolves its session as it does named directly; both read gates judge session headers by one predicate; refusals and `label_source` stop pointing at a second identity:** (1) The nested `/mcp/` `use_tool` path no longer copies the transport session (an `X-Session-ID` header, `Mcp-Session-Id`, the OAuth or `X-Client-Id` client, or the IP:UA fingerprint) into a session-injected target's `client_session_id`, flagged transport-injected. A direct call never did, since FastMCP None-fills the argument first. So a header-only `sync_state` through `use_tool` now derives `x_session_id` / caller-asserted and passes `STRICT_IDENTITY_REQUIRED` as it does directly, a proof-less `check_working_state` through `use_tool` reads `unbound` instead of resolving a server-inferred binding and serving its state, and a fingerprint-only write reaches the step-7 onboard pin. An explicit outer `client_session_id` still reaches the target as caller input. The parity is for the session only: `use_tool` does not apply the target's `/mcp/` argument schema, so an argument that schema drops (`agent_id` or `agent_uuid` on the metrics tools) still reaches a nested target. (2) The REST pre-onboard read gate no longer counts an `X-Client-Id` / `X-MCP-Client-Id` header as proof: it names a client, not a process, so two processes of one client could read each other's state. Both read gates now judge a session header through one predicate (`transport_session_is_read_proof`), applied to the source that produced the session key, under which only `X-Session-ID` proves a read; on REST that holds whether the header reached the derivation as an id the transport put in the body or because the body's `client_session_id` was null, empty or invalid. The predicate covers session headers only: a `client_session_id` the caller sent and a verified `continuity_token` still prove a read on both transports (`/mcp/` also counted an `agent_uuid` argument and a UUID `X-Agent-Id` header until #2490). REST writes are unchanged. No caller in this repo, the governance plugin, the discord bridge, anima-mcp or the host adapter sends `X-Client-Id`. (3) The strict refusal for a required call that resolved no identity picks its recovery from the resolver's result on both transports (`identity_bootstrap.unbound_call_refusal`). A session miss no longer leads with `onboard(force_new=true)`: with no `client_session_id` of the caller's own on the call, it leads with retrying with the id `start_session` returned, then the `identity(agent_uuid, continuity_token, resume=true)` rebind, and offers a mint only to a process that never called `start_session`; with a caller-sent id that names nothing, the rebind leads and the mint is for a process that cannot rebind. On REST, where every unbound required call got the default mint-first refusal, a resume the hijack guard rejected, a substrate-anchored resident over HTTP and a token naming an inactive agent now get the cause-specific hint `/mcp/` gives. A session lookup that raised (`pg_lookup_exception`) gets the server-failure retry on both transports instead of a mint-first recovery. The recovery sentences the strict write refusal and the unbound read quote now live in `identity_bootstrap` with their text unchanged. The bare-onboard lineage refusal keeps its mint-first defaults. (4) `agent_signature` and the knowledge display payload report a label the server assigned as `label_source: "auto"` instead of `"claimed"`: the mint's `claude_code-opus_<uuid8>` label (logged as `[AUTO_NAME]`), which the mint now records as `auto_label` in the identity metadata, and the `Agent_<uuid8>` name a knowledge write gives an agent with no meaningful label (recorded in memory, where that name is set). A label is server-assigned while it still equals the record, so a claimed name renamed `<name>_<uuid8>` by a collision still reads `claimed`. Agents minted earlier, and lazily persisted mints, carry no record and read as before. No tool description or input schema changed. Every surface that branches on whether the caller sent its own client_session_id (the REST prebind and strict gate, the /mcp/ session-miss refusal, both unbound metrics reads) now uses one rule: an id the transport put there, or that a REST session header supplied, is not the caller's, and one that normalizes to nothing was never looked up; so a header-only REST read gets the same no-id recovery as on /mcp/. The unbound read's caller-sent branch leads with the identity(agent_uuid=..., continuity_token=..., resume=true) rebind, as the strict refusal does, with the mint under otherwise (#2478).
- **inference provenance:** a delegated result now carries `model_reporting_status` (why `model_used` is empty when a host CLI reports no model) and `model_reroutes` (a mid-run model switch the host observed), which the host adapter already produced but the delegated lane dropped. Both reach the consult diagnostics and the `consultation` audit row. A failed upstream attempt that actually sent the brief now carries the attempted host's registry `host_id`, `provider_kind` and `privacy_class` (never the raw base URL), on both lanes, so the consult failure details and the audit row say whether a failed call was tried locally or off-box. The `consult`, `call_model` and `delegate_inference` descriptions now say that a successful call also runs one governance update on the caller's state (Energy accounting). What this does not do: it does not change or remove that update, which waits until after the 2026-12-01 registered read so the read's inputs stay fixed; it does not record which Ollama build served a call (tags carry no digest); and a failure before any host is resolved still names no route (#2483).
- **identity: a pre-onboard self-read never shows state from a binding the server only inferred; unbound reads say why; a claimed name replaces a knowledge write's auto-name; name hints carry the session:** (1) Three paths that served a server-inferred binding's real state to a proof-less `check_working_state` / `get_governance_metrics` are closed. On `/mcp/` the sticky transport binding is no longer consulted for a pre_onboard read: it ran ahead of the #945 short-circuit, so a connection that sends its own `Mcp-Session-Id` (Claude Code subagents share the parent's) or a UDS caller on the bare fingerprint read whichever agent last resolved there, directly and through `use_tool`. On `/mcp/` an `agent_uuid` argument (reachable only through `use_tool`) and a UUID `X-Agent-Id` header no longer count as read proof: the derivation ignores both, so such a read resolved on the onboard pin or the connection's session and served that binding, not the named agent. On REST a body with a null or empty `client_session_id` and no session header no longer reaches the sticky binding. Such reads now return the unbound payload. Writes are unchanged: they still use the sticky binding, which the strict write gate treats as server-inferred. A read that carries proof (the caller's `client_session_id`, an `X-Session-ID` header, a verified `continuity_token`) reads as before. In the 14 days of `audit.tool_usage` before the change, one pre-onboard read with no session id in its arguments was served from a binding, by the agent this batch uses for its probes; the count covers the recorded transports only. (2) The unbound read's `next_action` is keyed on why nothing bound, with the recovery the strict refusal gives for the same resolver result: a session lookup that raised (`pg_lookup_exception`) or a resolver failure is retried with no onboarding offered, and a hijack-guard rejection, a substrate resident reading over REST or a token naming an inactive agent is named with its reason, where both used to be told their id "names no identity" and offered a rebind or a mint. `get_governance_metrics` then carries `unbound_reason` with the keys the strict refusal puts in its `surface_context`; `check_working_state` carries it under `raw_governance` at `verbosity='standard'` or `'full'`, and its default envelope gives the cause through `next_action` only. A read without proof and a plain session miss get the same recovery as before. The server-failure texts shared with the strict refusal now say "a missing identity" and "mint" instead of using "onboard" as a verb, and the hijack-guard hint says "Mint a new identity with" instead of "Re-onboard with". (3) After a knowledge write names an agent `Agent_<uuid8>`, a later claim (`identity(client_session_id=..., name=...)`) is the name displayed, and `label_source` reads `claimed` (also when a collision renames the claim to exactly that `Agent_<uuid8>`: the claim clears the knowledge write's in-memory auto-name with it); it used to keep showing the auto-name as `auto`. (4) Thirteen recovery, onboarding and description hints that showed `identity(name='...')` now show `identity(client_session_id='...', name='YourName')`, since a name alone lets identity() resolve a co-located agent; the argument-less `identity()` steps in every recovery pattern (including `ownership_required`, `missing_client_session_id`, `session_mismatch` and `permission_denied`) now pass `client_session_id` too, and a test holds every pattern to it. (5) Interface contract 1.21.0, numbered after 1.20.0 (#2489): tool descriptions only. `check_working_state` and `get_governance_metrics` say which proof reads your own state and that an inferred binding is never read; `/mcp/` drops `agent_id`; through `use_tool` or REST it names the agent to read unless the caller is bound as a different agent, which is refused (`identity_mismatch`), except that `get_governance_metrics` over REST answers it for any caller; `check_working_state` marks such a reading `caller_proven=false` on an inferred session. No input digest or the surface digest moves. The `dialectic-reasoning` and `governance-fundamentals` skills' self-read examples now pass `client_session_id`, since an argless `check_working_state()` from a client that sends no `X-Session-ID` header now answers `unbound`. Strict identity refusals also carry `refused: true` beside `rollout_flag`, a marker that says what it means to a caller that does not know the #425 contract; the payload stays success-shaped, with no `error` key, and `identity_refusal_status()` still keys on `rollout_flag` (#2490).
- **tool docs:** `make docs` runs again. It had called a `scripts/generate_tool_docs.py` that no longer existed. The generator it meant scanned handler files for decorators: on this tree it found 4 of the 43 registered tools and missed every `action_router` tool. Widening its glob does not fix that model: when #2355 tried it with every category rendered, review found it documenting `register=False` internals as tools (it counts every decorated function) and `consult`'s 480s as the 30s default (it reads only literal timeouts). `scripts/diagnostics/generate_tool_docs.py` now reads the runtime registry, through the same loader as `tool_edge_index.py`, and writes `docs/dev/TOOL_REFERENCE.md`: every registered tool by category, with its identity class, its timeout and a ceiling on each action's (the smaller of the router's and its delegate's, e.g. `dialectic` `request` 105s, `thesis` 90s), its workflow alias and older names (and the transports that still resolve them), and the description `describe_tool` serves, with JSON examples and aligned columns kept verbatim. Both generators now pin `UNITARES_DIALECTIC_REVIEW_BUDGET` and `UNITARES_CALL_MODEL_TIMEOUT` to their defaults before import, so a local override is not committed as if it shipped. The CI `smoke` job and the doctor (`tool_reference_fresh`) check the reference is current; `make docs` now regenerates all four generated docs. `tool_edge_index.py` also stops calling a machine without `mcp` installed a "defect in the tree": a dependency missing at the handler package's own import now gets the install remedy, as one missing deeper already did. And a handler subpackage that raises while it imports (anything under `middleware/` or `updates/`) is now reported as a failed module; the package walk used to re-raise it and crash both generators (#2425).
- **check_working_state and size/next-step hints:** the default read says each fact once, and every hint names something the caller can use (external agent report, 2026-09-25). Measured from the real handler: a provisional default read goes from 3,350 to 1,986 bytes, an uninitialized one from 2,751 to 1,586, and `include_state=true` from 7,145 to the default's 1,986. `include_state` only made the envelope attach the whole canonical payload (the nested state it described surfaced only when state interpretation failed), so the envelope no longer escalates on it, the handler no longer requests that state, and its description now says it is retained for compatibility with no effect (the in-repo `ollama_bridge` passes `verbosity="standard"` instead). The default envelope gives E, I, S, V and `risk_score` as bare values (the per-field contract is on the tool description and the risk band in `risk_summary`), trims the default read's coherence badge to sync_state's `{value, status, source, role}` shape so the "not health-rated" disclosure stays inline (the `standard` tier still serves a bare coherence float, as before), drops `legacy_diagnostics` when it only repeats that badge (before the first check-in the badge reads "pending", so the block stays), drops `action_summary.reason` when it is the verdict's `meaning` or the payload's guidance, and teaches the tier ladder once, in `raw_governance_hint` (before the first check-in it names only `verbosity='full'`, since no tier has basin or mode yet; an unbound read gets no tier hint, since every tier is the same unbound payload until the caller binds); `response_options` still reports the tier on `standard` and `full`. The canonical `get_governance_metrics` minimal shape is unchanged. Every tier now carries `next_action`, which `unitares.lifecycle-envelope.v1` requires and the default and full reads lacked (the conformance test hand-supplied it); it comes from the payload's own next step, then, for a paused or rejected agent, the step that follows that decision (interpreted guidance does not know the decision), then the interpreted guidance or the verdict's recommended step, and `state_summary` no longer repeats a second one. A real read on a call whose session the server only inferred is now marked with a compact `identity_assurance` (`tier`, `caller_proven: false`, `session_source`); caller-proven reads are unchanged. Since #2490 no self-read is served from an inferred binding, so the mark appears only on a read that names its target with an explicit `agent_id` (through `use_tool`, or `check_working_state` over REST). `verdict_caveat` names only a path present in the response: `state_summary.evidence` on a metrics read, a `raw_governance` path only when raw is included, and nothing on a bounded `sync_state`, whose basis is already inline (it used to point into an omitted `raw_governance`, and following it meant another check-in). `_response_size.reduce_with` never names the mode in effect (read from the request, not from `response_options`), and `start_session` gets none, since acting on it would mint a second identity; the size receipt stays. `list_tools(lite=true)` carries no `agent_signature` (for a caller-asserted transport-session or `x_client_id` binding its 1,559-1,943 B signature put the 4 KiB handshake at 4,957-5,341 B; a server-inferred binding's already collapsed to `{"uuid": null}`), and its tip names `describe_tool(..., lite=true)` for parameters rather than the 10-17 KB default record. `describe_tool`'s in-process default now matches its advertised `lite=false`; the Wave 3a probe asks for `lite=true` explicitly and serves the same bytes. Interface contract 1.19.0: the `include_state` and `verbosity` descriptions (which promised EISV meanings `standard` never carried) move the two metrics input digests and the surface digest, and `process_agent_update`/`sync_state`'s `response_mode` description now names the degraded-identity and warnings triggers that make `auto` resolve to mirror (#2472).
- **oauth:** an authorization code now yields at most one token pair. `exchange_authorization_code` removed the code with `pop(code, None)` and went on to mint tokens whether or not the code was still there, while `load_authorization_code` only reads. Two `/token` requests carrying the same code could therefore both pass the load and both receive valid access and refresh tokens, contrary to RFC 6749 section 4.1.2, which requires an authorization code to be used once. In the current asyncio flow nothing between the load and the exchange appears to suspend, so the window may be narrow or unreachable in practice, but the guarantee now holds by construction: the exchange claims the code before minting anything, exactly as `exchange_refresh_token` already claims its refresh token, and a second redemption gets `invalid_grant` ("authorization code already used") with no token issued. Also fixes the server's startup error line, which printed a literal `\n` before `❌ Error:` because the source escaped the backslash. Both defects were found by the Antigravity reviewer (Gemini 3.1 Pro) in the changed files of an unrelated PR during the file-mode review experiment. (#2498).
- **ci(skills):** the plugin skill-sync workflow checks unitares out with full history. On its first live run it refused with exit 4: when the mirror and canonical declare the same verification date, `sync-plugin-skills.sh`'s direction guard decides from canonical's git history, and a depth-1 checkout has none, so it fails closed. `open-plugin-skill-sync-pr.sh` now prints enough of a failed sync's log to include the refusal's reasons, not only its hint. When the mirror is already in sync, it also closes its own sync PR if one is still open (a hand-made sync merged first), deleting the automation branch and saying why in a comment. The shared contract adds the plugin skill mirror to the single-writer surfaces: do not sync it by hand. That tie-break is itself new in this release (#2132): on an equal date the guard syncs only when the mirror's exact bytes are a past version of the file in canonical's history, a mirror dated later than canonical still refuses, and any failure to read history refuses (#2471).
- **SDK metrics and finding closes:** `get_metrics().action` is now the policy action (`proceed` or `pause`) at every verbosity, as its docstring and the `MetricsResult.action` field comment already promised. The resolver used to read the canonical lifecycle word (`raw_governance.last_decision_action` or `verdict.decision_action`) before the envelope's `action_summary.action`, so the same agent read `proceed` at the default tier but `guide`, `resumed` or `not_paused` at `standard` and `full`, where `raw_governance` rides along. It now reads `action_summary.action` first and maps a canonical lifecycle word the way the server's envelope does (`_POLICY_ACTION`, pinned to `envelope_step._ACTION_ALIASES` by a test), so callers that compare `action == "proceed"` behave the same whatever tier they request. `resolved_at` is now stamped on the transition into `resolved`, or on a resolved finding that has no `resolved_at` yet; repeating `resolved` leaves an existing stamp alone. The closure note's follow-up call repeats the closing status, because a non-owner of a high or critical finding may append resolution notes only together with it, and that repeat used to move the finding's resolution time to the moment of the follow-up. Both are review follow-ups to #2479, which merged before they could land on its branch (#2482).
- **ci(skills):** merging master into a branch no longer turns a skill STALE when master re-checked that skill across the source change it brought in after the branch last stamped it. A stamp now records `superseded_digests`, the digest a changed source was last verified at, which says its verifier re-checked the skill across that change and left its text alone; a stamp records one only when the newest record certified exactly the text being stamped, so text edited in the re-check, even back to wording certified earlier, records none, since the wording it replaced did not survive. That sign is best-effort: a merged-in record of another version can defeat it, and what then slips through is the given-up pairing below. The checker carries a digest certified for the current text forward along transitions recorded after that text's newest certification, even when the stamp that recorded them certified another version of the text; a transition recorded earlier says nothing about the current text and carries nothing. Before this, a branch that edited and stamped a skill went STALE whenever master changed a cited source, even though master had re-stamped its own text against the new content, and every base merge meant another manual re-stamp. Replaying the 19 failing CI runs from 2026-09-24 20:40 to 09-25 08:05 UTC (6 branches) found 29 of 65 STALE sources whose content a stamp had recorded for another version of the skill text, an upper bound on this class. The order is by stamp time, the only order the records carry: a branch that re-stamps after master's re-check and then merges master looks exactly like an unstamped re-land after a revert, and still has to re-stamp. The one pairing given up is the branch's own text edits against master's source change; AGING still bounds it. A revert, a stale branch's stamp of old content, and content no stamp has recorded (a branch's unstamped change, or both sides editing one file) stay STALE. Records written before this carry no transitions, so the relief starts with the next stamps. Re-stamping a carried text where a cited source is absent (another repository) carries the certified digest, not the carried one, so that source reads STALE again where it is visible and is re-stamped there. STALE now lists every drifting source, not only the first. `--prune` keeps every record inside the AGING window whose transition leads to a cited source's current content, for any version of the text; a branch whose own text sets a longer window can lose a carrier to a prune on master and then re-stamps, as before (#2438).
- **start_session:** a default `start_session(force_new=true)` now returns the routine shape for the two common mint classes that were still getting the full onboard record a second time under `raw_governance` (4.4-5.2 KB). One is a fresh uuid on a thread earlier sessions occupied (`sibling_locus`, e.g. behind a shared IP:UA fingerprint). It keeps `state_summary.predecessor_uuid`, adds `state_summary.episode_fork_kind`, and keeps the sentence saying co-location does not establish lineage. The other is a named mint, whose `resident_registration` verdict is lifted in compact form (`status`, `on_roster`, and for `not_on_roster` (and, briefly, `caller_supplied_tags`) a short `detail`: what it costs, that this identity cannot gain the tags, and the fix: add the name to the roster, restart, mint fresh). A written bootstrap ack is lifted whole. Measured on the real onboard handler: sibling 4,411 → 1,461 B, named 4,488 → 1,434 B, named sibling 5,229 → 1,743 B; the anonymous position-1 mint is unchanged at 1,152 B. Budgets are now per class: 1,200 B anonymous, 1,550 B sibling, plus up to 300 B per lifted notice. A declared, provisional or rejected lineage, any spawn reason other than the default `new_session` (including `explicit` with no parent), a sibling whose earlier nodes were pruned (no `predecessor_uuid` to lift), a missing or unknown fork kind, a mint onboard could not place on a thread (no `thread_context`), a label rename, a deprecation, `temporal_context` and any unknown key still keep the whole record, and `response_shape_reason` now names the field that kept it (absent when a plain mint asked for `response_mode="full"`). A root-node mint with a lineage spawn reason (`subagent`, `dispatch`, `compaction`, `dialectic_reviewer`) and no parent is classified `identity_lineage` and now keeps the whole record too; it was treated as routine before. `explicit` with no parent is classified `none` and keeps the record through the spawn-reason rule (`response_shape_reason: thread_context.spawn_reason=explicit`) (#2473).
- **identity docs:** the governance-lifecycle skill no longer says S1-a accepts `onboard(continuity_token=...)` during a deprecation window. S1-c refuses it (`status: continuity_token_resume_rejected`); the skill names the `identity(agent_uuid, continuity_token, resume=true)` rebind instead. The `_is_routine_proceed` docstring now matches the code: an unassessed edge stays visible on a routine proceed, and only a near edge or a review nudge disqualifies one (#2465).
- **agent response envelopes:** two accuracy follow-ups to the write acknowledgements that omit `raw_governance` by default (#2439). First, `store_finding` and `update_finding` inject their action (`store`, `update`) only when the caller sent none, and an explicit `action` wins. The `/mcp/` schemas of these aliases do not declare `action`, but REST and stdio pass it through, so a call such as `update_finding(action="details", discovery_id=...)` runs that other `knowledge` action. The envelope used to treat the result as an update ack: it dropped the payload the caller asked for and pointed them at the details read they had just made. The omission now applies only when the resolved action is the alias's own, and any other action's response keeps its payload under `raw_governance` with no omission hint. Its `next_action` names the `knowledge` action that ran instead of reporting it as the alias's own store or update, makes no claim about whether that action wrote, and says to read the response before repeating the call. Second, the `record_result` hint said an outcome's full payload "cannot be read again". That is true only for an outcome recorded without a `prediction_id`. A prediction-bound outcome is idempotent, and its digest does not cover `response_mode` or `include_semantics`, so repeating the identical call with `response_mode="full"` returns the stored outcome in full (`idempotent_replay: true`) and records no second outcome while the binding is retained. The hint for a prediction-bound outcome now names that route, and says that a changed outcome is refused with `PREDICTION_REUSE_CONFLICT`. It also keeps the warning that a repeat without a `prediction_id` records a second outcome. The hint for an unbound outcome is unchanged. The integration guide, the manual and the governance-lifecycle skill say the same. The acks still do not set `raw_governance_available`, because no read returns the omitted payload. No input schema changes, so the interface contract version and digests are unchanged. The replay is idempotent only under the same identity: the identical call must be repeated by the same agent, passing its `client_session_id`, and the hint, the integration guide, the manual and the skills now say so (#2487) (#2457, #2462).
- **dialectic:** the auto-resolve sweeper now gives a SYNTHESIS stall where the move is its reviewer's (its first synthesis verdict is still owed, or the paused agent has answered its standing objection) the same 4-hour `awaiting_facilitation` window an ANTITHESIS stall gets. It reads the transcript to decide, because at SYNTHESIS the flag means the reviewer owes reconsideration and arms `check_timeout` auto-replacement of a stuck reviewer; a stall where the paused agent owes the move when first swept raises no request and is reaped at the stuck threshold as before. The check runs when the request is raised. Nothing clears the flag when the reviewer later answers (already true of the standing-objection flag), so a row flagged while the reviewer owed the move keeps the 4h hold, and `check_timeout` can still replace a paused or missing reviewer, after the move passes back to the paused agent. The rescue branch was gated on the ANTITHESIS phase, so a reviewer that delivered a verdict and then went silent left a SYNTHESIS row the sweeper never looked at: it fell straight through to FAILED at the 2-hour stuck threshold while the operator escape (`reassign`, which admits any phase once the flag is set; inside the window the session stays in SYNTHESIS and the replacement submits synthesis, and only a refused self-review or an already-reaped row is rewound) was never offered. The sweeper raises the request and stops; it does not pick a replacement reviewer at SYNTHESIS, because the protocol requires the same reviewer to revise its own verdict and a sweeper-chosen substitute would write a completed review no reviewer completed. Once raised, the flag lets `get(check_timeout=true)` replace a reviewer that is paused or missing, as a standing objection's flag already does; a reviewer whose stored status reads active is left for an operator `reassign`. The shared facilitation write path (sibling to the #1893 ANTITHESIS entry) is hoisted out of the phase branch so both phases use one copy; the ANTITHESIS behaviour, writes, events and counts are unchanged, only its sweeper log line now prints the reason token. Disclosed cost: a SYNTHESIS session whose reviewer went silent is now held 4h before FAILED instead of 2h, and a SYNTHESIS stall costs one transcript read on the sweep that first finds it (it is then either flagged or reaped). Reporting shifts too: such a session, once flagged and reaped at 4h, is classified `UNRESOLVED_AWAITING_FACILITATION` by `dialectic_outcomes` (no reviewer synthesis means `standing_rejection` is unknown, so the flag decides) where it was `FAILED` before, and `resolution_rate` excludes it from both terms, as it already does for a flagged ANTITHESIS stall. The read-then-write window on `mark_awaiting_facilitation` (inherited from #1893, now covering both phases) is tracked in #2367. (#2361)
- **identity lineage:** onboarding no longer reports or records lineage the caller never declared. With no `parent_agent_id` and no `spawn_reason`, `infer_spawn_reason` returned `compaction` for any Claude Code client hint once the thread held earlier nodes. That fork was then classified `identity_lineage`, persisted with `spawn_reason="compaction"`, stamped into later knowledge-graph write provenance, and answered with "Declared lineage for this fork is already recorded; do not redeclare it", in the same response that said `lineage_state: no_lineage_declared`. A thread derived from an IP:UA fingerprint is shared by every unrelated session behind one egress address and client, so on cloud-hosted harnesses the "predecessor" was an unrelated process. Inference now returns `new_session` whenever no parent is declared, so the fork is a `sibling_locus` and the response says co-location does not establish lineage.
