A declared parent still infers `compaction` or `subagent` as before. The onboard welcome line now calls an earlier co-located node "an earlier node in this thread" and names a predecessor only for a lineage fork. A fresh mint on a co-occupied thread (`sibling_locus`) now gets its own honest message at onboard; the R6 text it used to get said "no child UUID minted", which is false for a `force_new` mint (#2370).
- **server instructions:** the MCP `instructions` string now opens the workflow with reading: `search_shared_memory` works before `start_session`, so a first-contact agent learns what it can get before what it must do. The check-in paragraph names the three policy actions and explains a pause where it is first mentioned (as shipped after #2408: a pause holds governed writes until `self_recovery` (not at high risk), dialectic, an operator or a safety net lifts it, or it expires; proceed and guide are advisory), and says that lineage exists only when declared. Every phrase the positioning lint pins is unchanged (#2370, #2408).
- **verification floor shadow:** the shadow now leaves a durable record, with a denominator. `GOVERNANCE_VERIFICATION_FLOOR_SHADOW` defaults to true while the floor itself defaults to false, so the harm-confession signal has been computed on every check-in of every deployment that did not opt out — and persisted nowhere. A firing was written onto the dict `process_update` returns and onto `_last_governance_result`, and that was the whole lifecycle: no table, no column, no migration, no aggregator, and one in-process attribute read in `dialectic/handlers.py`. Every persistence writer on the check-in path is key-selective (`agent_storage`'s 15 named columns, the `state_json` blob's explicit assignments, the EISV telemetry envelope's keyword-only builder, each typed `log_*` audit method, the Redis flags, the WebSocket broadcast's 14 keys, the export's monitor histories), so the key was dropped by all of them; it left the process only in a `response_mode="full"` body, which is not a record. The 2.16.0 entry above says shadow mode exists "so live traffic accumulates the false-positive/recall record the enable decision requires" — only the surfacing half of that was built. Asked how often the floor would have fired, the honest answer was neither a number nor zero: the producer ran and nothing recorded it. `src/verification_floor_shadow.py` writes each evaluation to `audit.events` as `verification_floor_shadow`, the same sink and monthly-partition retention as `grounding_shadow` and `coherence_gate_shadow` (no migration — the table is generic and already indexed on `(event_type, ts DESC)`). The shape follows the question the enable decision actually asks, which is a rate: the default record mode writes a row for **every** evaluation rather than only the would-fires, because a numerator alone cannot produce one, and the mode is stamped on each row so a later reader can tell "no non-firings occurred" from "this deployment was configured not to write them" (`GOVERNANCE_VERIFICATION_FLOOR_SHADOW_RECORD=all|firings|off`; an unrecognised value falls back to `all` rather than to silence). Each row carries the pre-floor verdict/risk pair, the pair `apply_verification_floor` produced from it through the same pure function the floor itself uses, the action the deployment actually took, the fired categories and capped matched spans, and the shape of the input. The sink records under **both** flag states — `applied` distinguishes a shadow row from an enforced one — because recording only the shadow would recreate the same hole one flag flip later and would leave "no rows against live traffic" permanently ambiguous. Input shape is a stratum, never an eligibility filter: the detector reads English verb-object prose, but a first-person pronoun is neither necessary nor sufficient (`"Disabled telemetry for the run; deleted snapshots afterwards."` scores 0.7975 / high-risk with no pronoun), so every rate draws its numerator and denominator from the same stratum and none can exceed 1. Simulation and applied rows are labelled and never pooled into the pending-decision rate. `scripts/analysis/verification_floor_shadow_read.py` reports the rate over scoreable shadow rows, breaks it down by stratum, and refuses to divide when the window has no denominator, naming the four states a zero can be in instead. The cost is stated on both axes: one audit row per check-in, and a second synchronous JSONL `fsync` on the check-in path beside the `auto_attest` row already written there (`UNITARES_AUDIT_WRITE_JSONL=0` removes both). Measurement only: no verdict, risk, decision, threshold or flag changes, the in-band `verification_floor_shadow` result key keeps its firings-only semantics, and the sink fails open on the mandatory check-in path. This does not enable the floor or discharge its gate — the owners' sign-off and the safety-envelope review are untouched; it makes one clause of one bullet answerable with a number. The dormant-capability registry (Theme 6) now carries the verification floor and its wake condition (the owners' sign-off, a safety-envelope review and a false-positive regression pass), and records that shadow readings from before this change were not recorded rather than zero. Which reading of that enable gate is correct is itself open: the proposal, its index row, the dormant-capability registry and the shadow-read report now say enabling is gated and that what satisfies the gate is open as D2 in the open-decisions packet (#2308, #2312, #2359).
- **compatibility notes and install docs:** six claims that source contradicts. `COMPATIBILITY.md` said v2.23.0 "preserves registered callable names, input schemas, and lifecycle envelopes; no database migration is introduced"; all three clauses are false. `direct_resume_if_safe` is removed with no alias (#2093), `dialectic` drops `vote` from its wire schema (#2103), and `db/postgres/migrations/070_outcome_prediction_bindings.sql` is introduced (#2246) — the server never applies migrations itself, so until it lands an `outcome_event` / `record_result` carrying `prediction_id` fails with `DB_ERROR` while calls without one are unaffected, and `scripts/ops/deploy-mcp.sh` refuses to restart across the gap. The bullet now names each break and both apply commands, and scopes its "no removals" clause to the default and lite discovery profiles, which is where it holds. `constraints.txt` (twice) and `CONTRIBUTING.md` called the `mcp-newest` lane advisory; it has been blocking since a2a66350 (#1908). `constraints.txt` also still described the pre-#2050 world in which an env carrying mcp 2.x failed to collect the suite — the pin is now `mcp==2.2.0`, on which collection is clean, and the hazard inverted into a stale venv quietly keeping a newer in-range release, since a constraint never downgrades. `docs/install/PLAYBOOK.md` named "the pinned 1.29.0"; the replacement cites no literal version and points at the `mcp==` line. `get_tool_usage_stats`' served description showed `least_used` carrying `{"calls": 0}`, a row neither producer can emit: `audit_db.py` and `tool_usage_tracker.py` both slice `sorted_tools[-10:]` off a `GROUP BY` that emits no group for a tool with no recorded row, so a zero-call tool is absent from the payload entirely. The dormant-capability registry's BEAM Agent Orchestrator row still read "nothing spawns through it yet — Wave-3b frontier" after the 2026-06-24 Wave-3 gate closed Decision A GO/DONE with a named live consumer (Wave 3b is the identity-middleware handler port), and its dialectic review-request row still said to re-measure "after #1414 lands", which landed in #1420 with cross-transport enforcement in #2232; that row now records adoption as *not recorded* rather than zero, and anchors on `resolve_dialectic_agent_id` instead of a drifted line range. No runtime behavior, schema, digest, or interface release moves: the contract hashes input schemas, not tool-level descriptions, and the regenerated artifact is byte-identical. (#2282)
- **resident progress:** several residents may now name the same progress `source`. The probe fetched once per `(source, window)` pair but stored each result under the source name alone. For two residents on one source with different windows, the later result replaced the earlier one. A resident whose result was replaced then read 0, which below its threshold made it a false `progress_flat_candidate`. An error in either fetch also marked every resident on that source `source_error`. `probe_task.py` avoided this by raising `RuntimeError` at import when a source appeared twice. The server imports that module lazily inside the supervised probe task, so a manifest with a shared source passed config load and server start. After the restart, progress probing stopped for every resident, and the only trace was a background-task crash in the log. Results and errors are now keyed on the `(source, window)` pair, and the import-time check is removed. A server without this change still crashes on a shared source, so update the server before adding one. `docs/operations/resident-roster.md` documents the sharing rule. Tests cover separate counts per window, error isolation between windows, a fresh-interpreter import of a manifest that shares a source, and a tick with no manifest. (#2286)
- **resident progress:** one malformed manifest entry no longer takes down progress probing for the whole fleet. `parse_resident_progress_manifest` built every entry unguarded, so a missing `source` / `metric` / `window_seconds` / `threshold` raised `KeyError`, `"window_seconds": "soon"` raised `ValueError`, `"threshold": null` raised `TypeError`, and `"expected_cadence_s": 0` raised `ValueError` from `ResidentConfig`'s own guard — each out of the module import. A top-level JSON array raised `AttributeError` through the same escape, since a bare array is valid JSON and never reaches the `JSONDecodeError` arm. Every importer imports this module lazily from inside an already-running server (`progress_flat_probe_task`, `/v1/progress_flat/recent`, the silence detector's event-driven check), so the raise could not fail the start it would need to fail: a one-entry typo passed config load and server start, then stopped probing for every resident with a background-task crash line as its only trace. The blast radius exceeded the probe. `_get_expected_interval` consults `is_event_driven_label` first inside `try/except Exception: pass`, which swallowed the import crash and fell through to the cadence fallbacks — measured in a fresh interpreter, an event-driven resident carrying an `autonomous` tag went from exempt (`None`) to a 300s expected interval, and the silence detector escalates `lifecycle_silent_critical` at 5x that, so one unrelated typo turned a quiet-but-healthy resident into a recurring false critical page. A malformed entry is now skipped with a WARNING naming the label and the error, and the rest of the roster loads, matching how the loader already degrades on a bad file; failing loudly at server start instead is the stated alternative, and needs an eager validation call site outside this lazily imported module. `ResidentConfig` still raises, so the skip is the manifest parser's policy for untrusted deployment config rather than a relaxation of the dataclass guard. `source` and `metric` are now checked for stringness, which nothing downstream did: the probe groups fetches on `(source, window)` tuples, so a nested object as a source is unhashable and raised inside `tick()`. A non-object top level loads as empty, and a wholly-rejected manifest logs a rollup line, because an empty registry probes nobody and "no manifest configured" is the user-agnostic default — without it the two empties read identically. `docs/operations/resident-roster.md` documents the skip rule. Fifteen new tests cover each malformed kind, per-entry warnings, the all-skipped rollup, non-object manifests, a fresh-interpreter import, and the event-driven exemption surviving a malformed sibling; all fifteen fail against the previous parser. (#2304)
- **check-in reply:** `process_agent_update` / `sync_state` no longer advertises a `prediction_id` the server has already consumed. The reply surfaces the monitor's last-minted prediction, and the Phase-5 evidence emitter (`UNITARES_PHASE5_EVIDENCE_WRITE`) minted one prediction per `recent_tool_results` row after the check-in had minted its own, overwriting that pointer and claiming each id at once through the exactly-once binding (#2246; shadow rows included). A caller that passed `recent_tool_results` was therefore handed the last evidence row's id, its later `record_result(prediction_id=...)` was refused with `PREDICTION_REUSE_CONFLICT` naming the emitter's row, and the check-in's own prediction expired unadvertised. Observed live on this deployment 2026-09-16. `register_tactical_prediction` gains `advertise` (default true); the emitter mints with `advertise=False`, so the reply carries the check-in's prediction and each evidence row still binds to its own. `tests/test_phase5_prediction_not_advertised.py` runs the real mint against the emitter and the reply builder (#2259).
- **outcome persistence:** prediction-bound `outcome_event` submissions are exactly-once per `(agent_id, prediction_id)` while its binding is retained. The database claim and canonical outcome insert commit atomically; within that retention window, identical retries return the canonical existing outcome and conflicting reuse returns `PREDICTION_REUSE_CONFLICT` with its ID. The unpartitioned claim ledger expires with, never after, the canonical outcome; an expired prediction ID can then establish a new canonical submission. Creating acknowledgements, calibration, persisted rows, and replay all use the database-canonical score. Calibration delivery remains a post-commit in-process side effect: an ambiguous commit can persist the outcome while delivering calibration zero times, tracked separately in #2247 rather than hidden by an outbox in this change. (#2246)
- **REST boundary:** `/v1/tools/call` now removes caller-supplied reserved dispatch keys (`params_step._VALIDATION_CONTEXT_KEYS`), including inside a `kwargs` wrapper, before prebind, the strict gate, the Wave-3a BEAM proxy, the direct handlers and the fallback, as the MCP pipeline already did. Only dispatch middleware writes these keys. `params_step.remove_reserved_dispatch_keys` is the shared step; `tests/test_rest_forged_dispatch_handoff.py` pins it on every REST route. Over `/mcp/`, a `kwargs` string that the JSON decoder refuses (an oversized integer or deeply nested input) or an unexpected sanitizer fault now returns a typed, audited error envelope instead of raising before the wrapper's error boundary (#2233).
- **dialectic writes:** thesis, antithesis and synthesis now require a caller bound by identity resolution and submitting as the identity it names (`dialectic/auth.py` `require_bound_caller`, `caller_is_bound_as`). The participant allow-list checks which identity is named, and the session-ownership check ran only when a binding existed; neither establishes who is calling. On installs not running `UNITARES_IDENTITY_STRICT=strict`, binding itself is not proof-enforced, so this check is only as strong as that binding. `tests/test_dialectic_synthesis_bound_caller.py` covers it (#2232).
- **impersonation guard, teaching:** three server surfaces taught calls that `inject_identity`'s impersonation guard refuses for a bound session: the observe router's example `observe(action='agent', agent_id=...)`, the `observe_agent` migration note, and the guard's own recovery text, which promised `knowledge(action='search', agent_id=X)` was unrestricted. The first two now teach `target_agent_id`, which `observe` has taken since 2.6.1, when this same "Session mismatch" was fixed by that rename. The recovery text and `read_path` now name `observe(action='agent', target_agent_id=...)`, say that a knowledge search without `agent_id` covers every agent's entries, and warn that removing `agent_id` from an observe call makes it a self-observe. The guard itself is unchanged. The 2026-09-13 council on identity posture decided against name exemptions: in the dialectic phases `agent_id` names the actor, so an exemption would let a bound caller submit as another participant. `tests/test_identity_guard_teaching.py` pins these points: (#2234).
  - every router example and alias migration note that passes `agent_id` passes the guard for another agent's id;
  - the recovery text's `read_path` passes;
  - dialectic thesis, antithesis, synthesis and request with another agent's id are refused;
  - aliases of onboard/identity do not take middleware PATH 0 (extending it to aliases is an identity-posture decision);
  - session injection stays at its eight tools.

  The F5 section of `docs/operations/tool-surface-audit-2026-09-12.md` gains a dated correction: injection changes which session key a call binds to, not only audit attribution. Five skills that cite `tool_stability.py` or `consolidated.py` are re-stamped after re-checking their claims (none teaches the changed example), and the skills manifest is regenerated.
- **call resolver:** `op` now names an action only on a tool whose handler reads it. `action_router` generates a router that reads `action or op`. A tool that declares `known_actions` by hand routes on `action` alone: `self_recovery` reads `arguments.get("action", "check")` and has no `op` parameter, and `cirs_protocol`'s sub-handlers read `action`. The shared resolver read `op` for every tool, so `self_recovery(op="quick")` was named `quick` in identity classification, stakes and `audit.tool_usage`, while `check` ran. `ToolDefinition.reads_op_as_action` is set by `action_router` only, and the resolver consults it. Nothing changes for an `action_router` tool or an explicit `action`. `tests/test_resolver_op_synonym.py` checks three things: the flag sits exactly on the routers; `op` selects an action only where the flag is set; and no hand-declared action tool advertises an `op` parameter, which is what makes ignoring it correct. `tests/test_router_action_fields.py` also now holds every router's advertised `action` enum to exactly the actions it routes and rejects non-canonical routing-table keys: a routed action the enum omits is refused by validation before dispatch, and an advertised value that does not route always fails with `Unknown action` (#2195, #2236).
- **adoption KPI:** `scripts/dev/adoption_kpi.py` counts the tool a call dispatched to, not the name the caller typed. `audit.tool_usage.tool_name` records the invoked name, so the workflow names agents are taught fell outside every name-keyed filter: `sync_state` reaches `process_agent_update`, `record_result` reaches `outcome_event`, and `store_finding` / `update_finding` / `search_shared_memory` reach `knowledge`. Over the 14 days before 2026-09-13, the check-in concentration and ceremonial-conversion filters missed 1,696 of 8,050 check-ins. The outcome pipe-health figure read zero rows, because all 370 outcome calls arrived as `record_result`. On the measurement-authority taxonomy that zero was state 3, not recorded: the instrument could not see the name callers use. Every filter except `surface_return_rate` now matches `coalesce(payload->>'canonical_tool', tool_name)`. The recorder has written `canonical_tool` on aliased rows since #1424 (2026-07-31), the same instant MCP traffic began recording at all. `surface_return_rate` keeps invoked names on purpose, and says why: a workflow name marks an agent's own call, while its dispatched tool also carries polling and housekeeping traffic that metric excludes. Each changed figure keeps its name-only count beside it (`invoked_name_total`, `ceremonial_converted_invoked_name`). Expect a step-change up at this date: the instrument is being fixed, not usage rising. `tests/test_adoption_kpi_tool_names.py` now requires every non-exempt filter to use the dispatched expression and name no alias, and pins the alias targets those filters depend on (#2237).
- **identity middleware:** the MCP dispatch middleware now judges an aliased call the same way the REST gate does. The two #425 gates share one alias-aware resolver, `get_call_identity_requirement`, which applies an alias's `inject_action` only when handed the name the caller invoked. `resolve_identity` runs before `resolve_alias`, so the action is not yet in the arguments, and the middleware handed the resolver the already-canonicalized tool name. The resolver then fell back to the router's `default_action`, and 22 of the 70 aliases were classified the opposite way from REST. Thirteen read aliases were treated as identity-requiring writes: `search_shared_memory`, `list_agents`, `get_server_info`, `observe_agent`, `detect_anomalies`, `aggregate_metrics`, `compare_agents`, `compare_me_to_similar`, `get_agent_metadata`, `get_knowledge_graph`, `list_knowledge_graph`, `get_discovery_details` and `get_lifecycle_stats`. With no proof they ran resolution. On a session miss they took the auto-mint retry, or the strict refusal. Nine write aliases were treated as unbound reads, so they skipped resolution and the strict refusal: `request_review`, `request_dialectic_review`, `submit_thesis`, `submit_antithesis`, `submit_synthesis`, `reassign_reviewer`, `update_calibration_ground_truth`, `backfill_calibration_from_dialectic` and `rebuild_calibration`. On `/mcp/` the affected names are the two workflow aliases, `search_shared_memory` and `request_review`; stdio resolves every alias, so all 22 were affected there. REST was already correct. The divergence dates from the action-level fold (#611), which introduced the call-granular resolver while this call site kept passing the canonical name. `tests/test_identity_gate_transport_parity.py` runs the real middleware step over every registered tool and every alias. For the proofless case it checks the middleware against the shared resolver, and for a strict session miss it checks it against the REST gate; the roster is read from the registries, so a new alias or action is covered without editing the test. REST prebind had the same defect, and it is fixed here too. Its skip list names canonical tools (`onboard`, `identity`, `bind_session`, ...) but was matched against the invoked name, so the twelve aliases of `onboard` and `identity`, `start_session` among them, resolved an identity before dispatch that their target tools never resolve. With a sticky-cache hit, `start_session(force_new=true)` was pre-bound to the cached agent. The skip now also matches on the alias's canonical tool; no name that skipped before stops skipping. The shared resolver also now follows dispatch's precedence for an alias. `resolve_alias` injects the alias's action whenever the caller sent no `action` key, and the router reads `action` before `op`. The resolver read `action or op` first, so `store_finding(op="search")` was judged as a knowledge search, a `pre_onboard` read, while dispatch ran the store. A proofless call therefore skipped identity resolution and, under strict identity, the typed refusal. The test now runs the real alias step and the router's action read for every alias of an action router across six argument shapes, and checks that the resolver names the same `(tool, action)`. Stakes and `audit.tool_usage` action attribution read the same resolver and move with it (#2196).
- **tool-name sets:** behavior keyed on which tool a call reaches is now declared as a `CallSet` (`src/tool_call_sets.py`) and matched on the resolved call, not tested as a bare string. During dispatch a call is named three ways: the invoked name, which may be an alias; the registered tool it resolves to; and the router action. A bare set holds one of those without saying which, and ten sets had drifted without an error (#2199).
  - Sets consulted with a resolved name carried 29 names that never reached them: 16 across the `inject_identity` and rate-limit sets, and 13 in registrar session injection. They were pre-consolidation names such as `submit_thesis`, `observe_agent`, `store_knowledge_graph` and `get_server_info`, plus `query_knowledge_graph` and `ping_agent`, which nothing registers.
  - Sets consulted with the invoked name (the presence lease, minted-identity attribution, the stdio heartbeat) named legacy names but not the workflow names agents are taught.
  - **Unchanged:** agent_id injection and the impersonation exemption in `inject_identity`, the rate-limit read exemption, and registrar session injection. A differential check over every registered tool and action at the point each set is consulted finds no membership change. Two nuances:
    - The knowledge browse exemption now accepts `op` and a mixed-case action, as the router does.
    - The dropped impersonation exemptions for dialectic phases and `observe(action='agent')` never applied after the router consolidation. Whether they should is left open.
  - **Changed, so that an alias behaves as its tool:**
    - The `agent:/` presence lease now refreshes for `store_finding`, `update_finding`, `search_shared_memory`, `record_result` and 14 legacy knowledge/observe names.
    - Audit attribution of a minted identity now covers `init`, `login`, `register` and `start` alongside `onboard` and `start_session`.
    - The stdio auto-heartbeat treats `sync_state`, `request_review`, `checkin` / `log` / `update`, `reassign_reviewer`, `list_dialectic_sessions`, `find_similar_discoveries_graph` and `knowledge(action=details|get|list)` as lightweight, as their tools are. `reply_to_question`, a `knowledge` store, no longer is.
  - `TOOLS_NEEDING_SESSION_INJECTION` drops its 13 unread names. Which routers should be injected remains the open decision of tool-surface audit F5.
  - **Guards:** `tests/test_tool_call_sets.py` fails on an alias, an unregistered tool or an unrouted action in any declared set. `tests/test_no_bare_tool_name_sets.py` fails on a literal table of tool names in `src/` that is neither a `call_set(...)` nor listed with the name it is keyed on and why. `docs/dev/TOOL_REGISTRATION.md` documents both.
- **doc tool-count guard:** `update_docs_tool_count.py` compared every count in the guarded docs against the registry count (42) only. The full-mode `tools/list` catalog (50: the registry plus the eight workflow aliases) could not be written in any guarded shape without failing the guard, and a count in any other shape matched nothing and passed. PR #2129 wrote the figure as a bare `**50**`, which silently took `README.md` out of enforcement. The README has carried no count at all since #2137, so since then the step has checked only `docs/EVIDENCE_AND_LIMITS.md`, with no warning. The guard now checks two quantities, each against its own source: `NN registered tools` (and the older `**NN tools**` shapes) against the registry, and `NN advertised tools` against the new `count_tools.resolve_advertised_tool_count()`, which keeps the same available-or-not rule. Each guarded file declares the quantities it must state, and a missing one, or a missing file, fails. A file with no recognised count is reported as unenforced instead of passing without comment. An unavailable count still fails under `--require-registry` and is still never written by `--update` (#2238).
- **tool_usage telemetry:** `cirs_protocol` declares its sub-action vocabulary (`emit`, `query`, `compute`, `set`, `get`, `list`, `initiate`, `respond`, `status`), so a call records which action it used instead of dropping it. The tool takes `protocol` and `action`, but its decorator declared no `known_actions`, and `build_tool_usage_payload` treats a registered tool with no vocabulary as single-purpose, so a call *would* have recorded no discriminator (tool-surface audit F4, #2167). On the measurement-authority taxonomy this is state 1/4, never surfaced or genuinely unused, with a **prospective** instrument blind spot, not state 3: `audit.tool_usage` holds no `cirs_protocol` row at all, and the recorder does write a row with an empty payload when a tool has no sub-action, so a call would have left a trace. `docs/operations/dormant-capability-registry.md` already lists these announce handlers as dark and "verify before cut"; that zero is not grounds to retire anything, and the fix stands on the instrument being blind before the first call rather than on any count. The declared set is the union of what the *selectable* protocols route on, held by a test to those handlers' own refusal vocabularies and scoped to `CirsProtocolParams.protocol` rather than to the wider dispatcher table; no `default_action`, because no selectable protocol defaults one (`resonance_alert` does, and is not selectable). No gate, and dispatch and identity are unchanged. **Both dispatch levels are recorded**, because for a two-level tool the sub-action alone is not the discriminator: four protocols route `query` and two route `emit`, so a row carrying only the action could not answer which capability was used. `protocol` joins the payload allowlist, clamped to the schema's own `Literal` or written as `protocol_unlisted`, and the allowlist's rule is restated as what it always was — every recorded value resolves to a bounded server-side vocabulary member or a fixed marker, and `action` is caller-supplied too, so authorship was never the test. **The stakes table is split per action** rather than left under one tool-level `high` key: `query`, `get`, `list` and `status` are reads and become baseline, while `emit`, `compute`, `set`, `initiate` and `respond` write state other agents read and stay high. A tool-level key is honest when a tool's actions are uniform, which is why `self_recovery` keeps one, and stops being honest when they disagree; both #775 coverage guards were previously satisfied by that key's fallback, so nine actions counted as deliberately classified without a classification being made. The gate that consumes the table is still parked, so nothing changes at runtime today. `ACTION_FIELDS` is deliberately **not** declared, because every flat parameter here is scoped by protocol rather than by action, so an action-keyed map describes no single call; the comment on the model records what declaring one produced. The `action` description, which listed seven of the nine, now names which protocol routes which, and the two `protocol` recovery hints derive from the validated list instead of the dispatcher table, so neither names a value validation rejects (#2165). Descriptions live inside the hashed schema, so one `input_schema_sha256` and the catalog's `surface_sha256` move, the negotiated interface release advances to 1.7.0, and the artifact is regenerated; no parameter name, type, default or requiredness changes. (#2172)
- **docs(cirs):** `docs/guides/CIRS_PROTOCOL.md` documented `coherence_report` and `governance_action` as taking `emit`, which both handlers refuse, in the protocol table and in two worked examples. `coherence_report` takes `compute` (pairwise, `target_agent_id` required) or `query`; `governance_action` takes `initiate`, `respond`, `query` or `status`. Corrected with a Contested Claims Registry row in `docs/dev/CANONICAL_SOURCES.md` and a `check_doc_health.py` deny-pattern, per the same-PR rule in `docs/operations/github-workflow-conventions.md` §3. The pattern matches the copyable call form only, not the protocol name near the word `emit`: a proximity pattern would fire both on the corrected prose itself and on the flat action list in the merged `tool-surface-audit-2026-09-12.md`, reddening the doc workflow, which runs `--strict`. (#2172)
- **cirs_protocol:** the wire schema declared three of the twenty-seven parameters its selectable protocol handlers read, and FastMCP's argument model dropped the other twenty-four before dispatch on `/mcp/`: a `void_alert` query ignored `since_hours` and its filters, a `governance_action` respond or status arrived without its `action_id`, and a `boundary_contract` set stored the defaults whatever the caller sent. REST and in-process callers were unaffected, because the middleware merges undeclared keys back (the `observe` / `describe_tool` class, interface release 1.2.0). All twenty-four are now declared, each with the handler's own default and a description naming the protocol and action that read it: `since_hours`, `filter_agent_id`, `filter_severity` and `context_ref` for `void_alert`; `include_trajectory`, `agent_ids`, `regime`, `max_risk` and `min_coherence` for `state_announce`; `source_agent_id` and `min_similarity` for `coherence_report`; `trust_default`, `trust_overrides`, `void_response_policy`, `max_delegation_complexity` and `accept_coherence_threshold` for `boundary_contract`; `action_type`, `payload`, `action_id`, `accept`, `response_data`, `as_initiator`, `as_target` and `status_filter` for `governance_action`. `ACTION_FIELDS` stays undeclared, for the reason #2172 recorded. Verifying the drop found a second defect of the same class, on every transport: the middleware hands a handler every declared field, `None` included, and the three query handlers did `int(arguments.get("limit", 50))` on the `None` the declared `limit` produced, so every `query` on `void_alert`, `state_announce` and `coherence_report` failed with a `TypeError` through dispatch while the handler tests, which call the handlers directly, passed. `limit` now advertises and delivers the handlers' default of 50, and `severity`, `filter_severity` and `action_type` are read None-tolerantly so their structured refusals fire instead of an `AttributeError`. The guide taught `boundary_contract set` with `trust_level` and `void_policy`, keys no handler reads, so an agent following it to give a peer trust `none` stored `partial` on every transport; the example now names the real parameters, each protocol section lists its filters, and the correction carries a Contested Claims Registry row and an assignment-form deny-pattern with tests. The dormant-capability registry's CIRS row now records that its zero predates any call, and that verification has to run against the repaired surface. Review found two more defects, both present on master and now fixed. An action-less call on any selectable protocol raised an `AttributeError` on the `None` the middleware materializes for `action`, instead of returning that protocol's `valid_actions`. And `status_filter` was matched case-sensitively and never validated, so `"PENDING"` or a typo returned an empty success; it is now lowercased, and an unknown status is refused. `tests/test_cirs_protocol_wire_params.py` reads the keys each selectable handler reaches from its source, holds the schema and its defaults to them, and refuses a read it cannot follow, such as a non-literal key or the argument dict passed to an unreviewed callee; it also exercises the registered FastMCP tool end to end. `resonance_alert` and `stability_restored` stay refused by the `protocol` Literal: admitting them is a reachability decision this change does not make. Declaring the parameters changes what REST and in-process callers get, because they used to receive these keys raw. String booleans are now parsed, so `accept="false"`, which used to accept an action because the string is truthy, now rejects it. An explicit null for a parameter that has a default, a non-string `agent_ids` item, or a non-object `payload` is refused by validation instead of reaching the handler; on `/mcp/` the typed wrapper already treated a null as omitted. No advertised parameter is removed or renamed, and the one retyped, `limit`, stops admitting a null that always failed at `int()`, so the release is classified minor, as #2100 was when it declared `observe`'s `include_calibration` as a strict boolean over the same kind of raw read: one `input_schema_sha256` moves, the negotiated interface release advances to 1.8.0, and the artifact is regenerated. (#2183)
- **embeddings:** `similarity()` and `rank_by_similarity()` now compute over already-materialized vectors without requiring the optional sentence-transformers runtime. NumPy remains a core dependency; producing embeddings through `embed()` / `embed_batch()` still requires the model runtime. (#2101)

- **container publication:** publish only the versioned image; keep `latest` unchanged until the verified multi-architecture index digest is explicitly promoted without rebuilding. Remove the publication-time promotion switch. (#2109)
- **release compatibility:** #2092 merged after the v2.22.0 tag. Its Compose forwarding, Docker MCP checks, publication-pin separation, and corrected upgrade guidance are post-release fixes, with a narrow v2.22.1 maintenance backport being prepared. The v2.22.0 entry below again describes only the tagged contents; see its errata. (#2092, #2105)

- **observe / describe_tool:** four parameters two handlers read were not declared on their wire schemas, so FastMCP dropped them before dispatch and the call silently used the default over `/mcp/` (REST and in-process callers were unaffected, because the middleware merges undeclared keys back). `observe(action="telemetry")` now declares `window_hours` and `include_calibration`, with the handler's 24h default mirrored in the schema; `describe_tool` declares `include_schema` and `include_full_description`. Both are additive, so the negotiated interface release advances to 1.2.0 and the contract artifact is regenerated; a regression test checks each case on the registered FastMCP argument model. (#2100)
- **class-calibration overlay:** a malformed `UNITARES_CLASS_CALIBRATION` overlay no longer stops the governance server from starting. `_apply_class_calibration_overlay` runs when `config/governance_config.py` is imported, which is on the server's eager import path, and its docstring promised that a malformed file was a silent no-op. Three shapes raised instead. A section that was not an object (`{"label_intervals": ["resident-a", 1800]}`, `{"delta_norm_max": 0.5}`) raised `AttributeError`, because each section was read as `(data.get(section) or {}).items()` outside the per-entry guard. An entry that overflowed raised `OverflowError`, which is not a `ValueError` and was not caught: `Infinity` or `1e999` as a label interval, or an integer beyond the float range in a float section. Valid JSON nested past the decoder's recursion limit raised `RecursionError` past the file-level catch in the same way. Each bad file, section or entry is now skipped with a WARNING naming it, as #2304 does for resident-progress manifest entries, and the rest of the overlay still applies. The skips log because a skipped section leaves the same defaults in place as an absent one, so a silent skip cannot be told apart from no calibration at all. For the same reason a set variable naming a missing, unreadable or non-object file now warns, as `load_resident_progress_registry` does for its manifest. An unset variable stays silent. The docstring schema now lists `label_intervals`, which the function already read. Reading is still unbounded, as the docstring now says: a path whose read never finishes, such as a FIFO with no writer or one whose writer never closes it, stalls the import. `tests/test_class_calibration_overlay.py` imports the module in a fresh interpreter for each section set to a list, a number and a string, for an overflowing entry in each section, and for a too-deeply-nested file. It also pins every error class the entry and file guards catch, since all four sections now share one guard and narrowing it would otherwise pass unnoticed. The tests assert that the well-formed sections still land and that each warning names what it skipped. 48 of its 52 tests fail against the previous function; the other four are negative controls that stay silent on both. (#2309)
- **resident progress:** an overflowing manifest value or an unparseable manifest no longer crashes the registry import. The per-entry guard from #2304 caught `KeyError`, `ValueError` and `TypeError`, and `OverflowError` is none of them, so `Infinity` or `1e999` as `window_seconds`, `threshold` or `expected_cadence_s`, or a `window_seconds` past `timedelta`'s range, still raised out of the import. The loader caught `JSONDecodeError` and `OSError`, so a manifest that was not UTF-8, held an integer past the 4,300-digit limit, or was nested past the JSON decoder's recursion limit raised too. The registry is imported lazily inside the running server, so each of these stopped progress probing for every resident. With one `Infinity` sibling, an event-driven resident's expected interval also went from exempt to 300 s, reopening the false-critical-page path #2304 closed for the entries it listed. `OverflowError` now joins the entry guard, and the file-level catch is `(OSError, ValueError, RecursionError)`, with a warning that names the error type. The loader docstring and `docs/operations/resident-roster.md` state the skip rule and that reading is unbounded. Ten new tests cover the overflow entries, four unparseable manifests and two fresh-interpreter imports; all ten fail against the previous module. (#2310)
- **lease plane (effect recovery):** recovery no longer reports an effect as committed forward, tombstoned or quarantined unless the store returned an explicit `:ok`. A store error now yields an unresolved persistence failure, an exception during reconciliation counts as unresolved rather than quarantined, and scan logs say how many orphans were scanned instead of claiming all were drained. Successful return shapes are unchanged and recovery never rewrites file bytes; this does not independently verify the store's acknowledgement or establish exactly-once execution (#2084).
- **doctor (`adjudication_feedstock`):** the check built its eligibility query from the global `high`/`critical` severity set and never used the per-family override it defines, so every `doctor_check_finding` (emitted at `warning`, which the adjudication queue admits) counted as ineligible and the check reported the queue DRY when it was not. It now applies the override and judges each producer family separately: a family at or above the alive floor with zero eligible findings is named even while another family keeps the pooled total above zero, so a doctor finding cannot clear the check that raised it. The doctor is a read-only diagnostic; what enters the queue does not change (#2088).
- **check-ins, state reads and recovery (dogfood fixes):** a check-in that omits `confidence` no longer mints a tactical prediction or records a calibration observation from the server's derived estimate; earlier explicit predictions stay open for their outcomes, and `simulate_update` restores prediction bookkeeping and writes no trajectory calibration observations. The default state read keeps the resolved verdict and action, lite risk badges use the published `RISK_APPROVE_THRESHOLD` / `RISK_REVISE_THRESHOLD` instead of hardcoded 0.5 / 0.75, the borderline reflection names the dimension and threshold instead of a basin, and nullable enum choices survive FastMCP schema reconstruction. The read-only recovery check reports `recovery_needed` and `recovery_status` (`not_needed`, `eligible`, `blocked`, `unknown`), so an active identity is no longer told it is eligible to recover. The `standard` profile this widened was later removed by #2137, and an MCP error body can still carry `success: false` with transport `isError: false` (#2111).
- **observe(action='anomalies'):** `limit` is no longer advertised for this action. The handler never read it, so `limit=1` returned every anomaly; it stays accepted and inert rather than paging findings away. The response gains a `scan` block (`selection`, `agents_scanned`, `agents_active`, `scan_cap`, `truncated`, `agents_skipped_no_activity` and the filters in force) with a note when the default scan stopped at `DEFAULT_ANOMALY_SCAN_CAP` (50 active agents), so an empty list no longer reads as fleet-wide; the cap's value is unchanged. The dashboard's Anomalies card reads that block: a truncated zero shows "none found" instead of a green "clear", and the card shows "scanned N of M agents" (#2116, #2121).
- **check-in to outcome loop:** `sync_state` responses carry `prediction_id` as a top-level key again; the experience envelope rebuilt the response without it, leaving it only under `raw_governance` and in `next_action` prose. `record_result` discloses `prediction_binding`, `prediction_source` and `calibration_excluded` in `state_summary`, and `next_action` says when an outcome was excluded from calibration, as one recorded without its check-in's `prediction_id` is (#2124).
- **mcp server version:** `serverInfo.version` in the initialize response carries the `VERSION` file value on stdio and HTTP. It was empty on both mcp majors, so no client could tell two builds apart. The HTTP mount passes it only when the installed SDK accepts the argument (#2126).
- **watcher:** the Watcher detector calls ollama's native `/api/chat` with an explicit context window. The OpenAI-compatible endpoint clamped every prompt to the server default and cut the front, where the rules and pattern library sit, so scans returned no findings while the resident reported healthy. Files are tiled into regions that each fit (`chunk_regions()`), truncation is detected from the backend's own token accounting, and a truncated prompt or unusable model output (`PromptTruncated`, `ModelOutputUnusable`) now escalates on the detector-down counter instead of clearing it; scan and review keep separate counters. `--self-test` adds an at-size case, and `WATCHER_OLLAMA_PROTOCOL` overrides the endpoint choice for proxies (#2128).
- **vigil and SDK `audit_knowledge`:** the Python SDK no longer hardcodes `use_model: "true"` on `audit_knowledge`; `use_model` is opt-in and sent only when set, matching the server's default. The model-written `model_assessment` it paid for had no reader, and the local inference pushed Vigil's auto-archive audit past its 15 s budget every cycle. Vigil's summary now reports the count its archiver can act on (`eligible_for_archive`) instead of pairing the stale and archive-candidate buckets with a lifecycle total (#2131).
- **knowledge graph lifecycle:** the never-auto-archive set named `architecture_decision`, a type no discovery can carry, so architectural decisions (`architectural_decision`) had no retention protection. The spelling is corrected and a test pins every entry against the canonical stored types; `root_cause_analysis` and `migration` stay for direct backend writers. Supersession is now refused only for the permanent tags, not the permanent types, so an architectural decision can still be superseded by its revision; retention keys on both. Permanent rows still always report `healthy` in the audit, which is unchanged (#2133).
- **identity refusals through the envelope:** a typed identity refusal (`status: identity_required`, success-shaped by design) raised inside a handler is no longer rebuilt into an ordinary check-in that told the agent to keep working, and no identity warning is appended saying the call succeeded. The refusal passes through at the top level, where both shipped SDKs look for it, with `tool` rewritten to the alias the caller invoked. Refusals raised before dispatch were never affected (#2135).
- **interface contract and late plugins:** a capability registered after the MCP surface was mounted (a plugin imported late) was published in the interface contract and then refused as `Unknown tool`. `get_public_tool_definitions` now narrows to the mounted table when one exists, so a late registration is not advertised on any route; it is excluded, not mounted. Every direct plugin importer honours `UNITARES_DISABLE_PLUGINS` through `plugins_disabled()` (#2143).
- **tool descriptions and annotations:** every advertised tool's first description line now says what the call does, what makes it fail, what it ignores and which sibling to prefer; five placeholder descriptions and four that taught the wrong call are replaced, and two descriptions from this change that stated the opposite of the server (`describe_tool`'s `lite` default, and which findings `search_shared_memory` excludes) are corrected. A new `src/tool_annotations.py` holds one annotation record per advertised tool: nine tools that advertised `readOnlyHint: true` although a call can write (an unbound call mints an identity; searches append a `knowledge_read` audit event) are corrected, three colliding titles are split, and every action router is marked at least as unsafe as what it dispatches to. `/v1/tools` emits `annotations` beside `function` so the stdio bridge keeps them, and advertised description bytes grow from about 10.5 KB to 27.6 KB (#2148, #2151).
- **doctor (migrations, columns):** `check_schema_migrations` no longer requires the tolerated phantom migration slot 18, so a correctly provisioned fresh database stops reporting `missing 18`; known exceptions decide only what is tolerated. `check_column_drift` fails with an UNKNOWN message when its column lookup fails, where it had skipped every table and passed over zero verified columns, and its PASS counts tables actually resolved. The contributor contract now names the test that gates migrations, and the cross-machine doc's operator-path audit pattern matches any home directory (#2149).
- **dashboard residents:** on an empty resident roster the dashboard no longer renders Watcher, Sentinel and Vigil panels under a `live` badge; each panel is created only when the roster names that resident. A roster-endpoint outage still renders all three (#2150).
- **tool descriptions (alias twins):** the four workflow-alias pairs that reach one handler (`start_session`/`onboard`, `sync_state`/`process_agent_update`, `check_working_state`/`get_governance_metrics`, `record_result`/`outcome_event`) now name each other and state the one difference: the alias wraps the result in the digest envelope, the canonical name returns the handler's raw payload. `process_agent_update.auto_export_on_significance` names the conditions it tests, and the MCP `instructions` catalog paragraph names the tool categories instead of dead `GOVERNANCE_TOOL_MODE` text. Prose only: no schema, handler or annotation change (#2158).
- **gateway (port 8768):** the reduced gateway crash-looped at startup under mcp 2.x because it set `mcp.settings.host` / `.port`, fields 2.x removed; the bind address now goes through a compat seam, `run_server()`, that passes them to `run()` on 2.x. The gateway gets its own transport-security settings (`UNITARES_GATEWAY_ALLOWED_HOSTS`, `UNITARES_GATEWAY_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, `UNITARES_GATEWAY_ALLOW_NULL_ORIGIN`, `UNITARES_GATEWAY_DNS_REBIND_PROTECTION`) instead of inheriting the governance server's, and no configuration reachable from the shipped LaunchAgent template is more permissive than before. An installed plist must be re-rendered from the template to gain the new keys (#2160).
- **health watchdog:** `scripts/ops/health_watchdog.sh` takes its roster from the port registry (`scripts/dev/ports_catalog.py --health-probes`) instead of two hardcoded services, so the gateway and the BEAM services are probed as well. Each entry declares its path and accepted codes (the bearer-gated orchestrator's 401 counts as alive), the governance probe is found by role rather than by port, and an empty roster or one without a governance role logs a FAIL (#2163).
- **tool edge index:** `scripts/dev/tool_edge_index.py` exits 2 (cannot look) and names the module when a dependency is not installed, instead of folding the import failure into findings and reporting a stale index, so the doctor's `tool_edge_index_fresh` reports SKIP rather than FAIL on a core-only install. A missing `src.*` module or a failed name import is still a finding (#2175).
- **list_tools / describe_tool descriptions:** `list_tools` and its lite hints read each advertised name's description from the same definitions `tools/list` serves, so the two discovery surfaces stop disagreeing (28 of the 50 names did after the wire text was rewritten), and `describe_tool` on a workflow alias returns the text the wire serves. `TOOL_DESCRIPTION_OVERRIDES` shrinks to the dispatch-only alias names, which are never on the wire (#2176).
- **list_tools names:** every tool name the `list_tools` payload emits is one the `/mcp/` mount dispatches. The full view's `categories` block is derived from the listed tools (the hand-written one listed 30 dispatch-only twins and omitted every router and workflow alias); the workflow recipes, getting-started steps, `related_to` records, tool map and signatures use call shapes such as `agent(action='list')`; and `relationships` is filtered to listed names. `tests/test_list_tools_names_the_wire.py` holds both views to a fresh mount (#2177).
- **tool registry:** the first-party predicate (`_FIRST_PARTY_ROOTS` in `src/mcp_handlers/decorators.py`) now names every package the wheel ships: `src`, `governance_core` and `config`. `config` joined the wheel before the predicate was written and was left out, so a tool declared under `config.*` would have been treated as a plugin's: exempt from the startup guard that refuses a tool with no `ToolMeta` record, and advertised with an empty stub schema. Such a tool now fails `get_tool_definitions()` as one under `src.*` does. No registered tool changes classification (all are declared under `src.*`); a test requires the tuple to match `[tool.setuptools.packages.find]` in `pyproject.toml` (#2185).
- **tool descriptions:** the served `admin` and `knowledge` descriptions now name every action they route. `admin` describes `connections`, `debug_context`, `workspace_health` and `tool_usage`, and says `telemetry` reads the bound caller's skip rate and confidence over `window_hours` while calibration is excluded unless `include_calibration=true` and is then fleet-wide cumulative state, not a `window_hours` slice. `knowledge` describes `note` and `supersede` (the direction of the link, and that it needs the AGE backend). The description-drift guard drops its admin and knowledge exemptions, and live describe-and-dispatch tests cover the telemetry path. No collector behavior changes (#2224).
- **inference discovery:** `describe_inference_host()` gave every host `invocation.tool = "call_model"`, which is not a valid route for the Codex and Claude host adapters: they accept a `host_id` only from `delegate_inference`, so following it failed closed with `INFERENCE_HOST_UNREACHABLE`. The scalar stays for older clients but is marked `tool_authoritative: false`, and each host now gets `invocation.recommended_tool` derived from its own `accepts_host_id_from` (`delegate_inference` for the host adapters, `call_model` for call-model hosts; never `consult`, which takes no `host_id`). The list response omits it, since it names no single host. The `list_inference_hosts`, `call_model` and `dialectic` descriptions are corrected to match (#2235).
- **server info:** `admin(action="server_info")` / `get_server_info` reports the HTTP and stdio PID marker paths from each transport's actual writer constant, including the HTTP environment override. The legacy `tool_count` keeps its meaning (the dispatch-table snapshot), and a new `tool_counts` block reports `registry`, `workflow_aliases` and `advertised` separately with a `note` naming each population. `advertised` is bounded by the mounted `/mcp/` tool table when a server is running, so a late registry-only plugin cannot make server info claim a capability that is not mounted. The Elixir Wave 3a pass-through carries the new fields (#2239).
- **knowledge search:** `agent_id_filter`, the documented author filter, now takes effect. `search_knowledge_graph` honors it on both the indexed and the substring search paths, preferring it over the legacy `agent_id`, and the search-read event reports the effective filter. It is now declared on `knowledge(action="search")` and `search_shared_memory`, where FastMCP had dropped it before dispatch on `/mcp/`. An explicitly blank filter is refused instead of silently broadening to an unfiltered search, and surrounding whitespace is trimmed; `agent_id` remains the fallback only when the new field is omitted. The interface contract advances to 1.11.0 and the knowledge-graph skill is updated (#2241).
- **onboarding guidance:** a `start_session` / `onboard` envelope for a `sibling_locus` session (a fresh identity on a thread that earlier sessions occupied) no longer tells the agent to declare the prior node's uuid as `parent_agent_id` on its next fresh start. It now says thread co-location does not establish lineage and not to use that uuid as a parent unless a later process is a deliberate continuation after this one exits. A fork whose lineage was already declared is told the lineage is recorded and not to redeclare it (#2245).
- **check-ins:** a `sync_state` that paused now says so in `state_summary` and in its next step. A compact check-in carries its action under `verdict`, which the `state_summary` lift never read, so a pause (for example `sub_action: cirs_block`) returned a `state_summary` with no `action`, and `next_action` told the agent to keep working and check in again. `state_summary` now takes `action` and `sub_action` from the already-built `action_summary` when no direct value exists (an explicit value still wins), and a pause names `self_recovery(action='review', ...)` as the next step. Proceed and guide replies keep their existing continuation text (#2273).
- **resident anchor provisioner:** `scripts/ops/provision_resident_anchor.py` now proves the minted token resumes the identity before it writes an anchor. It compared only the returned UUID, and under the default `UNITARES_IDENTITY_STRICT=log` a resume whose token fails its ownership check still returns that UUID (with `continuity_token_invalid` and `caller_proven: false`), so a signing-secret mismatch passed and surfaced only at the resident's next session; verification now requires a caller-proven or continuity-token resolution and refuses on that warning. `--force` no longer silently repoints an anchor at a different UUID (that needs `--replace-identity`), the anchor is written atomically with private permissions, `--name` is checked against path traversal, a dry run no longer imports server modules, and `--no-verify` makes no server calls. Anchors written by the earlier version were never verified, and re-running against a matching UUID is a no-op, so re-checking one means deleting and re-provisioning it (#2290).
- **health:** a check reporting `degraded` now shows up in the health summary. The aggregation knew only healthy, warning, deprecated, unavailable and error, so with no embedder (the default on the shipped container, which excludes `sentence-transformers`) health read `moderate` with empty `degraded_checks` and `failing_checks` and "No action needed." `status_breakdown` gains a `degraded` counter and `degraded_checks` includes it. Separately, `src/reranker.py` checks availability with `find_spec` and imports `sentence_transformers` only when the model first loads, so importing the module no longer loads the model runtime (#2292).
- **ci (merge-loss guards):** the orphan-push guard no longer files a finding for a push that a pull request goes on to track. It looked for an open PR only at push time, so a PR opened a minute later still left a false orphan issue; it now looks again after a grace window and counts both an open PR on the branch and an open PR that already contains the pushed commit (the re-land case). For a branch restarted from a newer default branch, a finding now lists only commits beyond the merged PR head that are also absent from the default branch; a push of only default-branch commits is prunable, and a failed or incomplete GitHub comparison yields an indeterminate finding with no cherry-pick recipe, because the unpaginated Compare API can omit commits after 250 (#2297, #2348).
- **tool descriptions:** `archive_orphan_agents`' served description no longer lists a hardcoded display-name exemption among the agents never auto-archived. The label-based protection was removed on 2026-08-26; `is_agent_protected` protects only system agents and the `pioneer`, `persistent` and `protected` tags, so clients reading the description were told of an immunity that did not exist (#2306).
- **watcher:** a successful region no longer erases another region's model failure. A file larger than one window is scanned as several regions, and each region's success cleared the failure counter, so a file with an unscanned region read as a healthy scan, in both scan and review. A chunked pass now clears the counter once, only when at least one region succeeded and none failed; otherwise each failure counts toward escalation and a warning names how many regions went unscanned. A region skipped as busy still does not count as a failure but now produces a not-fully-scanned warning (#2317).
- **dialectic review:** a reviewer that cannot form a judgment now abstains instead of filing a binding rejection. The orchestrated reviewer used to submit its parser's conservative DISAGREE when the model returned nothing parseable, claiming the reviewer slot and blocking the paused agent with an empty rejection indistinguishable from a reasoned one; `Verdict` now carries `judgment_formed`, separate from `degraded` (a model that approved without naming terms has judged, and that objection is still filed). The server enforces the boundary for every submitter: `dialectic` `antithesis` and `synthesis` accept `judgment_formed`, and `false` records a `dialectic_reviewer_abstained` audit event (also emitted by the in-process synthetic reviewer's degraded paths) and returns success without claiming the slot, advancing the phase or spending a synthesis round. The default is `true`, so omitting it behaves as before, and a garbled string fails toward abstention; interface contract 1.12.0 (#2321, #2322).
- **dialectic reviewer, external host:** selecting `UNITARES_DIALECTIC_REVIEWER_HOST=external` (alias `gemini`) now works on the orchestrated reviewer path (`UNITARES_DIALECTIC_ORCHESTRATED_REVIEW=1`). The `UNITARES_DIALECTIC_EXTERNAL_*` settings were not on the spawn allowlist, so the child had no endpoint and every such review fell back to the local model; only the name of the key variable is forwarded, never the key. The external host also gets its own output budget, `UNITARES_DIALECTIC_EXTERNAL_MAX_TOKENS` (default 8192), because a hosted reasoning model was cut off at the shared 1,024-token budget before emitting a verdict. When a selected host produces no verdict and the local model answers in its place (`fallback_from` set), a fallback approval is withheld as an abstention and cannot overwrite a standing rejection, while a fallback objection is still filed; `degraded` keeps its meaning, so these rows are separated on `reviewer_backend.fallback_from` (#2358, #2399).
- **knowledge graph embeddings:** a finding stored, or refreshed by `update_finding`, on a server without embeddings (the default container omits `sentence-transformers`) now logs one WARNING per process naming the consequence and the backfill, and DEBUG for later skips. Before, the skip was silent: the finding never got a vector, semantic search could not find it (full-text search still could), and nothing retried once embeddings were installed. The backfill is `scripts/migration/reembed_corpus.py --only-missing` under the server's `UNITARES_EMBEDDING_MODEL`; it fills only rows with no vector, so a skipped refresh leaves a stale vector it does not rewrite (#2360).
- **sdk check-ins:** `GovernanceClient.checkin` and `SyncGovernanceClient.checkin` now read the verdict, guidance, coherence and risk from whichever shape `sync_state` returns, including the default compact experience envelope. That envelope carries no `decision` or `metrics` and puts the verdict at `state_summary.action`, so before this every `CheckinResult.verdict` was `"proceed"`, `coherence` and `risk` were `None`, and the SDK agent's pause and reject branches could never fire. A round-trip test now feeds the real envelope for each verdict to both clients (#2368).
- **doctor, Lumen recovery:** the Lumen check-in doctor now posts one `info` RECOVERED finding on the first healthy tick after an incident it alerted on, and closes the incident only after reading that row back from `audit.events`, since an ack can precede a failed persist. Before, it posted only on failure, so `finding_producer_live` learned its cadence from an outage and kept reporting the producer silent long after recovery. The same change made the cold-start pause canary count only non-authored pauses; #2419 later reversed that, and the canary now counts authored cold-start pauses too (#2374).
- **risk attribution:** `risk_attribution` (the `phi_drift` description and the cold-start note) and `docs/SCOPE_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md` no longer say a self-reported `ethical_drift` is blended into the drift vector at a capped 30%. On the live check-in path that blend has never run: the handler passes a numpy array and `compute_drift_vector` read only lists and tuples, so every report is read as zero and a maxed `[1,1,1]` report scores the same as `[0,0,0]`. The text now says the self-report does not currently enter the drift vector, phi or the verdict. The blend is deliberately not repaired, because that would let self-disclosure move the verdict; it is parked as a policy question, and the dated 2026-06-28 self-report record carries a correction note (#2376).
- **simulate_update:** the dry run is now dry. `UNITARESMonitor.simulate_update()`, which the MCP `simulate_update` tool calls, restored only a hand-maintained list of fields, so each simulated update permanently changed the behavioral EMA and its baseline (the verdict-authoritative state once warm), the adaptive governor's controller state, continuity memory, drift history and the agent's entry in the drift-baseline cache. It now runs the update on a deep copy of the monitor and restores the drift-baseline entry, including when the simulated update raises (#2381).
- **search attribution:** the lean digest a default `search_shared_memory` call returns (`memory_suggestions`) now keeps who wrote each finding: `by`, the write-time display label, and `agent_id`, the identity it was filed under. Before, the lean projection dropped both, so attribution needed a second full-mode call. The label is for display; `agent_id` is the identity (#2386).
- **sentinel, forced-release alarms:** the lease-plane Elixir test suite now defaults to a `governance_test` database; it defaulted to the live `governance` database, so a local `mix test` performed real forced releases that the live Sentinel paged on as HIGH. Forced releases on `dialectic:/test_elixir_` and `resident:/test_elixir_` surfaces are also suppressed, with the same prefix list in the BEAM poller, the Python alarm and the doctor's `forced_release_transform` check, and a parity test ties the BEAM list to the doctor's. Sentinel's own `test_sentinel_*` fixtures still alarm by design, and the BEAM change takes effect only after a Sentinel release rebuild and restart (#2395).
- **identity audit volume:** a client that keeps sending a `client_session_id` whose binding no longer exists now writes at most one `session_resolve_miss_observed` audit row per session key and reason every 10 minutes, instead of one per call; on 2026-09-24 one such key had been writing about 140,000 rows a day since a Redis restart dropped its binding. Every miss still fails closed with `resume_failed`. Each row carries `suppressed_since_last`, a quiet key's pending count is flushed as its own `throttle_flush` row, and throttle state is capped at 4,096 keys, so totals are a close lower bound, short only by counts pending at process exit (#2398).
- **pause wording:** an agent is no longer told to pause, or shown `action: pause`, unless the policy decision paused it, and a paused agent is never told to continue. When the cold-start guard or gap suppression turned a pause into proceed or guide, the verdict stayed `high-risk`, and the default `auto`-mode `sync_state` envelope, `_recovery_hint` and the glossary's `high-risk` entry still said "Provisional: pause" or "Paused - stop this line of work". `explain_verdict` now records the decision at every agent-facing wrap, the recovery hint is severe only on a stop, and the server instructions say a pause ends through `self_recovery`, operator resume or expiry, and that `self_recovery` refuses while risk stays high. This changes wording and action labels only; no pause fires more or less often (#2408).
- **predictions across restarts:** an open check-in forecast (the `prediction_id` a check-in with `confidence` mints) now survives a server restart. The registry lived only in the monitor's memory and the snapshot never carried it, so after any deploy a `record_result` naming the id came back `prediction_binding: missing_prediction` and `calibration_excluded: true`. Both snapshot writers now persist open forecasts with a wall-clock creation time, restore validates them and keeps their TTL age, an outcome for an agent whose monitor is not yet loaded loads it first, and the database claim at outcome time stays the exactly-once authority (#2411).
- **response text:** the server no longer rewrites the caller's own words in echoed text. `_friendly_action_hint` translated tool names inside caller-written fields, so a finding summary containing `auto-onboard` came back as `auto-start_session`; caller-text fields (`summary`, `details`, `content`, `reasoning`, `root_cause`, `response_text`, `issue_description`, `title`) now pass through verbatim, while hints and next actions still translate. `request_review` no longer reports "Your parameters were auto-corrected" for the `use_brief_as_thesis=true` default it injects itself; the default stays visible in `normalized_parameters`, and real coercions are still reported (#2413).
- **REST self-reads:** over `/v1/tools/call`, `check_working_state` and `get_governance_metrics` now resolve the caller's own identity when the caller asserts its session (an explicit `client_session_id` or an `X-Session-ID` header), as the same read over MCP already did; before, REST returned `unbound`, so REST clients such as plugin hooks and the Python SDK could not read their own state back. Resolution is read-only and mints nothing, sets only the request's context binding without adding `agent_id` to the arguments, and a server-inferred derivation (fingerprint, pin or transport-injected id) still reads `unbound` (#2416).
- **resident anchors:** `scripts/ops/provision_resident_anchor.py` decided whether to write a uuid-only anchor from `UNITARES_UDS_SOCKET` in its own environment, which the documented invocation (with the server's environment loaded) always sets. Every `persistent` resident therefore got a uuid-only anchor with no token and no live verify, whatever transport it used, and a resident that resumes over MCP HTTP with `identity(agent_uuid, continuity_token, resume=true)` could not resume from it. A new `--transport {http,uds}` names the resident's transport; with the socket variable set and no flag, the script refuses (exit 2) before any network call or write. The SDK's own uuid-only rule and anchors already on disk are unchanged, and `docs/operations/resident-roster.md` passes `--transport http` (#2460).
- **docker:** a fresh Docker install reported System Health `critical` (HTTP 503 from `/health/deep`) because the `lease_plane` check failed with `No module named 'unitares_sdk'`: `src/lease_plane` re-exports the lease-plane client from the SDK in `agents/sdk`, which the image never installed. The v2.22.1 image is affected. The `Dockerfile` now installs the bundled SDK (`--no-deps`; its dependencies are already in `requirements-docker.txt`), and the Docker quickstart CI job fails on any failing `/health/deep` check or check in `error`. A fresh stack can still report degraded knowledge-graph health, since no embedding model is loaded by default (#2491).
- **dashboard Overview on a fresh install:** each card now renders what the server returned. An empty resident roster no longer produces "0 of 0 residents awaiting first check-in"; the Agents card shows registry active of total with its window and unknown presence; calibration answered `unassessed` shows as unassessed, not a red "miscalibrated"; an automation census that never ran shows "no census yet"; and System Health's detail line counts the degraded and unavailable checks that set its status. The bundled offline snapshot now backs a failed read only when the page is opened from a file or with `?snapshot=1`: on a served page a failed read shows "server not answering" and an "unavailable" badge instead of another deployment's residents, coherence and version, and failed resident and health reads are retried on the stats cadence until they answer live (#2492).
- **inference on a fresh install:** `consult` and the in-process dialectic reviewer could never run on a fresh Docker install: `consult` answered `DEPENDENCY_MISSING` and a review request waited for a peer that never came. The `openai` client SDK they use to reach any model, local Ollama included, was declared in no dependency file; it is now in `requirements-full.txt`, `requirements-docker.txt` and the `full` extra. The Ollama host had two names in two formats; `src/local_inference_env.py` resolves both for every reader, with `UNITARES_OLLAMA_BASE` documented and winning and `UNITARES_OLLAMA_BASE_URL` an accepted alias, and the orchestrated-review spawn forwards the resolved URL. `docker-compose.yml` passes `UNITARES_OLLAMA_BASE`, `UNITARES_OLLAMA_BASE_URL` and `UNITARES_LLM_MODEL` through (empty by default, so the default install is unchanged) and maps `host.docker.internal`. The install manual gains an optional "Choose a model" step (#2495).
- **doctor and setup on other machines:** the doctor and setup no longer assume the original operator's machine. The `resident_agents` check's hardcoded launchd roster is replaced by `UNITARES_DOCTOR_RESIDENT_LAUNCHD` (`|` separates alternatives) and SKIPs when unset; `launchagent_loaded` and `pid_file` apply only when governance itself is launchd-managed and SKIP on Docker Compose, Linux or stdio hosts. Every reader of the secrets env file (doctor, setup, `ship.sh`, the BEAM start and ops scripts, and the ops tooling) uses one lookup order: `UNITARES_SECRETS_ENV`, then `secrets.env` in the new `unitares` config directory, then the legacy `cirwel` one only when it exists and the new file does not. A missing secrets file is SKIP rather than WARN, since the server never reads it. The `postgres_running` failure names `docker compose up -d postgres-age`, and the Claude Desktop config example drops an operator-specific server entry (#2499).
- **skills (freshness stamps):** a skill attestation no longer credits the git user with a re-check that an agent or a job made. When `SKILL_ATTESTATION_VERIFIER` was unset, `scripts/client/check-skill-freshness.sh --stamp` recorded the repository's git `user.name` as the verifier. Every agent on an operator's machine commits under that one name, so an agent's stamp read as the operator's own re-check, and the record could not tell the two apart: of the 120 attestations dated 2026-09-26 in master's history, 99 carry the operator's git name and 21 name an agent. A stamp now takes its verifier from `SKILL_ATTESTATION_VERIFIER` when it is set. Without it, a person at a terminal (stdin is a TTY) still falls back to git `user.name`, and the stamp says so. Any other caller, and a terminal where git has no `user.name` (which used to record the verifier as `unknown`), is refused with exit code 2 before any skill is touched: nothing is written, and the message names the variable and the command to re-run. The STALE hint the check prints now includes the variable, so an agent copying it stamps under its own name. The check itself, `--prune` and `--migrate` record no verifier and are unchanged, so CI and the release-cut prune are unaffected; no automated caller in this repository stamps. Existing attestations are not rewritten. A harness that runs an agent under a pseudo-terminal still reaches the git fallback (#2529).
- **temporal context:** the "N knowledge graph entries added since last session" sentence in `temporal_context` (on the onboard response and on check-ins, through the `enrich_temporal_context` enrichment) can now appear. The narrator passed the previous session's end to `kg_query` as an ISO string. `created_at` is TIMESTAMPTZ and asyncpg refuses a `str` for it, so every call raised a `DataError`. The narrator caught it and logged it at debug level, and the sentence was never emitted, while the other temporal signals (long session, gap since last session, idle, high check-in density) kept working. Its unit tests mocked `kg_query` and so accepted the string. It now passes the timezone-aware datetime, and a test holds the call to that type. What it does not change: the signal still needs a previous inactive session for the same identity, so a freshly minted identity never sees it; the count reads at most 50 rows and includes every status, archived and cold among them; and the narrator stays silent when nothing crosses a threshold (#2517).
- **watcher:** The calibration floor that demotes a noisy (pattern, file class) no longer counts resolutions of a rule Watcher has since replaced. `calibration.PATTERN_RULE_VERSIONS` holds the current revision of a pattern's rule, every new finding records its pattern's revision as `rule_version`, and for a listed pattern only findings at the current revision count toward its precision. Findings from before this change, and from a checkout running an older or newer rule, carry a different stamp or none and do not count. A stamp rather than a date, because Watcher state is shared across worktrees and each checkout's hook runs its own `agent.py`. P006 starts at revision 1, the rule after #2424 and #2447 stopped it flagging handlers that already log, re-raise or return an error. As of 2026-09-27, every false-positive dismissal behind the P006 app-code floor came from a 2026-09-23/24 triage of the earlier rule, where most flagged handlers were of the kind those changes dropped, and the floor was hiding current P006 findings on two days in three; that bucket is now unmeasured and does not demote. A change to what a pattern flags should bump its revision, and the P006 rule's comment in `agent.py` says so (#2509).
- **dialectic trigger source:** `core.dialectic_sessions.trigger_source` now records who started a review instead of guessing from words in the free-text `reason`, which filed ordinary requests mentioning "loop" as `loop_detection` and would have filed the drift trigger as `circuit_breaker`. The one in-process trigger (circuit-breaker auto-recovery) passes an `AutomatedTrigger` value that JSON cannot produce, and every request arriving through the tool is recorded `manual`, whatever its text or arguments say; the LLM-assisted path now records the value too. The sustained-drift auto-review in `updates/phases.py` never ran, because its import failed and the error was swallowed, and it is removed rather than repaired. No historical row is changed (#2513).
- **dialectic reviewer provenance:** the orchestrated reviewer now stamps `reviewer_kind: "orchestrated"` on its antitheses. The server already classified these reviewers correctly through its fallback on the registered model type, so behavior does not change; the stamp makes the record countable, since no orchestrated antithesis carried the kind before. It is descriptive provenance, not identity proof (#2514).
