- **fresh-install messages:** the doctor's `producer_never_reported` check now skips on an install that has never recorded a finding and runs none of the declared producers, instead of listing the reference fleet as producers that never posted; an installed producer that has never posted still warns, and installs with finding history are unchanged. The OAuth dynamic-registration warning and the `UNITARES_OAUTH_REQUIRED` refusal name the three places to set the flag (the launchd plist, a shell export, or the `governance-mcp` `environment:` block in `docker-compose.yml`, since `.env` alone does not reach the container) and say to restart with `docker compose up -d`, and the governed-spawn log no longer names a plist (#2523).

### Security

- **dialectic:** a converged peer-review synthesis no longer signs party A's slot with the synthesis caller's `api_key`. When the paused agent had no key on file, `handle_submit_synthesis` fell back to the caller's own argument, usually the reviewer's, so `signature_a` was keyed on the other party's secret and read as an attestation by the paused agent. It now falls back to an empty key, as the LLM-assisted path has since #2155: `signature_a` stays empty and `describe_attestation` reports the record as `unsigned` (or `single_signer` when only the reviewer signed). A paused agent with a key on file still signs with its own key, and `signature_b` is unchanged. Handler-level tests drive `handle_submit_synthesis` to convergence with a reviewer caller and pin all three cases; two of them fail on the previous code. As of 2026-09-25 no stored peer-path resolution since 2026-01-01 carries a non-empty `signature_a`, because callers pass no `api_key`, so the fix closes the path rather than correcting a stored row. It does not restore party-level key material: with no keys issued, peer-path records stay `unsigned`, which is now what they say. The `describe_attestation` docstring is corrected to match: the four 2026 signatures it counted were the uuid-derived fallback #2155 removed. Superseded the next day by #2463, which stops all v2 party-HMAC minting, so in this release no new resolution carries either party signature (see Removed) (#2450).
- **identity session binding:** `identity` no longer binds the calling agent onto a session key it does not own. A second, argument-less session derivation fell through to the onboard pin, which is keyed on the User-Agent alone, and its guard admitted any key that differed from the caller's, so an agent could be written onto another caller's session in Redis, Postgres and the transport cache. That auto-bind is removed, `bind_session` refuses a destination resolved from `pinned_onboard_session`, and auxiliary derivations no longer re-stamp the request's identity provenance (#2144).
- **/mcp/ auth gate:** when an OAuth issuer is configured and its provider cannot be built, `/mcp` answers `503 auth_unavailable` instead of serving the route unauthenticated; a bearer allowlist still admits its tokens, and a wrong token is 401. `UNITARES_OAUTH_REQUIRED=1` refuses to start the process unless OAuth or a bearer allowlist is in force. A new operator-mode doctor check, `mcp_route_gate`, probes the listener with the external `Host` and names the closed gate (a 421 fails naming `UNITARES_MCP_ALLOWED_HOSTS`; an anonymous 401 is inconclusive, not healthy), and `docs/integration/MCP_CLIENTS.md` gains a "When nothing can connect" section (#2162).
- **dashboard data files:** `dashboard/redesign/preview.html` and `PLAN.md` join `snapshot.js` in the authenticated-only set; both shipped in the image and were served unauthenticated, and `preview.html` carried the same governance-state capture as the snapshot (it was later deleted). The same change makes the doctor's `constraint_drift` check return SKIP with compared and skipped counts when no declaring table exists, where it had reported every constraint present after comparing none, pins `estimate_selective_null` in the ablation-matrix script at fixed seeds with its docstring corrected, and records a council review in the open-decisions packet (#2170).
- **strict identity:** with `STRICT_IDENTITY_REQUIRED` on, a write could still run with no identity bound on `/mcp/` and stdio in two ways, and `knowledge(action="note")` would then store it under a derived anonymous writer id. When identity resolution raised, the middleware logged at debug and dispatched unbound; a sticky transport binding with an empty `agent_uuid` was bound as-is. A raised resolution now returns the typed strict refusal (`status: identity_required`) when nothing was bound before the exception and the call is not one allowed to run unbound, and an empty sticky binding is treated as a cache miss. The refusal's `safe_options` offer retrying or staying read-only instead of `onboard(force_new=true)`, and the exception text is not returned. `pre_onboard` calls (`onboard`, `start_session`, `identity`, `health_check`, `check_working_state`, `list_tools` and similar) still run during a resolver outage. Strict mode is off by default, so a default install sees no change; the REST path already failed closed (#2212).
- **outcome evidence:** a caller can no longer buy evidence authority with a string. `detail={"source": "sensor_sync"}` graded `substrate_observed` (0.85, above the 0.65 calibration gate) with no verified marker; substrate evidence now needs a verified marker and a trusted source, as external evidence already did, the grade ceiling is default-deny (omitting it or passing an unrecognised value caps; only an explicit sentinel lifts it), provenance caps at what it asserts (`external_signal` at `externally_verified`, `server_observation` at `substrate_observed`, everything else at `tool_observed`), the public MCP handler drops a caller-supplied `_trusted_ingestion`, and the shared write path no longer stores a higher grade than the response reported. Rows capped at `tool_observed` stay calibration-eligible at exactly 0.65 and a caller can still reach that grade by description; the read-only `outcome_evidence_provenance_split.py` can split that population by how it got there but had not been run against a live database. `POST /v1/harness/outcome` still relies on its operator gate for caller-chosen provenance (#2316).

### Documentation

- **attestation docs:** `EVIDENCE_AND_LIMITS.md`, `SCOPE_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md` and the `drr.v1` decision packet no longer describe resolution attestations as HMAC keyed on each agent's `api_key` as if the deployment used it. That party-HMAC scheme is in the code, but as of 2026-09-25 no resolution record in the maintainer deployment carries a signature keyed on a party's `api_key` under the current scheme: the four 2026 records that carry a signature used a uuid-derived fallback key, forgeable from public data and removed in #2155, and the most recent records carrying two signatures are legacy v1 rows from 2025-12-13 UTC, which cannot be verified. `EVIDENCE_AND_LIMITS.md` also states more precisely what was shelved on 2026-04-19: asymmetric (DPoP-style) keys for agent identity, which party-level non-repudiation would also need, and that remains shelved; the record of that decision does not address server-side signing. `SCOPE_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md` no longer cites that agent-identity decision as a decision to keep the party HMAC, which the code records as still open. Server-side Ed25519 signing exists: as of 2026-09-25 the maintainer deployment's lease attestations use it, and the dialectic resolution receipt (`drr.v1`) is wired, dormant and has issued no receipts. A receipt would attest the deployment's stored record, not either party's intent. The packet also noted that on the peer-review path a paused agent with no key on file got a `signature_a` keyed on the `api_key` the synthesis submitter supplied (closed by #2450, and minting stopped entirely by #2463), so `both_signatures_present` records that strings were stored, not who held the keys. The consequence is unchanged: a record from this system still cannot be verified by an operator who does not already trust its issuer, and nothing here enables or proposes enabling `drr.v1`. The dormant-capability registry's `drr.v1` row now lists a second principal as its own precondition, alongside custody, a pinning channel, key history and a revocation or transparency policy, and the packet's summary names all five instead of two (#2441).
- **federation wording (attestation docs):** the four attestation documents that the 2026-09-25 federation work left alone to avoid conflicts now link the federation trust decision record and follow the wording rule from #2451 (#2454). `docs/EVIDENCE_AND_LIMITS.md` and `docs/SCOPE_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md` no longer describe multi-principal trust as an open track. They say it is not scheduled (D3a) and give the recorded wake condition: a real outside party, a person or organisation with its own administrative root, asks to verify a record from this deployment. The threat model also says that the choice to restore or retire party-HMAC key issuance was decided (D5: retire minting and keep history readable), and that it is implemented in #2449. The `drr.v1` decision packet and its dormant-capability registry row now point to the record for reason 4, the second principal. Both keep all five wake preconditions as they were and state that citing the record does not satisfy the non-exportable custody criterion. Where "federation" meant several authority domains, these files now say "cross-operator" or "multi-principal". The evidence page's section heading becomes "Local control and cross-operator trust", and its test pin moves with it. The note in `docs/dev/CANONICAL_SOURCES.md` that called these files pending now says they are done. The single-operator sense keeps "federation kernel". No decision, code, flag, schema or deployment changes (#2461).
- **federation decisions:** a new decision record, `docs/proposals/active/federation-trust-decisions-2026-09-25.md`, records three selections the working agent made on 2026-09-25 under a one-criterion operator delegation, after its own adversarial design review; merging is the operator's ratification. Multi-principal trust is not scheduled: as of that date there is no second principal and no external verifier, and a second host run by the same operator is not one. It wakes when a real outside party asks to verify a record from this deployment; outreach is not scheduled either, with a review backstop on or after 2027-03-25. Custody for the `drr.v1` receipt key is deferred behind that, and the record keeps the framing for whoever wakes it. Minting of v2 party-HMAC signatures on dialectic resolutions is retired going forward, with history kept readable and the record's fields unchanged; implementation is a follow-up tracked in #2449. The roadmap moves multi-principal trust from "Now" to "Later" as not scheduled, with its wake condition, and the identity plan's S20 entry gains a dated pointer answering its external-verifier question. No code, flag, schema or deployment change (#2452).
- **eisv contract:** a dated section places fitted or outcome-learned estimators (fitted blend weights, a learned readout) in the efficacy, label-gated bucket the ROADMAP already defines, and summarises, pointing to it as the authority, the reopening-clause clarification decided the same day in the registered outcome stop rule and ratified on merge (see the Changed entry): an estimator fitted to the same trusted task/test label channel does not by itself constitute a new premise. Label-free work (self-predictability confidence, windowed baselines, system identification) may proceed offline; any live-path change waits for the 2026-12-01 report. The section carries a dated deployment snapshot of how many identities run on the ODE fallback alone, with its read-only query committed as `scripts/analysis/ode_fallback_reach_snapshot.sql`, and marks as an inference its use of the 2026-09-23 instrument-supply read. The sensory-split table now anchors on function names instead of stale line numbers, and its V row states that the sensor's `_compute_V` reaches the ODE only through spring coupling, while the verdict V is an EMA of `E_obs − I_obs`. One line mirrors the placement into `docs/ROADMAP.md` and a pointer into the maths roadmap. Documentation plus one read-only query file: no read, estimator, weight or threshold changes, and the outcome question stays unresolved. (#2440)
- **positioning:** every headline definition now says "single-operator federation kernel" (README, product definition, capabilities guide, docs index, Pages landing, Glama description, package description, MCP server instructions), so the one sentence a reader stops at carries the scope; the multi-authority sense is written "cross-operator" or "multi-principal" in the live docs this change touches. This qualifies the noun adopted in #2159 rather than replacing it; no capability, schema or interface-contract digest changes.
- **ontology:** `docs/ontology/competitive-analysis-2026-09.md`, a claims falsification audit and market map. Every public claim from the README, the product definition, the public site, the trust contract, the preprint title and the Mercor EoI draft is typed (capability, uniqueness, benefit) and classed (unique, differentiated, commodity, unsupported) against the strongest substitute opened: NeMo Relay and Agent Toolkit, A2A 1.0.0, Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, Microsoft Entra Agent ID, Google's SPIFFE-based agent identity and Agent Gateway, OpenTelemetry GenAI, Langfuse, AgentLens, the IETF Agent Audit Trail draft, Temporal, DriftGate, MI9, Auton and multiagent debate. No claim is graded unique; "federation" and the preprint's "Information-Theoretic" title are graded unsupported as read; governed review and outcome grounding are the strongest differentiated rows. Recommended README, public-site and product-definition rewrites are listed there and not applied by that PR; #2277 and #2280 later applied them. Sources the session's network policy blocked are named, and snippet-only entries are marked as not byte-verified. Supersedes the June map as the market map; the MI9 and Auton paper-positioning verdicts stand. (#2256)

- **skills:** four skills were re-verified against the sources they cite after the description trim moved them, and stamped. `governance-lifecycle` and `unitares-governance` gain the one new agent-facing fact -- the parameter descriptions a client lists are abridged to their first sentence and `describe_tool(tool_name=..., action=...)` returns the full text -- and both add `src/schema_brief.py` to their source lists so the claim is covered by the freshness gate rather than left to drift. `dialectic-reasoning` and `knowledge-graph` cite schema modules that gained only additive `brief` metadata; their claims were re-checked against the code and all hold (`_REVIEWER_KINDS` is exactly agent_submitted / external_consult / orchestrated with an unknown kind falling back to agent_submitted; `CLOSURE_CLASSES` is exactly the five the skill names, `_CLOSING_STATUSES` the four, and `_CLOSURE_EVIDENCE_REQUIRED` maps fix_verified to {deployed, observed} and unobserved to {window, instrument_check}), so those two are stamped unchanged. The plugin mirror lags until the next bundle re-cut, which the next release entry must declare with its `plugin-bundle-recut` marker. (#2107)

- **skills:** five skills whose cited sources had changed after `last_verified` were re-verified claim by claim and corrected: `knowledge-graph` (`search_shared_memory` defaults to lean summaries; date filters are not honoured by search; `supersede` and response chains need the AGE backend; `cleanup` is the 7/30/90-day lifecycle pass and staleness scoring is `audit`; closing statuses take `closure_class` and `closure_evidence`; lifecycle tags documented), `dialectic-reasoning` (the provenance-stamped antithesis example lacked the required `reasoning`; `whose_move` literals; the outcomes table lists what live code produces; policy and enforcement blocks arrive on the check-in, not the metrics call), `governance-fundamentals` (`verdict` is a glossary wrapper of `metrics.verdict`, not the action; `nearest_edge` can be `oscillation`; the coherence margin edge is gated on provenance, not history), `unitares-dashboard` (`snapshot.js` is auth-gated and can be absent, so snapshot fallbacks must guard every level; the WebSocket connects cookie-first; `RELOAD` is the polling fallback only while the stream is closed; the verify section describes the vitest harness that exists), and `unitares-governance` (the default surface is the five-tool `minimal` mode). Source lists now name the files the claims resolve to; all seven skills carry `source_digests`; `discord-bridge` keeps its date because its sources live in another repository. The plugin mirror lags until the next bundle re-cut, which the next release entry must declare with its `plugin-bundle-recut` marker, and the plugin's copy of `_check_freshness.py` should be synced at the same re-cut. (#2097)
- **skills:** the `governance-lifecycle` skill's MCP Tools Reference now says which of its names a tool mode advertises: the default `minimal` lists only the five-tool checkpoint loop, `lite` (29 tools) lists every name in the reference plus `list_tools` / `describe_tool`, and a mode filters only `tools/list`, so every registered tool still dispatches by name in every mode. The discovery line names the modes that carry the two introspection tools instead of implying they are always listed. Every other claim in the skill was re-verified against its cited sources; `last_verified` moves to 2026-09-07 and the source list gains `src/tool_modes.py` and `src/tool_mode_listing.py`. The plugin mirror lags until the next bundle re-cut, which the next release entry must declare with its `plugin-bundle-recut` marker. (#2095)
- **proposals (BEAM capabilities):** the BEAM verbs-as-capabilities RFC now states proposed first-slice contracts: sender-scoped send receipts with a bounded retry horizon and explicit unknown outcomes, non-consuming cursor reads for one logical inbox receiver, and dispatch provenance owned by governance with execution evidence owned by BEAM. It aligns the attestation-issuance scope and the proposal index. It is a proposal only: no runtime implementation, concrete retry bounds or acceptance evidence ship with it (#2090).
- **install docs (ODE):** `UNITARES_DISABLE_ODE` is documented as what it is: the runtime never reads it, so setting it skips neither the ODE engine nor its dependencies, and there is no supported behavioral-only startup mode. The install playbook, the running-the-server manual, `requirements-core.txt` and the `governance_core` module docstring stop offering it, and the docstring states the remaining coupling (ODE-derived inputs to the behavioral sensor, warmup fallback, structural pause gates). A new `glama.json` names the repository maintainer for the Glama listing (#2112).
- **README and evidence record:** the README opens on the operator's problem, what an operator gets and does not get, and the quickstart, ahead of the tool surface. The deployment-evidence record (where the numbers come from and what they measure, structural build counts, the named federation blocker, lease-plane detail) moves to a new `docs/EVIDENCE_AND_LIMITS.md`, indexed from `docs/README.md`, and `update_docs_tool_count.py` guards the tool count it restates. Later README rewrites in this release changed the opening again; the evidence page remains (#2114).
- **claims against code:** after #2137 removed discovery profiles, `COMPATIBILITY.md` no longer promises a `standard` profile or says `minimal` restores the old surface, `docs/EVIDENCE_AND_LIMITS.md` no longer describes tools as gated by `GOVERNANCE_TOOL_MODE`, the Glama environment schema no longer defaults to a mode that selects nothing, and `hint_target_advertisement.py` no longer defaults to a dead profile. Fifteen stale file-and-line anchors across nine docs become symbol references, and CI now runs the shared-contract parity check the contributor docs said it ran (#2145).
- **proposals (governed-effect convergence):** the convergence proposal's state model says a `record_only` run terminates at `approved`, not `verified`: `verified` means post-commit read-back, which a `record_only` run never performs. The evidence of authorization is the terminal `custody.record_only` trace event, and promotion continuity must bind to hashes and identifiers, never to a `verified` state (#2146).
- **open decisions packet:** `docs/proposals/open-decisions-packet-v0.md` packages seven open operator decisions (options, non-binding recommendation, reversibility, blast radius, default if silent) and records a relayed review's recommendations as recommendations only. All seven remain open; nothing in the packet is approved or implemented (#2156).
- **ontology (NeoHorse-1 triage):** `docs/ontology/neohorse-1-triage-2026-09.md` triages the NeoHorse-1 paper against UNITARES: its routing records feed post-training, so its output is model weights rather than a runtime state estimate, and four terms collide (harness, capability, tier, the per-turn vector). The v7 related-work draft places it, and `docs/ontology/README.md` indexes the external triage documents (#2157).
- **tool registration docs:** `docs/dev/TOOL_REGISTRATION.md` names the `ToolMeta` record as the edit point and describes `TOOL_ORDER` as derived from it, and stale tool counts in code comments and the drift ledger are corrected. The full `list_tools` response computes its essential-tool count at response time instead of claiming about ten. The contributor contract lists all eight workflow aliases (#2171).
- **README / capabilities guide:** reconstruction's open question is no longer framed as "benefit over Git plus a structured handoff". The README now says only that whether reconstruction recovers more in practice is unmeasured, and `docs/CAPABILITIES_AND_DEPLOYMENT.md` describes the existing rehearsal against that baseline as a harness check (both arms receive the same declared facts), not a held comparison. `check_doc_drift.py` accepts a set of wordings for this requirement and still fails if the unmeasured-benefit caveat is removed (#2186).
- **README:** the landing page is rewritten product-first and release-independent. It opens with the accountability problem in plain language ("accountability infrastructure" as the reader-facing category, "federation kernel" as the architectural explanation), moves Quickstart ahead of architecture detail, and installs from the tag named in `PUBLISHED_VERSION` instead of an embedded version. Release numbers, runtime counts and running-since claims leave the landing page; the former README claim ledger moves to `docs/EVIDENCE_AND_LIMITS.md#claim-ledger`, which product and reviewer docs link to. Documentation contracts now hold the README to a 160-line budget, release-independent prose and those evidence links (#2188, #2193).
- **operations:** `docs/operations/agent-analysis-provenance.md` asks every load-bearing claim in an agent's analysis of a deployment to be marked `tree`, `system` or `inferred`, and, when live instruments are unreachable, to mark what needed them as unverified rather than verified or refuted. It is illustrated by four misreadings of a checkout taken as the running deployment (for example a plist template's backend setting read as the live backend), and says what a system reading does not license either. The earlier decision record it follows is left unedited with a dated forward note (#2227).
- **tool names in docs:** the slash-command docs (`commands/checkin.md`, `commands/dialectic.md`), the manual's signals chapter, the Reviewer Guide, the circuit-breaker dialectic guide and the cross-machine install guide now use tool names every transport can invoke, distinguish legacy dispatch aliases from the names `/mcp/` exposes, correct the `self_recovery` actions and drop a hardcoded catalog count. SDK and server changes are docstrings and comments only; dispatch is unchanged, and no measured reduction in call errors is claimed (#2244).
- **public site:** the Pages landing now carries the README's tagline and opening sentences verbatim under the headline "Many agents, one record.", and its federation section is retitled "Cross-operator federation is a research direction", saying which sense is deployed and which is not. The Pages build (`scripts/dev/build_public_site.py`) aligns the glossary intro and title with the README and moves the page template to the CIRWEL house style (system serifs, no webfonts); the social-preview tagline constant follows the README, though the image itself is not regenerated. `docs/operations/public-site.md` gains a copy-discipline section naming where the canonical sentence lives, and `docs/plans/public-presentation-critique-2026-09-16.md` records the review behind it (#2251).
- **ontology:** `docs/ontology/competitive-survival-audit-2026-09.md` compares UNITARES with A2A, NeMo Relay, cloud agent control planes, workload identity, harness records, memory and review systems, and observability standards. It keeps the cross-harness accountability hypothesis while treating its necessity as untested, identifies EISV as an unvalidated estimator currently coupled to the write-gating path, treats Relay, OpenTelemetry, external identity and cloud systems as optional adapters, names the platform investments that need an explicit reaffirm-or-freeze decision, and proposes a capture-versus-retrieval pilot against Git, PR, CI and transcripts. `docs/plans/competitive-survival-audit-review-2026-09-17.md` is the cross-agent adversarial review of it (not an external one): a verdict on each claim, disagreements kept as disagreements, and corrected dates for four competitor announcements. Both are product-investment analysis; neither applies a rewrite or changes policy (#2257, #2258).
- **skills:** the `discord-bridge` skill is corrected against the bridge source it cites. It said delivery de-duplication kept cursors per channel per event type; the bridge keeps one event cursor over its event feed. It also said an unset `ANIMA_MCP_URL` omits the Lumen live reads; the pollers start regardless, so an unset URL shows Lumen as down. Four neighbouring lines are narrowed to what the code does; the umbrella `unitares-governance` skill is re-stamped with it (#2263).
- **stale passages:** nine passages that later merges had made false are corrected. The 2.22.1 release notes now say it was tagged and published; `CONTRIBUTING.md` points at the `mcp==` line in `constraints.txt` instead of pinning 1.29.0; the dialectic receipt docs say a keyless LLM-assisted session stores an empty signature and reports `unsigned`; the install manual no longer describes a released v2.23.0 with a fifteen-tool `standard` default (that profile existed only on unreleased master); the verification-weighted verdict proposal states its enable gate as the owners' sign-off plus a review and records that the sign-off has not been given; the cohort preregistration's amendment log records amendment v0.1; and the agent-orchestrator plist template describes loading it wherever the orchestrated dialectic reviewer runs. The served `get_tool_usage_stats` description now presents its counts as telemetry that carries no authority to retire a capability. No behavior changes (#2264).
- **positioning guards:** the product sentence is registered once as `CANONICAL_TAGLINE` beside `PRODUCT_CATEGORY` and pinned on the README, `CITATION.cff` and the Pages landing; the Reviewer Guide and `CITATION.cff` now carry it. The doc-health deny-pattern scan, which walked Markdown only, now also covers declared non-Markdown surfaces such as `CITATION.cff`, and widens to the "AI-agent fleets" constructions. `scripts/diagnostics/check_repo_description.py` checks the GitHub repository description and reports it as unverified, never as passing, when `gh`, auth or the network is unavailable. The social-preview image is still checked by hand, and the DOI-bound citation title is unchanged (#2270).
- **evaluator path:** the manual overview's integration snippet read the policy action from `state_summary`, which has no `action` on a pause verdict, so code copied from it never saw a pause; it now reads `action_summary.action`. The Reviewer Guide's one-sentence read now matches the product definition, its check-in loop uses the workflow names (`start_session`, `sync_state`, `record_result`, `check_working_state`), its fast path clones the pinned `PUBLISHED_VERSION` release instead of `master`, and its alternate-port recipe adds `LEASE_PLANE_HOST_PORT`. Documentation only: the runtime `next_action` text on a pause response is not changed here (#2271).
- **positioning:** the in-repository rewrites recommended by the September claims audit are applied as boundary statements, each narrowing a claim. The README and `PRODUCT_DEFINITION.md` say that runtimes interoperate over their own transports or A2A and UNITARES is the record behind them, not the transport between them, and that a process identity is an attribution record, not a credential, with platform workload identity remaining the credential layer. The README's closing sentence names adjudication (disagreement, conditions and outcomes bound to the process that made the claim) as what the record adds. The Pages landing names what is delegated rather than owned (tool-boundary enforcement, trace emission, agent-to-agent transport), and the roadmap records an IETF Agent Audit Trail-shaped export and OpenTelemetry GenAI emission as gaps; neither integration exists (#2277, #2280).
- **proposals:** `eisv-core-boundary-v0.md` proposes a neutral checkpoint spine (an immutable `checkpoint_id` that claims, evidence, objections, conditions, outcomes and artifact links attach to) with EISV as a versioned, path-dependent subscriber that produces advisory assessments, and enforcement as a separately configured consumer. Documentation only: no runtime, schema, flag, threshold or response change. Its governed architecture review returned `agrees=false`, and the unresolved condition (no Stage 1 or Stage 2 runtime code before the preservation horizon of the registered 2026-12-01 read without a recorded operator waiver) stands as the operative default; until then only a tests-only preservation harness and documentation proceed (#2278).
- **README:** the README opens with the problem before the definition: an agent reports a fix, its session restarts, another process takes over, and the answers to who said it, what supports it, who challenged it and what happened scatter across per-run logs. The definition's third sentence drops its "The goal is simple:" filler and now opens by stating that UNITARES preserves accountability across discontinuities in agent identity, context, process and time; its closing clause changes from "agent work should remain attributable, reviewable, and recoverable" to "remains". Two lines the claim ledger does not support are demoted: outcomes are "recorded where the next decision can use them" instead of improving the next decision, and "residents", fleet vocabulary the page never defines, becomes "background agents" (#2279, #2281, #2315, #2339).
- **relay re-layering decision:** `relay-substrate-relayering-v0.md` records the operator's decision on the Relay packet: keep the record kernel unchanged; point new harness integrations at NeMo Relay while the existing host hook chain runs unchanged; publish the integration upstream first; and make no decision on the governed-effect execute plane. The ninety-day evidence gate becomes a 2026-11-03 renew-or-stop checkpoint. A companion `execute-plane-design-read-v0.md` pre-registers disconfirming conditions for all three execute-plane options in a separate commit before its analysis, and finds that Relay's conditional-execution guardrail sees call arguments and can await a server decision, so the shipped integration's content-blind gate is a choice of that integration rather than a Relay limit. No option's status changes and nothing is implemented (#2288, #2291).
- **documentation accuracy:** two source-checked audit passes correct 32 documentation claims the code contradicts, across the manual, the interface contract, the architecture and scope docs, operations runbooks, integration and developer docs, the agent-executed `commands/`, component READMEs and four skills. Among them: the tool-usage table is `audit.tool_usage`, not `core.tool_usage`; `governance_core` is plain Python in the repository, not a compiled package; an un-allowlisted `Host` gets 421 after auth, not 403 before it; hybrid RRF search needs the AGE backend; no CI lane installs `mcp` 1.x; `margin` names the nearest decision threshold and has five values; `record_result` aliases `outcome_event`, not `knowledge`; `VALID_DISCOVERY_STATUSES` has eight members including `cold`; the migration series runs to `070`; and `governance-start` no longer reads `session_resolution_source` from a `raw_governance` block the default envelope drops. Documentation only (#2289, #2305).
- **substrate attribution:** `UNIFIED_ARCHITECTURE.md`, the glossary, the agent bootstrap contract and `scripts/perf/profile_checkin.py`'s printed output no longer call the anyio/asyncpg shared-loop amplification a structural bug class "three incidents deep" that "still surfaces on new call sites". The ~60x measurement and the cited recurrence were one event, and its own follow-up experiment withdrew the attribution (the floor was an N-await metadata loop). The architecture doc now states what remains at its real strength: no measured instance is not absence, the record is three shipped mitigations, the asyncpg half is mitigated and the async Redis half is untested, and the choice between the two readings is left to the operator. The profiler's residual now prints as unattributed (#2296).
- **demo:** `scripts/demo/quick_demo.py` no longer says complexity divergence shows at step 4. Derived complexity reads the shape of the output (length, code blocks, list items, paragraphs, questions, tool mentions), never what the text claims about the work, so step 4's one-sentence description of a hard rewrite derives as simple and has the smallest divergence of the six steps. The docstring and comments now describe what the server reads, and `test_step4_description_is_not_divergence_evidence` pins step 4 as the minimum and steps 5 and 6 as the maximum (#2313).
- **glossary:** a semantic re-sweep of `docs/ontology/glossary.md` against its sources. It no longer quotes the retracted line that the ODE "does NOT drive verdicts": the ODE owns the cold-start verdict (Φ is evaluated on the ODE state) and reaches the post-warmup verdict only through compatibility couplings. The ODE is described as a dynamical-systems ODE, new homonym entries separate the meanings of `V` (three quantities), `coherence` (three producers), `drift` and `entropy`, a note records that `agent_state.entropy` holds S and `volatility` holds V, and `governance_core/dynamics.py`'s docstring now states the clamped bounds (V in [-1, 1], S in [0.001, 1]; comment only). The mechanical drift gate passed before and after, and the audit record is `glossary-drift-audit-2026-09-18.md` (#2314).
- **repository layout:** supporting documents moved out of the repository root, which now holds only `README.md`, `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`: `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `SUPPORT.md` and `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md` are under `.github/`, where GitHub still surfaces them; `COMPATIBILITY.md`, `GOVERNANCE.md` and `ROADMAP.md` are under `docs/`; and `CODEX_START.md` is under `docs/guides/`. Links, the docs index, support routing, CODEOWNERS and the release and version checks follow the move, and document content is unchanged. Links to the old root paths from outside the repository no longer resolve (#2329).
- **evidence ledger:** `docs/evaluations/review-correction-traces-2026-09-21.md` is a retrospective of a fixed sample of 20 recently created, merged PRs, chosen before their review findings were read: eight carry a public finding-to-revision chain, two an author-recorded internal review with a matching revision, and one more PR implements a condition retained from an earlier structured review. `EVIDENCE_AND_LIMITS.md` splits the old row: review-driven correction traces are an operational observation, while comparative benefit from review and coordination stays untested (no matched control, some records author-relayed or filed under the same account, incomplete common instrumentation). It establishes inspectable correction traces, not causality, incident reduction or lift over ordinary review (#2336).
- **eisv contract:** `docs/ontology/eisv-proprioception-contract.md` gains a canonical operational doctrine: digital proprioception senses, EISV estimates state, autonomic governance regulates, and an EISV reading does not by itself authorize intervention. EISV is described as designed as a runtime state estimator for autonomic regulation; the document does not claim it enables autonomic agents or improves regulation over simpler state representations, and biological, allostatic and free-energy framings are research interpretation unless separately evidenced. Three follow-ups, each prompted by a fresh-reader check, remove the remaining language that presented EISV as formally instantiating interoceptive inference or as a homeostatic construct, and classify the dimensions by dataflow instead (E incorporates externally referenced signals, I and S summarize self-referenced runtime information, V is derived from the others with no independent input). No equation, weight, threshold, verdict or runtime change (#2343, #2344, #2345, #2346).
- **proposals layout:** `docs/proposals/` is split into three reading paths, `active/`, `registered/` and `archive/`, and the docs guide now leads through product, architecture, interface and evidence to current research. Folder placement does not authorize work, change a registration or retire a question: moved documents keep their prose, digest-bound protocols and frozen artifacts keep their bytes and paths, and compatibility locators keep published references (including those the Zenodo deposit cites), but other old GitHub deep links to moved files cannot redirect, and `docs/dev/proposals-layout-2347.md` records the original commits. Follow-ups repaired links and anchors found by two technical-reader traversals (not a human usability study), added dated re-read notes beside proposals whose status tags the code and deployment had overturned (13 retags, original status text kept), and gave the open-decisions packet a decision index. A new CI ratchet (`scripts/dev/check_proposals_length.py`) fails an active proposal over 800 lines unless it is one of the five baselined files, which may shrink but not grow (#2350, #2351, #2357, #2403).
- **registered cohort protocol disclosure:** the independent-operator cohort preregistration gains a dated Amendment-log entry disclosing that two commits after registration (#1840, #1929) changed its text without an amendment entry, although the protocol says its text never moves after merge; the entry does not claim they were authorized after the fact, and the text is not reverted because the registered read manifest depends on it. Neither edit relaxed a threshold, lane, stop rule or embargo, and both predate any enrollment. The entry also records three provenance errors, including that the dress rehearsal ran after registration, not before as the protocol said. Separately, four idle active proposals are parked and one is retagged Built (partial), by operator selection (#2371).
- **README and landing page:** the Pages landing page states its evidence and cross-operator federation facts plainly and links the claim ledger; `docs/EVIDENCE_AND_LIMITS.md` gains a "Scope in brief" section holding, verbatim, the caveats moved off the landing page and the README; and `docs/integration/MCP_CLIENTS.md` says to keep `client_session_id` and names the subagent and substrate exceptions. A rewritten README opening shipped in #2380 and was reverted by #2406: the README keeps its original opening, and only its evidence paragraph moves to the ledger behind a one-line link (#2380, #2406).
- **ops docs and comments corrected:** a third doc-truth pass fixes false claims, each re-derived from source, in the operations docs (`automation-census-setup.md`, `automation-overrides.md`, `ci-coverage-validation.md`, `kg-lineage-dashboard-handoff.md`, `resident-roster.md`), CI workflow comments, the release-series drift script, and the dialectic LiveView README and moduledoc. Among them: `resident-roster.md` stated the calibration rule backwards, and a comment in `src/grounding/class_indicator.py` sent deployments to the scale-constant dictionaries a test forbids them to extend, where per-resident values belong in the `UNITARES_CLASS_CALIBRATION` overlay; `version_manager` checks three files against `VERSION` and four against `PUBLISHED_VERSION`, not seven against `VERSION`; and `dialectic(list)` already returns `awaiting_facilitation`. No behavior changes (#2389, #2392).
- **ontology:** `docs/ontology/frontier-brief-2026-09-24.md` source-checks an external frontier-AI systems brief and maps its recommendations to existing work (outcome binding, the registered coordination ablation, effect grants, the governed-effect veto). It records where the brief is wrong or incomplete for UNITARES: UNITARES mints a new identity per process rather than keeping one per-agent identity across model upgrades, two of four proposed assurance claims do not hold as worded (the permit claim holds only with `UNITARES_GOVERNED_EFFECT_BINDING` or the per-type flag on), and only review latency among four proposed oversight-summary terms can be computed today. Sources blocked by egress are marked as backed by search snippets only, and a newly found gap in the registered coordination ablation is set out as options without amending the protocol (#2400).
- **flag catalog:** the generated `docs/FLAGS.md` no longer carries a `**N flags.**` line, which made any two pull requests adding different numbers of flags conflict; nothing read the count (#2409).
- **audit attribution docstring:** the `_joinable_audit_agent_id` docstring no longer says handle-only `lifecycle_paused` rows are permanently unattributable. The structured handle is stored (as `public_agent_id`) but is not unique, and rows since 2026-04-16 can be attributed through the `circuit_breaker_trip` event, by `actuation_id` from 2026-08-12 and by timestamp before that; only rows before 2026-04-16 are truly unattributable. The clamp's behavior is unchanged: a non-unique handle never becomes a join key (#2421).
- **architecture doc; review without `gh`:** `docs/UNIFIED_ARCHITECTURE.md` was audited against the code and 13 stale claims corrected, among them that verdicts are `proceed` and `pause` with `guide` a proceed sub-action and `reject` retired as an action, the `warning` and `settling` margins, the BEAM services and lease-plane routes in the transport table, and recovery aligned with #2414 (most pauses end through dialectic, operator resume or expiry). Client examples in the troubleshooting guide, install playbook, MCP clients guide and manual now lead with Claude Code, Codex and Hermes; no capability was removed. For the review gate, `review.sh record --emit` and `review.sh dispose --emit` render the exact record body without `gh` for the caller to post, and `scripts/dev/review_providers.json` is one tracked switch that removes a provider from the default choice, the fallback and native review; it shipped with Codex disabled, which #2488 later reversed (#2423).
- **assurance case:** `docs/evaluations/assurance-cases/outcome-binding-v0.md` is a first, draft assurance case. Its claim: while a prediction binding is retained, there is at most one canonical outcome record per `(agent_id, prediction_id)`; identical retries return that record and conflicting reuse gets `PREDICTION_REUSE_CONFLICT` with no new row. It covers only rows written at or after the cutoff anchored on the #2246 merge, and gives a threat model, mechanism, assumptions, falsification conditions, reproduction steps and an evidence manifest of existing tests. Its stated limits include that calibration can be delivered zero times (#2247), the operator-configurable retention window, rows without a binding, and that there is no production measurement. `docs/ontology/frontier-brief-2026-09-24.md` records how the operator's delegation of its five open questions was used: the assurance case adopted, the ablation context regime deferred to a pre-enrollment amendment, and the oversight summary, positioning wording and continuous record view not built or held; agent council output is labelled as neither independent nor operator authorization (#2432).
- **cloud-session runbook:** `docs/operations/cloud-session-plugin.md` no longer says hosted cloud images include `gh`. The plugin's `pre-push` hook queries PR state through `gh` only where the image has it and fails open otherwise, so without `gh` the merged-PR push guard allows every push. The review section now says to check for `gh`, describes reviewing without it (a fresh-context review rendered with `review.sh record --emit` and posted through the GitHub connector), and keeps the fallback of running `review.sh` on a machine that has the reviewer CLI (#2434).
- **host adapter for Hermes Agent:** the README's Ecosystem section lists `unitares-host-adapter` (Hermes Agent and OpenAI-compatible clients) beside the governance plugin, SDK and resident runtime, `docs/integration/MCP_CLIENTS.md` names the `unitares` Hermes plugin as the thin adapter for Hermes, and the host-adapter row in `docs/COMPATIBILITY.md` moves from `0.2` alpha to `0.3` alpha. The quickstart comment in `docker-compose.yml` now says to call `start_session()`; `onboard` remains callable. No runtime change (#2485).
- **install:** the install manual now says what the lease plane does for an install that is not the original operator's: with the governance plugin installed, Claude Code and Codex take a lease on each file before editing it and release it afterwards, so a second agent editing the same file in the same checkout is refused instead of overwriting the first. Before governance-plugin#165 the plugin looked for the lease-plane bearer only in the environment or the original operator's secrets path, so on a default Docker Compose install it never took a lease and never said so. The manual now states that the Compose defaults need no setup (the plugin presents the stack's development bearer to the loopback lease plane), that an operator who sets their own `LEASE_PLANE_BEARER_TOKEN` in `.env` must put the same line in `~/.config/unitares/secrets.env` where the agents run, and that the plugin reports at session start when leases are enabled but would not work. `.env.example` carries the same note beside the bearer setting. No server or Compose behaviour changes (#2520).
- **production snapshot:** the dashboard screenshots in `docs/PRODUCTION_SNAPSHOT.md` are now dated June 2026 and marked as predating the agent-first redesign, with a note listing what changed; the manual's dashboard section describes the current Overview, Agents, Risk and Security views and the extension seam. No screenshots were replaced (#2525).

### Tests

- **test isolation:** the test suite no longer reaches the live Redis. `tests/conftest.py` isolates PostgreSQL, but the Redis client connects to `REDIS_URL` (default `redis://localhost:6379/0`, the operator's live session store) the first time anything calls `get_redis()`, and identity code reaches it from paths a test does not obviously exercise: shadow-pin reads in `derive_session_key`, and the metadata-cache invalidation in `set_agent_label`, which one test run in this batch used to send a `DEL` for a key it had just minted. A new repo-root `conftest.py`, which covers both `tests/` and `agents/`, points `REDIS_URL` at `redis://127.0.0.1:1/0` before any test module imports, and drops `REDIS_SENTINEL_HOSTS`. An unmocked call then fails fast into the client's no-Redis branch. Tests that exercise the client use fakeredis or patch `REDIS_URL` themselves, which still wins; `test_redis_resilience.py`'s default-value test now removes `REDIS_URL` explicitly rather than relying on the developer's shell. Set `UNITARES_TEST_LIVE_REDIS=1` to opt out deliberately. An audit-hook probe over the full suite counted 0 connections to ports 6379 and 26379, and 15 to the guard's port 1 in the identity suites alone (#2481).
- **suite isolation:** the test suite no longer leaves its audit log, heartbeats, state locks or calibration state in the checkout's `data/`. The audit log, process heartbeats, state locks and the two calibration state files took their default path from the checkout, and `tests/conftest.py` redirected none of them, so every run appended test agents to `data/audit_log.jsonl` (a long-lived dev checkout reached 795MB / 1.4M lines, unrotated because rotation runs only in the live server) and left heartbeat and lock files behind in every worktree. The defaults are now module constants the suite redirects. `UNITARES_AUDIT_LOG`, `UNITARES_PROCESS_DIR`, `UNITARES_LOCK_DIR`, `UNITARES_CALIBRATION_STATE` and `UNITARES_SEQUENTIAL_CALIBRATION_STATE` do the same for servers the suite spawns as subprocesses, following `UNITARES_TOOL_USAGE_LOG`. Unset, every path is unchanged. Both servers' startup stale-lock sweeps (`mcp_server.py` and `mcp_server_std.py`), `cleanup_stale_state_locks()` without a root, and the distributed-lock file fallback now resolve the same lock directory `StateLockManager` writes to, so an override moves writer and sweeper together; `bootstrap_server` drops its `project_root` argument. The zombie-reaper test runs against an empty process table, since heartbeats now go to a tmp dir no host server writes. Not covered: per-agent state files under `data/agents/` are still written by tests that save a monitor and deleted after each test by `_cleanup_ghost_agent_state_files`, so an interrupted run can leave some; and some constructors still create empty directories under `data/` (#2417).
- **watcher tests:** the Watcher tests under `agents/watcher/tests/` no longer read the developer's calibration floor. `floor_state.DEFAULT_STATE_DIR` is bound when the module is imported, which happens before the directory's per-test sandbox sets `UNITARES_WATCHER_DATA_DIR`, so the 54 tests there that render findings read the real `~/.unitares/watcher/pattern_floor.json`. Where that floor had a `P006|app` bucket with `ci_lower` below 0.3, the listing tests' medium `P006` fixtures were demoted to low and never shown, and 19 tests in `agents/watcher/tests/test_agent.py` failed locally while CI, where no floor demotes those fixtures, passed them. The failures also came and went by UTC day: they showed only on days the exploration probe did not pick that bucket. The directory conftest now repoints the floor's default dir into each test's sandbox, and an isolation guard pins it by path so a regression fails on CI as well. With the fix, an audit-hook probe over `agents/watcher/tests/` and the `tests/test_watcher_*` files saw no test open any file under the real Watcher state dir. Test-only; Watcher runtime code is unchanged. The five Vigil `run_cycle` tests in `agents/vigil/tests/test_sentinel_coordination.py` also stub `maybe_recompute_watcher_floor`, as `test_hygiene_repeat_and_digest.py` already did: they read the live floor on every run, and once it was over 24 hours old they would have recomputed it from the live findings and written it, or proposed it to the lease plane under a mock identity (an audit-hook probe counted 5 opens of the live floor before, 0 after) (#2480).
- **elixir tests:** two order-dependent Elixir test failures are closed. The agent orchestrator's lifecycle-metrics test now waits, bounded, for the Registry to drop dead runners instead of reading `running` once, and the lease-plane `file_write_executor` ceiling test restores the payload-ceiling setting it lowers, which on some seeds leaked into sibling tests and rejected small payloads. CI's Elixir path filter no longer skips suites on pushes to master, so every merge runs all six Elixir suites; pull requests stay path-gated. No runtime code changed (#2087, #2166).
- **test cache and coverage evidence:** `scripts/dev/test-cache.sh` includes the effective `COVERAGE_CORE` and the coverage.py version in its cache key, so switching coverage cores reruns pytest instead of reusing an earlier success (unset, empty and `sysmon` count as the same selection). `docs/operations/ci-coverage-validation.md` corrects the coverage-evidence note: similar CI coverage totals support the coverage floor but do not establish that two tracers cover the same lines, and it documents a controlled comparison procedure. Knowledge-graph singleton lifetime tests now cover both backends (#2110).
- **identity gate lock:** `tests/test_path28_substrate_http_reject.py` now proves that both substrate-anchored-UUID HTTP reject gates in `resolve_session_identity` exist, each behind its own guard, and a behavioural test requires the second gate to refuse when the first falls through; before, deleting the second gate left the file green. `resolve_session_identity` is decomposed into four private helpers with its path waterfall unchanged (#2141).
- **identity recovery:** `tests/test_identity_premint_recovery.py` drives a real session-key rotation through pre-mint recovery: one call resumes and writes the identity anchor, a second call omits `client_session_id` so the derived key rotates, and the test requires the second call to bind the same UUID via `identity_anchor` with no `dispatch_auto_mint`. The existing tests seeded the anchor by hand, so a divergence between the keys the anchor write produces and the keys recovery probes would have passed. Not covered: a first call whose `model_type` differs from the one derived from the User-Agent (#2254).
- **dashboard route tests:** `tests/test_dashboard_redesign_route.py` clears `UNITARES_HTTP_API_TOKEN` for its tests. A local `.env` that set the token made the entry-page test reach the auth check with a fake request that has no client and fail, while CI, which sets no token, passed. Token injection itself stays covered by the dashboard token-injection and REST-posture tests (#2269).
- **eisv preservation harness:** a partial instrument-preservation harness pins what the check-in estimator produces before any EISV/core boundary work. `tests/eisv_preservation_driver.py` drives `UNITARESMonitor.process_update` through six scenarios (114 check-ins) under one injected clock with ambient `UNITARES_*` and `GOVERNANCE_*` settings removed, and `tests/test_eisv_instrument_preservation.py` compares a golden trajectory file in a hermetic subprocess, fails on any undeclared real clock call, requires the output to move under a one-observation mutation, pins post-update effect dispatch order, and checks against the test database that stored state rows are what the registered read selects. Tests only; no runtime change. It does not meet the proposal's Stage 1 gate, and `docs/operations/eisv-instrument-preservation-harness.md` lists what is not yet pinned, headed by the full `process_agent_update` path and real outcome recording (#2284).
- **test fixtures:** `test_kg_source_counts_recent_rows` deletes and re-inserts its fixture row instead of inserting with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, so a persistent local test database no longer fails it on every run once the row is an hour old. CI, which starts from a fresh database, never saw the failure (#2300).
- **test database cleanup:** `TRUNCATE_TABLES` now lists the four foreign-key children CASCADE was already reaching (`core.agent_behavioral_baselines`, `core.agent_process_bindings`, `core.substrate_claims`, `coordination.session_resolution_sagas`), so the lock order is declared rather than derived at runtime, and a new test fails when a future foreign key adds an unlisted table. `truncate_test_tables` prints a deadlock's `DETAIL` and retries. This is a mechanism and diagnosis fix for a CI deadlock that did not reproduce locally, not a confirmed repair of that failure (#2301).
- **pause-refusal test:** `test_a_huge_expiry_setting_does_not_crash_the_refusal` failed whenever a test that reloads `config.governance_config` ran before it, because it patched a module-level `GovernanceConfig` import that a reload leaves stale while `pause_ttl` imports the live class. It now uses the conftest `set_governance_config` fixture, which patches the live class. Test-only (#2431).
- **fleet-identity guard:** `scripts/dev/check_fleet_identity_leak.py` scanned only string literals in shipped Python, so it stayed clean while served non-Python files named one deployment's residents. It now also scans what the server serves verbatim: dashboard JavaScript (template interpolations included, regex literals skipped) under the same literal-is-a-finding rule, and skills, served Markdown and CSS, `src/tool_descriptions.json` and HTML markup, where any resident name in any case is a finding. Known references are listed with the reason each is deferred rather than silenced, so later cleanup removes entries as it lands (#2493).
- **plugin file leases:** the Docker Quickstart job now proves the companion governance plugin's edit hooks against the real lease plane of the Compose stack. Until now the plugin's file-lease tests stopped at a fake HTTP server, so nothing showed that its PreToolUse acquire, the `held_by_other` refusal and its PostToolUse release work against the service a new operator actually runs. A new step checks out `cirwel/unitares-governance-plugin` at one pinned commit and runs `scripts/ci/plugin_file_lease_proof.py`, which calls the plugin's own `run-hook.cmd pre-edit` and `post-edit-release` entry points with Claude Edit payloads. Session A acquires a lease on one file (exit 0). Session B, editing the same file from a second git worktree of the same repository, is refused with exit 2 and a message naming A's lease. After A's release, B acquires. Each step is also checked against `/v1/lease/status`, and the hooks run with `UNITARES_FILE_LEASES_REQUIRED=1`, because they fail open by default and an unreachable lease plane would otherwise also exit 0. The step passes the Compose default bearer explicitly until the pinned plugin commit includes its loopback fallback. It covers the Claude host payload and one file per edit only, and it does not show that a client without the plugin is stopped (#2521).

### Validation

- **retrieval eval:** `scripts/eval/retrieval_eval.py` no longer lets a labelled target that lifecycle policy has since archived pass silently as a ranking miss. Every labelled target stays in the denominator, so the metrics are computed as before, and `aggregate` gains `labels_out_of_scope`, `labels_undetermined` and `labels_total`, with reachability judged by the same exclusion list the search handler uses; missing or unreadable rows count as undetermined, not as decay (#2139).
- **accountability journey rehearsal:** `scripts/eval/accountability_journey.py` (`make accountability-journey`) scores reconstruction of one authored claim/evidence/review/correction incident from information-equivalent UNITARES and Git/structured-handoff representations, with mutation tests that make a missing link or wrong attribution fail visibly, and `docs/evaluations/accountability-journey/result-v0.md` reports the run. This is mechanism validation on an authored fixture: it does not measure natural capture, real-world benefit or reconstruction speed, and it leaves the preregistered accountable-testbed results untouched (#2179).
- **coordination ablation (preregistered, not run):** `docs/proposals/accountable-coordination-ablation-v0.md` preregisters a four-arm evaluation (independent work, ephemeral coordination, durable UNITARES coordination, full accountable coordination) whose primary contrast is full accountable coordination against ordinary ephemeral coordination, with independent task families as the inference unit, external scoring, matched budgets, a forced process discontinuity in every family and an outer experiment ledger that no arm can disable. It enrolls no cohort, runs no model calls, adds no production switch and reports no result; it is indexed in `docs/EVALUATION_INDEX.md` (#2249).
- **accountability journey, capture stage:** `docs/evaluations/accountability-journey/` gains the first capture-stage datum, one real 2026-09 guard incident reconstructed twice from naturally produced records: once from Git, PR, CI and issue artifacts only, and once from UNITARES records only, each checked by adversarial verifiers, with a fact-by-fact ledger. The artifacts arm recovered the whole causal chain; the UNITARES arm recovered no positive fact about the incident, with its zeros checked against positive controls. The miss is traced to three separately repairable causes: nothing bridges CI or git into the record, nothing surfaced the guard's finding to a bound agent, and the hosted connector was unreachable that morning. UNITARES added only class-level context (a prior rule with its remedy, sibling instances in other repositories, corrections bound to the claims they correct). One incident, scored descriptively; `result-v0.md` links it (#2267).
- **relational instrument supply:** the registered one-time read for `relational-calibration-instrument-supply-v1` (cutoff 2026-09-17) ran once, late, on 2026-09-23 with the frozen SQL in a read-only snapshot, and returned `instrument_supply_not_ready`: 38 behaviorally mature identities and none at the `temporal_established` stage or beyond. Under the contract's stop rule this closes the attempt; it measures instrument supply only, not participant or federation capacity. The result packet is `docs/evaluations/relational-calibration-instrument-supply/result-v1.md`, with disclosures on the late execution and on retention (#2377).
